# Gokan architecture

How the system works: data models, services, state, pages, build and deployment. The rules for writing code are in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md); this file explains the design those rules protect. Read the section for the area you are changing, and update it in the same change when behaviour changes.

## Project Overview

**Gokan SRS** (語感 - "sense of language") is a Japanese vocabulary learning application using Spaced Repetition System (SRS) algorithms. It's designed as a serious study instrument, not a gamified app.

This repo (`gokan-srs`) is a **monorepo** (Bun workspaces) hosting `apps/gokan-srs` (this app) and `apps/gokan-dictionary` (a companion SEO-crawlable static dictionary site - see [gokan-dictionary App](#gokan-dictionary-app) below and [issue #19](https://github.com/gokan-dev/gokan-srs/issues/19)). Both live under the `gokan-dev` GitHub org, alongside the separate `gokan-dataset` repo (the open, CC BY-SA-licensed vocab/kanji/sentence dataset both apps consume). See the root [README.md](../README.md) for the full ecosystem layout.

### Main Goals
- **Vocabulary Acquisition**: Teach Japanese vocabulary based on user's kanji knowledge
- **Spaced Repetition**: Optimize review timing using custom SRS algorithm
- **Kanji-Aware Learning**: Only introduce vocabulary containing kanji the user knows
- **Google Drive Sync**: Persist user progress across devices

### Tech Stack
- **Framework**: React 19 + TypeScript
- **Build Tool**: Vite (rolldown-vite variant)
- **Styling**: Tailwind CSS v4
- **State Management**: React Context + useReducer
- **Authentication**: Google OAuth (@react-oauth/google)
- **Icons**: Lucide React
- **Animations**: Framer Motion

## Design System

Visual rules live in [DESIGN_SYSTEM.md](DESIGN_SYSTEM.md), and the enforced subset (colours only
from tokens, one hue for ordinal data, CJK fonts, motion) is in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#design-rules).
Colour values are written only in `apps/gokan-srs/src/index.css` and
`apps/gokan-dictionary/src/styles/app.scss`.

## Project Structure

Bun workspaces monorepo (`workspaces: ["apps/*", "packages/*"]`). The compiled dataset lives in
the separate [`gokan-dataset`](https://github.com/gokan-dev/gokan-dataset) repo, consumed as a
git submodule at `apps/gokan-srs/dataset/` (see Build & Development, Dataset Consumption).

```
gokan-srs/                              # monorepo root
├── AGENTS.md                           # the project rules every agent loads (CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md import it)
├── eslint.config.ts                    # ONE lint config for the whole repo (typing, purity, storage, app boundaries)
├── tsconfig.json                       # typechecks root config and scripts/
├── scripts/
│   ├── check-conventions.ts            # rules ESLint cannot express (em dash, tokens, schema, tests, agent stubs)
│   └── check-duplicates.ts             # jscpd, two passes (production code, tests)
├── packages/
│   ├── dataset-schema/                 # @gokan/dataset-schema: compiled-dataset types + TagsLookup, used by both apps
│   └── deploy/                         # @gokan/deploy: content-addressed S3 deploy of both apps (scripts/deploy-s3.ts)
├── apps/
│   ├── gokan-srs/                      # the SRS learning app
│   │   ├── dataset/                    # git submodule -> gokan-dataset
│   │   ├── public/data/compiled/       # NOT committed: synced from dataset/compiled/ at dev/build time
│   │   ├── scripts/sync-dataset.ts     # copies dataset/compiled/ -> public/data/compiled/
│   │   ├── src/
│   │   │   ├── commons/                # constants.ts (app config), theme.ts (CSS-variable colours for inline styles/SVG)
│   │   │   ├── components/             # shared UI
│   │   │   │   ├── quiz/               # the card shells (SingleAnswerCard, SentenceClozeCard, StudyCard), card frame, mastery corner, submit/continue, feedback, cloze blank, senses, headword, result styles
│   │   │   │   ├── detail/             # detail-page cards, error screen, SRS stats card
│   │   │   │   └── ui/                 # Button, Card*, Pagination, SettingToggle, SettingSlider
│   │   │   ├── hooks/                  # useAsyncData, useNow, useDebouncedValue, usePersistedControls, useQuizFocusManagement, useIntroducedGrammarIds
│   │   │   ├── context/
│   │   │   │   ├── quiz/               # the quiz state machine (see State Management)
│   │   │   │   │   ├── quizReducer.ts / grammarReducer.ts       # pure reducers
│   │   │   │   │   ├── quizSelectors.ts / grammarSelectors.ts   # pure selectors (selectNextView, computeBlankPlan...)
│   │   │   │   │   ├── useQuizOrchestration.ts / useGrammarOrchestration.ts # all effects and actions
│   │   │   │   │   ├── exerciseReducer.ts, useExerciseTurn.ts   # the exercise turn every card answers through
│   │   │   │   │   ├── useSessionLifecycle.ts, sessionRoutes.ts # shared session start/pause/resume/end
│   │   │   │   │   └── QuizProvider.tsx, useQuiz.ts
│   │   │   │   ├── GoogleDriveContext.tsx, useGoogleDrive.ts, ThemeContext.tsx, useTheme.ts
│   │   │   │   ├── KanjiForm/, Responsive/
│   │   │   ├── models/                 # APP STATE ONLY: vocabulary.model (VocabProgress, SRSEntry), grammar.model, user.model, media.model, state.model, theme.model
│   │   │   ├── pages/                  # main, quiz, grammar, exercise (ExerciseCard), listening, setup, settings, profile, stats, vocab, kanji, about
│   │   │   ├── services/               # srs.service, scheduling, calibration, grammarSrs.service, grammarScheduling, vocabulary/grammar/media services,
│   │   │   │   │                       # http.ts (fetchJson), storage.service, progressCompaction, progressHydration, progressSerialization, migration.service, llm.service, jlptWalk
│   │   │   │   ├── exercise/           # the exercise engine: types, slots, grading, builders, effects, aiRefine
│   │   │   │   └── sync/               # driveClient, mergeProgress, googleDriveSync, googleAccount, types
│   │   │   ├── utils/                  # pure logic, each with a colocated test (answerMatching, sentenceRanking, inflection, knowledge, activity, coverage...)
│   │   │   ├── test/fixtures.ts        # shared test fixtures
│   │   │   ├── App.tsx, main.tsx, index.css (the only colour values)
│   │   ├── terraform/                  # AWS infra (S3 + CloudFront)
│   │   ├── docs/                       # SRS formula research notes, the earlier architecture audit, future refactors
│   │   └── vite.config.ts, tsconfig*.json, index.html, package.json
│   └── gokan-dictionary/               # static crawlable dictionary (see gokan-dictionary App)
│       ├── src/
│       │   ├── pages/                  # page components + shared PageLayout, VocabEntryList, GrammarPointCard, ExampleSentenceList, SiteHeader/Footer
│       │   ├── lib/                    # pure builders and helpers (urls, seo, documentShell, sitemap, grammar*, sentenceSegments...), each tested
│       │   ├── client/                 # search.ts, grammarBrowser.ts (the only browser scripts)
│       │   ├── styles/                 # app.scss (tokens + shared rules), _tokens.scss, pages/*.scss
│       │   ├── test/fixtures.ts        # shared test fixtures
│       │   └── App.svelte, main.ts     # `vite dev` placeholder only
│       ├── scripts/                    # prerender.ts, svelte-ssr-loader.ts
│       └── svelte.config.js            # the one JavaScript file (Svelte tooling requires it)
├── docs/                               # ARCHITECTURE.md (this file), DESIGN_SYSTEM.md, FUTURE_FEATURES.md, MODIFICATION_LOG.md
└── .github/workflows/                  # CI (checks), deploy-site.yml (both apps), agent workflows
```

Other file paths in this document are relative to `apps/gokan-srs/` unless stated otherwise.

## Core Data Models

Compiled-dataset types (`Vocabulary`, `Kanji`, `Sentence`, grammar and media shapes, index
shapes, `TagsLookup`) live in `packages/dataset-schema` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`) and are shared
by both apps. App model files under `src/models/` hold app state only (progress, settings).

### Vocabulary (`vocabulary.model.ts`)

**`Vocabulary`** - Represents a Japanese word/phrase
- `id`: JMdict word ID (stable identifier)
- `writtenForm`: Kanji form + contained kanji characters
- `reading`: Primary reading + alternatives
- `frequency`: Kanji rank + optional kana rank
- `jlptLevel`: optional JLPT level (1=N1 hardest ... 5=N5 easiest). Shown on cards and pages, and drives the `jlpt` learning order and the listening pages' JLPT marks. Resolved per JMdict id at build time from two copies of Jonathan Waller's lists, vendored in the submodule's `data/raw/jlpt/` (`scripts/jlpt-levels.ts`): an id list that assigns every entry a JMdict id by hand is the authority, and Waller's Anki decks only fill entries it lacks (顔, 母, 頑張る, matched on exact spelling and reading). The id list sends a few kana-written rows to an interjection (N5 これ to "hey; oi" instead of the pronoun 此れ); those are remapped in `data/raw/vocab/jlpt-corrections.json`, which also sets の to N5, and the build fails on any such row left unsettled. A word formed from a listed word by one affix (一緒に, 早く, お店, 私たち) takes its level, with `jlptLevelFrom` naming that word. A homograph's level reaches a merged word only when read like its base. The submodule's generated `docs/JLPT_COVERAGE.md` shows where each level comes from and every entry no compiled word answers. About 6.9k of the ~36k words carry a level.
- `textbooks`: optional Genki / Intermediate Japanese lessons that teach the word (from the decks' lesson tags), shown on the vocab detail page via `textbookLessonsLabel` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`).
- `progression.kklcStep`: KKLC step requirement
- `usuallyKana`: optional `true` when the word is learned in kana (ここ, not 此処): shown by its reading (`headwordOf`), needs no kanji, outside the kanji-driven orders, no reading quiz. Decided at dataset build time; see Learning Queue Logic, item 8
- `components[]`: IDs of other vocabularies contained within this one
- `senses[]`: Array of meanings with POS, glosses, misc tags
- `usageHints`: Optional context hints

### Kanji (`@gokan/dataset-schema`)

**`Kanji`** - One of the dataset's 3,128 kanji (KKLC's 2,300, every kanji another learning order teaches, the JLPT list, the jōyō and the jinmeiyō), from `kanji.json` or its own `kanji/{character}.json`
- `character`: The kanji glyph itself
- `steps`: its position in each learning order that teaches it (1 = taught first): `kklc`, `rtk` (Remembering the Kanji, 6th edition), `rtkLegacy`, `kanjiInContext`, `kanjiKana`, `henshall`, `essentialKanji`, `kanjiFlashcards`, `tuttleCards`, `maniette` (KANJIDIC2's book numbers), plus `jlpt`, a level (1=N1 hardest ... 5=N5 easiest) from the Bluskyo/JLPT_Vocabulary kanji list. KKLC positions never move, so every word's `kklcStep` is unchanged. `KANJI_BOOKS` names each book; `kanjiBookPositions` lists a kanji's, with the order label (the name the order pages use) for a book that is an order.
- `meanings`, `readings` (`on`, `kun` in KANJIDIC2 notation, split for display by `kunReadingParts`, `nanori`), `strokeCount`, `grade` (`kanjiGradeLabel`), `radical` (Kangxi number and character), `components` (KRADFILE, real forms: 海 is 氵 母 毋 𠂉), `newspaperFrequency` (KANJIDIC2's rank, how often a reader meets the kanji)
- `frequency`: JPDB rank of the kanji as a word on its own (学 5839, 海 722), not how common the character is; the frequency order uses `newspaperFrequency`

**Kanji orders** (`KANJI_ORDERS`, `KanjiOrderId` in `@gokan/dataset-schema`): the orders a learner can follow, one `index/kanji-orders/{id}.json` each (the kanji in teaching order): every book above but Essential Kanji (its numbering skips positions), plus `jlpt` (N5 first, most frequent first within a level) and `frequency`. `KanjiKnowledge.method` is one of them; `step` is how many kanji into it the learner is, and their known set starts as its first `step` kanji.

**`VocabProgress`** - User's learning progress for a vocabulary item
- `vocabId`: Reference to Vocabulary.id
- `stage`: `'learning'` | `'graduated'`
- `introductionAt`: When user first saw this vocab
- `nextReviewAt`: Next scheduled review (null = new item)
- `lastReviewedAt`: Last review timestamp
- `totalReviews`: Total number of reviews
- `consecutiveFailures`: Consecutive wrong answers
- `reading`: SRSEntry for reading reviews
- `meaning`: SRSEntry for meaning reviews
- `production`: optional SRSEntry for production reviews (English meaning prompt, Japanese reading answer). Optional on the type because progress saved before this quiz type existed has no such field; the migration fills one in on every load. A filled-in entry is **inert** (`dueDate: null`, which no due-check matches) until activated, so adding this direction made nothing due. See Services & Business Logic -> Production quiz rollout.
- `needsRetry`: optional `{ reading?: boolean; meaning?: boolean; production?: boolean }` - per-quiz-type immediate-retry flag. A wrong reading answer only forces a reading retry and never blocks a due meaning or production review (and vice versa).
- `usuallyKana`: the word is learned in kana, so it has no reading direction (see Functional Workflows → Learning Queue Logic, item 8). A synced copy of the dataset's `Vocabulary.usuallyKana`; required on the type, stored compacted as `uk` and left out when false.
- `nextReviewAt` is always **derived**, never hand-set independently - see `scheduling.ts` in Services & Business Logic.

**`SRSEntry`** - Detailed SRS state for reading/meaning/production
- `memoryStrength`: Current memory strength (days)
- `interval`: Current interval (days)
- `difficulty`: 0.0 (hard) to 1.0 (easy), default 0.3
- `dueDate`: When next review is due
- `history[]`: Array of ReviewLog entries

### Sentence Models (`@gokan/dataset-schema`)

**`Sentence`**
- `id`: Original Japanese sentence ID (from source)
- `original`: Japanese sentence text
- `en`: Array of English translations (id + text)
- `indices`: Reading hints/furigana string (optional)
- `matches`: each word's spans in `original`, linked and highlighted. The dataset build finds a
  word by its written forms, and a word written in kana under a kanji headword (それ for 其れ,
  して for 為る) through Tatoeba's annotation of that kana, never by guessing from the kana
  alone; see `docs/SCHEMA.md` in the dataset.

**`SentenceSet`**
- `vocabId`: Reference to Vocabulary.id
- `sentences`: Array of associated Sentence objects


### User Models (`user.model.ts`)

**`UserProgress`**
- `kanjiKnowledge`: KanjiKnowledge object
- `learningQueue`: Array of VocabProgress (all vocab ever introduced)
- `grammarQueue`: Array of GrammarProgress (all grammar points ever introduced) - independent of `learningQueue`, added additively (no format-version migration needed, just a default-to-`[]` at hydration time - see Grammar Activity below)
- `completedChapters`: `string[]` of grammar chapter ids whose end-of-chapter review step has already been shown (or, for a chapter with no anchored contrast lessons, silently acknowledged) - same additive pattern as `grammarQueue` (default-to-`[]` at hydration, no version gate). The CURRENT chapter (what to introduce next) is always derived from the teaching order, never stored - this field only remembers which chapters' one-time step has already fired. See Grammar Activity → Chapter-driven introduction below.
- `watchedEpisodes?`: `Record<string, WatchedEpisode>` keyed `${mediaId}:${episodeNumber}` (`episodeKey`), the listening library's per-episode marks. Optional and **deliberately not in `DEFAULT_PROGRESS`**: progress that never used the library carries no field, so shipping it changed nothing (no forced re-upload) for anyone. See Listening Library below.
- `retiredVocabIds?`: `string[]`, tombstones for vocab whose compiled data no longer exists (the dataset build dropped it), discovered lazily when a load returns not-found (`VocabNotFoundError`). Optional and **not in `DEFAULT_PROGRESS`** (additive like `watchedEpisodes`). The invariant "`learningQueue` holds no id in this set" is enforced on every load (`migrateUserProgress`) and after every Drive merge (unioned, then the merged queue re-filtered), so a retired word stays gone even if a union merge respawns it from another device. See Error Handling.
- `stats`: Counters for newLearnedToday, totalLearned, totalReviews
- `dailyOverride`: Allow bypassing daily new vocab limit

**`KanjiKnowledge`**
- `method`: `'kklc'` | `'rtk'` | `'jlpt'` | `'custom'`
- `step`: Current step (e.g., KKLC step 500)
- `kanjiSet`: Set<string> of known kanji characters

**`UserSettings`**
- `preferredLearningOrder`: `'kanji_coverage'` | `'frequency'` | `'kklc'` | `'jlpt'` (`'jlpt'` walks N5→N1, frequency-ordered within a level; kanji-filtered by default like every other order - see Services & Business Logic)
- `kanjiCoverageTarget`: 1 to 5 (how many words to learn per known kanji before prioritizing new words, default 1)
- `learningFrequency`: `'high'` | `'medium'` | `'low'`
- `enableMeaningQuiz`: boolean (default true)
- `enableProductionQuiz`: optional boolean (default true). The production quiz (English meaning prompt, Japanese reading answer). Turning it off excludes production from queue selection, mastery and `nextReviewAt` derivation, exactly as `enableMeaningQuiz` does for meaning.
- `geminiApiKey`: optional Gemini API key for AI context validation
- `enableGeminiContext`: boolean (default false)
- `alwaysUseAiForMeaningContext`: boolean (default true)
- `meaningContextThreshold`: `'early'` | `'normal'` | `'late'` (default `'normal'`). **Deprecated**, superseded by `meaningContextThresholdPoints` (issue #91) - kept, untouched, so a tab on an older build or a Drive-synced settings payload written by one still has a value to fall back to.
- `meaningContextThresholdPoints`: optional number, 0-200 in steps of 10, **deliberately absent from `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`** (an unset value resolves to 50). Settings load as `{ ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS, ...stored }`, so a default would override every existing user's legacy preset. The meaning-ring value (`calculateMasteryPercentage`, spanning the full two-loop 0-200 curve) at which meaning quizzes switch from the bare word to sentence/context mode. 0 = context from the first meaning review on; 200 = only once the word is mastered (mastered words are retired, so effectively never). Resolved via `meaningContextThresholdOf` (`utils/srs.utils.ts`), which prefers this field (clamped to 0-200, rounded to the nearest step of 10), falls back to the legacy `meaningContextThreshold` enum (early=30, normal=50, late=70) when unset, then to 50. Replaces the old three-preset `OptionGrid` with a `SettingSlider` (see Per-activity quiz settings below) - strength now grows much faster under the per-quiz-type calibration, so a learner needed finer, wider control than three points in the first loop.
- `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement`: optional boolean (default false). When true, drops the "all contained kanji must already be known" filter for the `frequency`/`kanji_coverage`/`jlpt` orders. Has no effect on `kklc` (gated by step, not by kanji set). Settings UI surfaces it for every order except `kklc`.
- `kanjiCountStep`: optional number (default 10, `CONSTANTS.setup.defaultKanjiCountStep`). The increment the profile page's known-kanji stepper moves by, editable there and persisted so it follows the user across devices. Purely a UI preference; nothing in the SRS reads it.
- `listeningGenres`: optional `string[]`, the listening library's genre filter ("I like" chips): titles with at least one of these genres are listed, every title when empty or absent. Kept in settings so it follows the learner across devices. Not defaulted.

### Grammar Activity (`grammar.model.ts`)

New SRS-driven activity alongside the vocab quiz (issue #17) - reuses `srs.service.ts`'s formula rather than a new algorithm, per the issue's resolved decision. See Services & Business Logic → Grammar Services and Application Pages → Grammar Screen for the rest of the design; this section only covers the data shapes.

**`GrammarPoint`** - One grammar point, sourced from the hanabira.org-japanese-content dataset (CC license, attribution required - see the About page's credit link)
- `id`: Stable id assigned at build time from the vendored snapshot (e.g. `"n5-001"`) - the upstream dataset has no ids of its own
- `title`: Japanese/pattern portion only, e.g. `"～けど、～"`. The upstream source originally welded this together with a romaji transliteration in a trailing parenthetical (e.g. `"～けど、～ (〜kedo、～)"`) - `gokan-dataset`'s build step (`splitTitle`) splits the two apart (issue #47) so the app can choose independently where to show each.
- `romaji?`: The transliteration split off `title`, e.g. `"〜kedo、～"`. Absent for the ~1.3% of points (9/828) with no cleanly-splittable trailing parenthetical - a dataset-side gap, not something the app fixes. Rendered on `GrammarDetailScreen`, `GrammarIntroCard` and the All points list (a small meta line beside the title); the grammar quiz cards show only the kana `title`.
- `shortExplanation`, `longExplanation`, `formation` (a template like `"Noun + が + いちばん + Adjective/Verb"`, shown to the user and also the source `gokan-dataset` matches against `GrammarExample.words[]` at build time to precompute `patternWordIndices`, below)
- `jlptLevel`: 1 (N1 hardest) .. 5 (N5 easiest) - every grammar point has one, unlike vocab's optional `jlptLevel` (this dataset is itself organized by level)
- `examples`: Array of `GrammarExample` (3-5 per point)
- `formalityLevel?`: `'casual' | 'neutral' | 'polite' | 'formal' | 'very-formal-literary'` - register, for points that have one (most don't - only points with a close synonym differing mainly by register). Sourced from `gokan-dataset`'s hand-authored `formality.json` mapping, not derived at build time.
- `usageNote?`: One short (~60-80 char) line covering whatever actually disambiguates this point from its near-synonyms - usually register, sometimes connotation/nuance instead (criticism, surprise, unmet expectation). Surfaced in the grammar cloze card's feedback once answered and on the study card (issue #42) - deliberately not shown on `GrammarIntroCard`/`GrammarDetailScreen`'s `title`/`formation`, which already answer the question this exists to disambiguate.
- `family?`: `{ id: string; name: string; relatedPoints: string[] }` - the named cluster of other `GrammarPoint`s expressing the same core idea at a different formality/nuance. `id` is a stable slug (e.g. `"contradiction"`), `name` is a display label (e.g. `"Contradiction (But / However)"`), and `relatedPoints` holds the ids of the *other* points in the family (excludes this point's own id, symmetric - if A's family lists B, B's family lists A). Replaces an earlier flat `relatedPoints?: string[]` field. All derived at build time in `gokan-dataset` by grouping every point that shares the same `family.id`; the app never hardcodes a family id/name. Surfaced by `GrammarRelatedPointsCard` on `GrammarDetailScreen` (issue #45), using `family.name` as the card header instead of a generic label.
- `variantOf?` / `variantRelation?`: set when this point is a **realization variant** of another point (`variantOf` is the canonical's id; `variantRelation` is `'contraction'`/`'politeness'`/`'particle'`/`'particle+politeness'`). A realization variant is the SAME construction written differently (じゃ and それじゃ are contractions of それでは); it is **not** independently taught - only the canonical is in the teaching order, and its quiz card rotates through the realizations (`applyVariantRotation` in `grammarSelectors.ts`, graded interchangeably by register) against the canonical's single SRS entry, so mastery is one climb rather than N. Authored in `gokan-dataset`'s `data/raw/grammar/variants.json`, compiled into `index/variant-groups.json` (`GrammarVariantGroupIndex = Record<canonicalId, { id, relation, formalityLevel?, title }[]>`, includes the canonical with relation `'canonical'`). **A realization variant never ALSO carries a `family`**: the canonical carries the family, so a variant is not an independent family member and never appears in a sibling's `relatedPoints`. `build-grammar.ts` throws if `formality.json` gives a variant both (the fix for the じゃ-vs-それじゃ inconsistency, where じゃ (n5-004) had wrongly stayed a `sequence-then` member while それじゃ (n5-005) did not). Surfaced on `GrammarDetailScreen` by `GrammarVariantsCard`.

> [!NOTE]
> `formalityLevel`/`usageNote`/`family` are populated for every point with a sibling a learner could confuse it with, via `gokan-dataset`'s `data/raw/grammar/formality.json`: 123 families cover 665 points since the family-coverage pass, and the 76 left without one are 40 conjugation drills plus one-off constructions (`docs/GRAMMAR_TEACHING_MODEL.md` in the dataset explains the model). All three fields are optional and inert wherever a point has none. `compiled/grammar/index/families.json` (`Record<familyId, { name, memberIds }>`, mirroring `index/jlpt.json`'s shape) is also compiled but not directly consumed anywhere in the app yet - reserved for a possible future family-browsing view.
>
> **Contrast lessons (issue #62)**: `compiled/grammar/index/contrasts.json` (`Record<familyId, { name, lessons: { id, title, points, cases: { focus, vs[], situation, guidance }[], taughtInChapterId? }[], interchangeable? }>`) carries authored *situational* disambiguation between confusable family members - the "which one do I reach for, and why not the obvious alternative" that a per-point `usageNote` cannot answer (から and ので both mean "because", but only ので fits an apology). Three distinct concerns: a **lesson** is a confusability-first sub-grouping of a family (target 5-6 points, soft cap; a small family can be one lesson, a large one is split); a **case** is one concrete situation inside a lesson, and may only name points that lesson covers; and **interchangeability** is a separate grading rule (below). A lesson is NOT a chapter - a chapter is a slot in the introduction order, a lesson is a set of points learners mix up, and they cut across each other by design (a lesson was called a "chunk" until the collision proved confusing in practice). The dataset's `docs/GRAMMAR_TEACHING_MODEL.md` explains the whole model in plain English and `docs/CURRICULUM.md` is the generated inventory of every chapter and lesson. Authored in the dataset's `data/raw/grammar/contrasts.json` (AI-drafted, human-reviewed), compiled + strictly validated by `build-grammar.ts`'s pure `compileContrasts` (every `focus`/`vs`/`points` id must be a genuine, non-dropped member of the family it claims, never a `variant`-axis point, and each case's `focus`/`vs` must be among its own lesson's `points`). Each case is **directed** - `focus` is the point met LATER in the teaching order. Consumed via `GrammarService.loadContrasts`/`loadContrastsByFocus`. Surfaced by `GrammarContrastCard` at the `focus` point's introduction, but only once every `vs` sibling is already known (the deferral rule is `utils/grammarContrast.utils.ts`'s pure `selectReadyContrasts`), and revisitably on `GrammarFamilyScreen` (route `/grammar/family/:familyId`, linked from `GrammarRelatedPointsCard`'s "Compare when to use each"). Coverage is now the full set the curriculum calls for: **68 families, 99 lessons, 144 cases**, one per chapter-cluster with a real distinction to teach (it was 4 families / 6 cases at the starter batch). The family-coverage pass added the confusions N5 learners hit first: だれ / どなた, だれか / だれでも / だれも, なに / なん, and もう / まだ. lessons are scoped to the confusable set rather than to the family wherever the family grouping was too coarse - `sequence-then` is split into a そして/それから lesson and a じゃ/それでは lesson, because those mean different things and a four-way lesson over them would be incoherent.
>
> Two fields come from the curriculum rather than the authored file. **`lesson.taughtInChapterId`** is stamped by `build-curriculum.ts` (the only build step that knows the chapters): the chapter of whichever point it covers the teaching order introduces last, i.e. the earliest moment at which every point the lesson names is a real memory. The app does not gate the introduction-time card on it - `selectReadyContrasts`'s "every `vs` sibling known" check is the per-learner runtime equivalent - but it IS what the end-of-chapter review step keys off (see Grammar Activity → Chapter-driven introduction below: `selectChapterEndFocusIds` collects every lesson anchored to a completed chapter). The curriculum issue assumed the stronger rule that a lesson may not span chapters **at all**; that rule would delete the から/ので lesson issue #62 opens with (から is introduced in `n5-c16`, ので in `n4-c12`), so what the build enforces instead is the invariant that carries the weight: **a case's `focus` may never be introduced before one of its `vs` siblings** (a hard error). 10 of 99 lessons span chapters, and the build counts them.
>
> **`family.interchangeable`** is the `axis: 'variant'` members, when a family has two or more. Those can never carry a lesson (a build error if one tries), but staying silent about them is worse than a line of text: a learner meeting the fourth of ten near-identical "whether A or B" forms with no comment concludes a distinction exists and goes hunting for one. `GrammarService.loadInterchangeableByPoint()` keys them by point id; `GrammarContrastCard` renders the note in place of a lesson, deferred by `selectKnownInterchangeableSiblings` until at least one sibling is actually known, and `GrammarFamilyScreen` renders an "Interchangeable" card. A family can carry this and `lessons: []`, so an entry with no lessons is valid rather than missing.
>
> **Interchangeability grading (issue #62)**: a family sibling typed into a cloze pattern blank in place of the expected marker is graded by *proximity* rather than flat-wrong, gated by a per-point **`slot`** (`clause-final` / `sentence-initial` / `predicate-final` / `pre-noun` / `adverbial`, authored in `formality.json`). `applyFamilyInterchange` (`grammarSelectors.ts`) widens a pattern blank with siblings that fill the **same slot** and are `register`/`variant` axis: same formality -> added to the pattern slot's `accept` (grades `correct`), different formality -> its `near` (grades `minor_error`), reusing the exact two-tier mechanism the variant-group rotation uses. A different-slot sibling (でも in a clause-final けど blank is ungrammatical) or a `constraint`-axis sibling (changes meaning) is never added, so it stays `wrong`. `slot` is authored only for the starter families' register members so far.

**`GrammarExample`** - `jp`/`romaji`/`en` plus `words: GrammarExampleWord[]`, a build-time tokenization of `jp` (via kuromoji) where each word is resolved against the compiled vocab dataset. Concatenating every word's `surface` reconstructs `jp` exactly. Also carries `patternWordIndices: number[]` - indices into `words[]` for this example's grammar-pattern markers, precomputed in `gokan-dataset` by matching `formation`'s literal Japanese against `words[]` (surface/`baseForm`/reading); empty when the pattern couldn't be confidently located in this specific example (rare - see the Grammar Dataset section and `gokan-dataset`'s pattern-location issue). The app never re-derives this - `computeBlankPlan` just reads it.

**`GrammarExampleWord`** - `{ surface, vocabId, reading?, baseForm? }`. `vocabId` is `null` for particles/symbols/anything unresolved (always shown literally, never blanked); when resolved, `reading` is the reading of this occurrence for a conjugated word (き for the 来 of 来ました) and the matched vocab's primary reading otherwise, embedded at build time. A conjugated token is linked by its dictionary form first: its stem is often also another word (来 "next", 教え "teaching"), and the old surface-first lookup linked those instead, which made a blank's kana answer grade wrong and sent reinforcement credit to the wrong vocab. `baseForm` is kuromoji's dictionary/base form (e.g. `"思う"` for the conjugated token `"思っ"`), only set when it differs from `surface` - captured for build-time pattern-matching, currently unused at runtime. `computeBlankPlan` (`grammarSelectors.ts`) additionally does one runtime `VocabularyService.loadVocab(vocabId)` fetch per selected blank, at card-load time (not on submit, so grading stays synchronous) - this both supplies a full accept-list of writing variants for grading (surface, embedded reading, plus the fetched vocab's `writtenForm.kanji`/`alternatives`, `reading.primary`/`alternatives`, and any `mergedVocabs` readings) and the English gloss shown by the per-blank hint control.

**`GrammarProgress`** - User's SRS progress for one grammar point, mirroring `VocabProgress` but with a single `entry: SRSEntry` (no reading/meaning split - a grammar quiz has exactly one quiz type) and a single `needsRetry?: boolean` (not per-type).

### Listening Library (`media.model.ts`)

Compiled per-episode anime vocabulary (`compiled/media/`, see Build & Development → Media Dataset), plus the learner's own marks.

- **`MediaIndexEntry`** (`media/index.json` is an array of these): `id` (the Jiten deck id), `kind: 'anime'`, `title: { original, reading, romaji?, english? }` (`reading`: the original title in hiragana, read by kuromoji at dataset build time, for the search; names are sometimes misread, AniList's `romaji` covers them), `releaseYear?`, `episodeCount`, `speechSpeed` (morae per minute, 0 = unknown), `difficulty` (Jiten's estimate, ~0-5, stored but not displayed), `links: { anilist?, myanimelist? }`, `genres: string[]` (Jiten's genre names), `tags: string[]` (up to five of Jiten's community tags voted by at least 60%), `cover?: { urlSmall, url, urlHiRes, color?, source: 'AniList' }` (URLs of AniList's 100px, 230px and 460px files on its CDN, never the images; see Media Dataset), `source: { name: 'Jiten', url, license: 'CC BY-SA 4.0' }`.
- **`MediaTitle`** (`media/{id}.json`): the index entry plus `episodes: MediaEpisode[]`.
- **`MediaLibraryWords`** (`media/library.json`): `Record<mediaId, [vocabId, occurrences][]>`, each title's whole-series words, so the library page ranks every title by coverage without fetching each title file. Kept out of `index.json`, which the Main hub loads just for covers.
- **`MediaEpisode`**: `number`, `title`, `speechSpeed`, `sourceUniqueWords` (every word Jiten counted, Gokan vocabulary or not), `words: [vocabId, occurrences][]` (**Gokan vocabulary only**, most frequent first).
- **`WatchedEpisode`**: `{ watched, updatedAt (epoch ms), coverageAtWatch? (0..1) }`, stored in `UserProgress.watchedEpisodes`. `coverageAtWatch` records how much of the episode's vocabulary was known at the moment it was marked, so the page can show progress since ("71% when watched").

### Session State (`state.model.ts`)

**`SessionState`** - Current quiz session state
- `'review'`: Due reviews exist
- `'learn'`: Can add new words
- `'session-complete'`: This session cleared everything it committed to, but reviews the per-session cap left out are still due (see Session quiz cap under State Management). Vocab only: `GrammarSessionState` excludes it alongside `'learn-kanji'`.
- `'waiting'`: Waiting for next review
- `'exhausted'`: No vocab left at all

---

## Services & Business Logic

### SRS Service (`srs.service.ts`)

Core SRS algorithm implementation. **This is the heart of the learning system.**

#### Key Methods

### Answer matching (`utils/answerMatching.ts`)

**One comparison for every quiz.** Reading, meaning, production (both cards), grammar blanks and the conjugation drill all compare a typed answer with an accepted form through `matchAnswer(input, expected, leniency)`, and pick the best of an accept-list through `matchBest(input, accepted, leniency)` (correct > minor_error > wrong, a literal "pass" short-circuits). A quiz decides only WHICH forms it accepts (its accept-list) and, where warranted, its `Leniency`; it never compares strings itself. The meaning quiz used to carry its own copy of the fuzzy logic (`compareMeaning`/`normalizeMeaning`), now folded in here: a typo is graded the same whichever quiz asked.

The rule depends on the script of the expected form:
- **Kanji**: never fuzzy (会社/会話 are one character apart and different words). Same kanji skeleton plus a dropped okurigana tail (六 for 六つ, 食 for 食べる) is `minor_error`; it is a prefix test, so 上げる/上がる stays `wrong`.
- **Kana**: edit distance in **romaji keystrokes** (`kanaToRomaji`): つま for つむ is one key (`minor_error`), やむ for つむ is three (`wrong`). An answer **shorter** in kana than the expected one is a typo only from `kanaDropFromLength` kana up (6 by default): below that a missing kana usually makes a different word (おばさん/おばあさん, ゆき/ゆうき, きて/きって), above it it does not (ひつようじゃなかた for ひつようじゃなかった, reported). This replaced a flat "never shorter" rule that marked every dropped っ wrong whatever the word's length.
- **English**: normalized first (case, punctuation, parenthesised notes, a leading "to"/article), a partial answer is `minor_error` ('pain' for 'painful'), and the typo budget scales with length (0 under 3 letters, 1 from 3, 2 from 6).
- **A descriptive gloss** (the `gloss` leniency, set only by `meaningSlot`): when the accepted form has two content words or more, the answer is also compared word by word, in any order (`matchGlossWords`, issue #122). "for", "of", "a", "an", "the", "to", "and" and "or" do not count. Words joined by "or" are alternatives: "counter for pieces or things" and "counter for things" are `correct` for "counter for (small) things or pieces". When "or" joins every content word ("victory or defeat", "more or less", "this or that", the common shape in the dataset) the pair is the meaning, so the gloss stays strict and either half alone is `wrong`; "or not" is never an alternative ("believe it or not"). An answer missing another content word ("counter" alone) or with a mistyped one is `minor_error`; an answer with a word the gloss does not have ("counter for people") is `wrong`. A one-word gloss never takes this path, so "this" stays wrong for "that" (see Meaning quiz: sibling demonstratives), and no Japanese answer does either.

**`Leniency`**: `'standard'` everywhere by default; `'lenient'` (two kana typos from 8 kana, a dropped kana from 4) is set by the conjugation drill (`GrammarBlankPlan.leniency`, set in `computeConjugationPlan`), whose answers are whole conjugated forms where a missed key is a slip. `'gloss'` (standard typos, plus the content-word match above) is set by `meaningSlot`. The tolerances are one table (`TOLERANCE`) in the module, so adding a level or retuning one changes every quiz consistently. Kanji stay strict at every level.

Inflection acceptance (`isFormOfWord`, "another form of the word") is a separate question from typo tolerance and stays an exact match: an answer that is both another form and mistyped is two errors.

Which forms a quiz accepts is decided by the exercise engine's slot builders (see Exercise engine below), never by a quiz itself: `readingSlot` (the readings), `meaningSlot` (the glosses, parentheses stripped, split on `;` and `,` but not inside a number, graded with the `gloss` leniency) and `wordSlot` (a word to produce, on a production card or in a grammar sentence). A word's written forms go through `matchAnswer`'s kanji-skeleton rule, and its tolerance is a **prefix test, not a distance test**, which is load-bearing: a distance test would also swallow 上がる/上げる and 始まる/始める, which differ by one okurigana kana and are genuinely different words. Absent kana cannot make a different word; a substituted one can. This replaced an exact-only written match that graded 六 for 六つ as `wrong` at -0.40 (reported from production), and fixed the opposite defect on the grammar side, where kanji blanks went through the plain distance test and accepted 会社 for 会話 as `minor_error`.

**`applyAnswer(vocab, quizType, quizMode, result, latencyMs, now, growthLevel?, frequencyModifier?, meaningQuizEnabled?, productionQuizEnabled?, strengthDeltaModifier?)`**
- Updates VocabProgress for an already-graded result: grading happens in the exercise engine, so this takes the result, never the typed answer
- Calculates new memory strength and interval
- Returns updated progress + result + interval
- Called only by `applyEffects` (`services/exercise/effects.ts`), enforced by lint, like every SRS write an answer makes

**`rescheduleForRetry(vocab, quizType, now, meaningQuizEnabled?, productionQuizEnabled?)`**
- No credit, no penalty: strength untouched, `needsRetry[quizType]` set, and the due date moved to now plus the unchanged interval (at least `minInterval`), since a retry never touches scheduling and the answer that starts one has to settle it. `GrammarSRSService.rescheduleForRetry(progress, now)` is the grammar twin. Both back the `retry` effect.
**`calculateNextState(entry, result, latencyMs, now)`**
- Core SRS formula implementation
- Adjusts memory strength based on:
  - Answer result (correct/minor_error/wrong/pass)
  - Response latency (faster = easier)
  - Current difficulty
- `growthLevel` (the quiz type's calibration level, see SRS calibration below) multiplies a successful answer's gain; the interval is `strength x lnTarget x frequencyModifier`, with no level in it.
- Optional trailing `strengthDeltaModifier` (default 1.0) scales the strength delta (`resultFactor * L * D`). Untouched for vocab; the Grammar activity uses it to modulate a successful grammar answer's gain by how many of the sentence's vocab blanks were right (see State Management → Grammar Activity State).
- Returns new SRSEntry + interval

**`refillQueue(currentQueue, kanjiKnowledge, settings, maxToAdd, ignoredIds)`**
- Adds new vocabulary to learning queue
- Respects kanji knowledge constraints
- Uses the learner's order: by kanji (their kanji method), frequency, kanji coverage or JLPT
- Returns updated queue

**`findCandidatesJLPT(activeIds, kanjiKnowledge, maxToFind, ignoreKnownKanji?)`** (private)
- Backs `preferredLearningOrder: 'jlpt'`. Walks N5 → N1 (`JLPT_LEVELS`), frequency-ordered within each level, off `index/jlpt.json`.
- **Kanji-filtered by default**, same as every other order - only skipped when `settings.ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement` is on. The mode's defining feature is following the official level lists exactly (a user studying for a specific sitting covers that level's vocabulary as published); enforcing known kanji by default keeps that combined with the app's kanji-aware learning goal instead of overriding it, while the opt-in toggle preserves the old unconditional behavior for anyone who wants a level's list exactly regardless of kanji already studied.
- The JLPT lists cover only ~6.4k of ~36k words, so once drained it **falls back to `findCandidatesFrequency`** (respecting the same `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement` flag) rather than stranding the user on the "exhausted" screen. `countLearnableVocabulary` mirrors this, delegating to the frequency count only when the JLPT pool hits zero - the two pools overlap heavily and must never be summed.
- `kklc` is unaffected by `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement` either way, since it's gated by step, not by kanji set.

**`preferredLearningOrder: 'kklc'` ("By kanji") follows the learner's own kanji method**, not only KKLC: `findCandidatesByKanjiStep` and `countLearnableVocabulary` walk steps 1..`kanjiKnowledge.step` of `VocabularyService.loadKanjiStepIndex(method)`. For KKLC that is the dataset's `index/kklc.json`, exactly as before; for any other method it is derived in the app by `kanjiStepIndex` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`, the same function the dictionary's kanji-order pages use) with the dataset's own rule: a word comes in at the position of its last kanji, and a word with a kanji the method does not teach, with no kanji, or learned in kana never does. When the method has unlocked no new word, the next kanji offered (`SET_NEXT_KANJI`) is the method's next one (`nextKanjiStep`). The setting keeps its stored value `'kklc'`.

**`applyVocabIntroChoice(progress, choice)`**
- Handles "Learn" or "Skip" on intro card
- **Learn**: Sets `nextReviewAt = now` (becomes immediately reviewable)
- **Skip**: Sets stage to `'graduated'`, maxMemoryStrength on **all three** entries (never appears again). Mastering production here is required, not incidental: skip is the one path that writes `'graduated'` directly, and leaving production unmastered would make every skipped word fail `isVocabFullyMastered` and return as a production review

### Exercise engine (`services/exercise/`)

**Every quiz is an exercise, and every exercise runs on one engine.** An activity (vocab, grammar) hosts exercises; what differs between them is data, never a separate code path. Four layers, each the only place its question is answered:

1. **What is asked** (`types.ts`, `builders.ts`). An `Exercise` is `{ kind, host, label, slots, cue }` plus what its card shows, keyed by `kind` (`'reading' | 'meaning' | 'production' | 'production-cloze' | 'grammar-cloze' | 'conjugation' | 'study'`). `host` is the item it reviews (`HostItem`: one direction of a word, or a grammar point). `slots` are the answers typed, one `AnswerSlot` each: `accept` (graded `correct`, typo-tolerant), `near` (`minor_error` at best), `leniency`, `role` (`core` slots decide the result, worst of them; `support` slots only scale the reward), `reveal` (what a fully revealed hint writes, and what a miss shows), `gloss` (the first hint) and, on a slot asking for a dictionary word, `word` (the `vocabId` credited when it is answered right, its `lemma` for inflection, its `otherForm` grade and its near-synonyms). `cue` is the English in front of the learner, which decides whether a near-synonym answers a slot. Exercises are built once, at load, by `vocabExercise(vocab, item, { sentence, cloze })` and `grammarExercise(point, plan)` (the plan from `computeBlankPlan` already carries its slots). Everything grading needs is resolved there, so grading is pure and synchronous.
2. **Grading** (`slots.ts`, `grading.ts`). The slot builders are the only place accept-lists are assembled: `readingSlot`, `meaningSlot`, `wordSlot(vocab, { vocabId?, occurrence?, otherForm, role, synonyms })`. `wordSlot` serves a production card (`otherForm: 'correct'`: any form of the word answers, issue #95) and a grammar sentence's word blank (`otherForm: 'minor_error'`: the sentence also tests the form, so another form of the right word, and the dictionary forms of a conjugated occurrence, are near misses). `gradeSlot(slot, input, hintLevel, cue)` runs the same steps in the same order for every exercise: a revealed hint is `minor_error`; an empty answer is `pass`; then `matchBest` over `accept`, then over `near`, then the slot's text with one of its conjugated words in another form (`inflections`, a grammar marker's: correct for がある, the same form in the other politeness, when the card asks for no register; a near miss for がありません), then `isFormOfWord` graded by `otherForm`; then, only on a genuine miss, the word's near-synonyms (see Production quiz synonym-aware grading), and on a demonstrative's meaning slot, on anything short of correct, a sibling's meaning (`paradigm`, see Meaning quiz: sibling demonstratives). `gradeExercise(exercise, answers, hintLevels)` returns `{ slots, overall, strengthModifier, autoAdvance, message }`: `overall` is the worst of the core slots (a neutral slot, `isNeutral`: a confusable synonym or a sibling demonstrative, is left out), `strengthModifier` is the support coefficient (`SUPPORT_COEFFICIENT_FLOOR` 0.5 up to 1, by the fraction of support slots right), `autoAdvance` is true only when every slot was strictly right with the word itself and the kind allows it (never the meaning card, which has glosses worth reading), and `message` is every feedback string. `gradeExercise` is the only grader: `matchAnswer`, `matchBest`, `isFormOfWord` and `orderSynonymsForCue` can be imported only inside `services/exercise/` (lint), and the dataset's synonym pairs (`VocabSynonym`, `Vocabulary.synonyms`) are read only by `services/exercise/wordRelation.ts` (check:conventions, `one-synonym-reader`).
3. **Effects** (`effects.ts`). What an answer does to SRS state is data. `effectsOf(exercise, grade, { latencyMs, hintLevels })` returns `Effect[]`: `review` (the asked item, through the one SRS formula), `retry` (no credit, no penalty, asked again), `reinforce` (indirect production credit to another word) and `defer` (the study card: only the due date moves). One rule set for every exercise: a slot answered with a near-synonym never credits the slot's word, and an accepted synonym credits the word typed instead; if that slot's word is the asked item itself (a production card), the item is retried rather than reviewed; if every deciding slot was neutral (`isNeutral`: a confusable synonym, a sibling demonstrative's meaning), the item is retried too; any other word answered right without its hint revealed earns indirect credit, once per word. `applyEffects(progress, effects, { now, settings })` is **the only place an answer writes SRS state** (lint allows `SRSService.applyAnswer` / `applyProductionReinforcement` / `rescheduleForRetry` and `GrammarSRSService.applyAnswer` / `rescheduleForRetry` / `deferWithoutCredit` nowhere else). Calibration is recorded for `review` effects only. A `reinforce` is dropped while the asked item is in retry (a training redo earns nothing, which is also what stops a synonym being farmed on the retry loop) and when production quizzes are disabled. It returns one `AnswerRecord` (the asked item's own delta, plus `vocabDelta` and its per-word `vocabBreakdown`) for the ticker and the session gains. Effects are decided at submit, with the latency frozen there, and written on Continue by the reducer against the **current** progress, so a Drive reconcile landing in between is kept.
4. **Turn state and cards** (`context/quiz/exerciseReducer.ts`, `useExerciseTurn.ts`, `components/quiz/`). `QuizState.turns` holds one `ExerciseTurn | null` per activity (`{ exercise, answers, hintLevels, feedback, isEvaluating }`), with one action family (`EXERCISE_SET_ANSWER`, `EXERCISE_REVEAL_HINT`, `EXERCISE_EVALUATING`, `EXERCISE_SUBMITTED`, `EXERCISE_CONTINUE`, each naming its `host`) that `quizReducer` delegates first. `useExerciseTurn` is the one submit / continue flow: it grades, runs the optional asynchronous second opinion (`aiRefine.ts`, the AI meaning check in context mode), decides the effects, writes them on Continue, and moves on by itself after an answer with nothing to read (`grade.autoAdvance`, only while the activity's own page is on screen). An empty answer can be submitted on the grammar cloze and the conjugation drill (`submitsEmpty`: see Submit is never gated on filled blanks), not on the single-answer vocab cards. Both orchestration hooks use it and keep only loading and the session lifecycle. Cards come from three shells, chosen in one exhaustive switch (`pages/exercise/ExerciseCard.tsx`): `SingleAnswerCard` (reading, meaning, production from a gloss, the conjugation drill), `SentenceClozeCard` (the production cloze and the grammar cloze: the sentence rendered through `InteractiveSentence` around one `ClozeBlank` per slot, split by `splitAtBlanks` in `utils/clozeSentence.utils.ts`, a missed blank revealing its form as a ruby linked to its word) and `StudyCard`. The per-kind prompts live with their pages (`pages/quiz/VocabPrompts.tsx`, `pages/grammar/GrammarPrompts.tsx`).

A new exercise kind fails to compile until it has a builder, a card and SRS handling (`switch-exhaustiveness-check` on `ExerciseKind`, `Effect['kind']` and `HostItem['kind']`). A parity test (`grading.test.ts`) grades the same inputs on a production slot and a grammar word blank, typos, other forms and near-synonyms included, so the two cannot drift apart. Outside the engine, and per activity: queue selection, the session commit and the intro flow, already shared where the activities agree (`computeSessionState`, `useSessionLifecycle`, `refillCandidates`).

### Production quiz rollout (`srs.service.ts`, `migration.service.ts`)

The production quiz (English meaning prompt, Japanese reading answer) is the third vocab quiz type and the only direction that starts from English, so it carries its own `SRSEntry` rather than sharing reading's or meaning's. It grades through `wordSlot` (see Exercise engine): every reading and written form of the word, and any form of it.

Adding a third direction to an existing queue is the whole difficulty here: done naively it multiplies a long-time user's daily workload overnight. Four things prevent that, and each is load-bearing.

- **The migration fills in an inert entry, never a due one.** `backfillProduction` gives a learning word `dueDate: null`, which no due-check matches, so shipping this made **nothing** due. The entry sits dormant until something activates it.
- **Activation is lazy, and rides the user's own review curve.** `SRSService.seedProductionEntry` brings a word's production entry online the first time that word is answered in any other direction. A word reviewed monthly therefore picks up production a month from now, so the new direction spreads over one full review cycle instead of arriving as a single wave. There is deliberately **no** backfill pass that sets real due dates.
- **Already-graduated words are grandfathered as production-mastered.** This is the single most important line in the migration. Graduation is *derived* (`isVocabFullyMastered`), not merely stored, so an unmastered production entry would fail that predicate for every word the user ever skipped or finished and hand the entire completed pile back as production reviews. `'skip'` is how users say "I already know this word", so that pile is typically large.
- **A word that an answer just finished is never re-opened.** `applyAnswer` checks mastery *excluding* production before seeding, and skips activation when the word is already fully mastered in the directions it was being trained in. Without this, the answer that would have graduated a word instead attaches a fresh unmastered entry to it, which is the same wave one word at a time. This is also what keeps the existing "graduates on reading mastery alone when meaning quizzes are disabled" invariant true.

Strength is seeded from the word's **meaning** entry at a discount (`CONSTANTS.srs.production.seedStrengthRatio`, 0.4) rather than from zero. Producing a word from English is harder than recognising it, so this is well under parity, but a half-known word should not be drilled from scratch: seeding lower than the user's real ability just inflates the backlog it took care to spread out. The first review is pushed `seedDelayHours` (24h) out so it lands outside the session that activated it.

One ordering detail that a future change could easily undo:

- **`applyAnswer` selects entries by a keyed lookup, not a reading/meaning ternary.** With three types a ternary is not a style question: `quizType === 'reading' ? reading : meaning` silently routes every production answer into the **meaning** entry, corrupting its schedule with no type error to catch it. The same applies to the mastery-delta computation in `applyEffects`.

**No same-session collision handling lives in `applyAnswer` anymore.** An earlier version had a correct reading answer stagger a due meaning's `dueDate` +12h so the two weren't asked back to back, but deliberately left production unstaggered (it re-seeds against reading's own cadence, so a fixed push just repeated every session and production was never once asked - reported from staging after the first release: reading and meaning came up daily while production never appeared). That asymmetry, and the stagger's second-order bug (it could resolve a committed meaning task's `SessionStats.done` without the user ever answering it - see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`), were both symptoms of solving a session-layer problem inside the SRS formula. It's now solved once, at the session layer: `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` (`quizSelectors.ts`) keeps at most one task per vocab per session (reading > meaning > production) when the committed set is built, uniformly across all three types, without mutating any entry's persisted schedule - see Session quiz cap below.

**Counted in the knowledge curve** (`knowledge.utils.ts`), alongside reading and meaning: production is a real direction the learner studies, and excluding it meant a production answer moved the session total while the curve ignored it. A word mastered in all three directions is worth 600.

The care needed is in the curve's **no-history fallback**, which credits an entry at the word's introduction date when it carries no review logs (that is what makes a skipped "I already know this" word count at all). The migration grandfathers every already-graduated word to production-mastered so it does not un-graduate, and those entries have no logs either - crediting them through that fallback would date ~200 points at each word's introduction and rewrite years of curve the learner never earned. So for production the fallback applies only when `totalReviews === 0`, which is exactly the skipped-at-intro case: there the learner asserted they knew the word, reading and meaning are credited on that same basis, and production is no different. A word with real review history waits for a real production review before counting. Daily activity (`buildDailyActivity`) includes `production.history` unconditionally, since that is purely a record of reviews actually done.

### Production quiz synonym-aware grading (`services/exercise/`, issue #71 Part B)

The production cue is a bare gloss list (or a cloze sentence), which cannot distinguish near-synonyms sharing overlapping glosses: 強い/丈夫 (both "strong"), 縛る/締める (both "tie/fasten"), 並ぶ/並べる (a transitivity pair, both "line up"), 作文/作曲 (both "composition" - an English homograph, essay vs music), 必ず/常に (both roughly "certainly/always"). A wrong production answer is therefore checked against the target word's near-synonyms and graded in one of two tiers rather than flat `wrong`. The default stance is deliberately **lenient** (accept a defensible near-synonym as `minor_error`, so a learner is never marked flat wrong for a word the gloss cue genuinely cannot exclude - this now extends to single-shared English homographs like 作文/作曲, a resolved product decision); only a hand-curated set of genuinely-distinct pairs (必ず vs 常に) is demoted to the no-credit `confusable` tier. See the Dataset bullet for the clustering rules and the start-lenient/tighten-later policy.

**A word is a synonym of another only in a given sense, so the outcome depends on the card's own text.** 狭い and 小さい share only "small": 小さい answers 狭い in "Japan is a small country" but not in a sentence about a narrow street. A verdict computed once per pair at build time cannot know which sense a quiz uses, which is why both reported misses (狭い / 小さい, 人物 / 男) were graded flat `wrong`: their overlap (0.14, 0.10) sat far below the ratio floor. The fix keeps detection lenient and moves the decision to grading time:

- **Membership is a shared gloss both words use the same way** (gokan-dataset's `usesMatch`, #162). It excludes four cases:
  - an English verb gloss vs a noun one (述べる "to state" / 国家 "state"; a suru noun's bare gloss and an expression match either);
  - a prefix, suffix or counter sense vs a word (来 "next ..." / ここ "the next ... days"); such a sense pairs only with another, unless the two share at least 3 glosses without this rule (`BOUND_FORM_SHARED`: 以外, a suffix, and 他 share six);
  - an interjection or conjunction sense vs a word of another kind;
  - a rare, archaic or dated sense, the ones a word page hides, unless the word has no other (婦人).

  **These rules change grading, on purpose.** #162 lifted "nothing accepted today is refused" (#150) for dataset pairs: a removed pair grades `wrong`, and a pair moved from = to ≈ is neutral out of context instead of `minor_error` (in context it is still `correct`). On a verb's card its own noun is still accepted through the verb's own forms (答え on 答える: `correct`); on the noun's card the verb is now `wrong`. Both directions, and 以外 / 他, are pinned in `reportedSynonyms.test.ts`.

  Each entry carries `shared` (those glosses), `overlap` (shared over the smaller gloss count) and `curated` (tier set by hand). The original three rules below only set `relation`, the **out-of-context tier**. The rules were measured against outside references before being adopted, with what they cost and what they cannot see (one English word shared in two meanings, 国家 / 容態 "state"): see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`.
- **One reader, one table** (`services/exercise/wordRelation.ts`, issue #150). `relationOf(pair, cue)` reads a pair against the card's text: its tier, whether it was curated, its shared meanings and the one the cue uses (`sharedMeaningInCue`). `outcomeOf` is the one table of what it earns:

  | Pair | Shared meaning in the card? | Earns |
  |---|---|---|
  | curated confusable | either | neutral |
  | any other | yes | `correct` |
  | interchangeable (auto or curated) | no | `minor_error` (accepted) |
  | confusable (auto) | no | neutral |

  In context the answer is `correct`, still flagged as a synonym with a message naming the tested word, and the card pauses instead of auto-advancing so the notice can be read. A curated `confusable` pair is never upgraded by context (必ず / 常に share "always" and stay confusable even in a sentence that uses it). The table is lenient on purpose and is never tightened to fix a report: a reported false positive is curated in the dataset instead. 止む and 埋める share "stop" (from 埋める's "to stop (a gap)") and are auto interchangeable, but on "Fill in the blanks" 止む is not an answer (reported), so the pair is curated `confusable`. The auto tier was switched off out of context from 2026-10-03 to issue #150 (see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). `OUTCOME_LOOK` says how each outcome reads everywhere: the slot's result, whether it is neutral, whether it credits the typed word, whether it is drawn as accepted. Grading, `effectsOf`, `missStyleOf`, `blankBorderClass` and the ticker read it rather than re-deriving it: a neutral answer is muted on the card and shown as neutral in the ticker (`AnswerRecord.shown`), and an accepted one (`minor_error` out of context) is never drawn as wrong (no overlay, no incorrect border, its heading "The word being tested").
- **Known false positives, kept on purpose.** Context is the whole English sentence, so a meaning shared on another word counts too: on a 後 card, "After that, all that is left is basic member.", 左 is accepted through "left".
- **Reported cases, on the real dataset.** `services/exercise/reportedSynonyms.test.ts` builds the real exercise for each reported word from its compiled vocab file and grades the typed answer through `gradeExercise`; it fails when the dataset submodule is missing rather than passing vacuously. A newly reported case is added to its table. gokan-dataset's `build-synonyms.test.ts` pins the same pairs as built, and its build fails on a curation it would apply wrongly (an id the scan skipped, a pair both excluded and clustered, one pair in two clusters with different tiers).
- **The cue** (`productionCueOf`) is the English sentence on a cloze card and the printed glosses on the gloss card (`glossPromptLines`, the lines the card renders, from the same builder). Sentence matching is whole-word, phrase-aware and tolerates light English inflection on any word ("lined up" for "line up", "thinks" for "think"); printed glosses compare after the dataset's own `normalizeGloss`, mirrored in the app and pinned to it by a test. The cloze card bolds the word with the same matcher (`glossSpanIn`), so the meaning bolded is the meaning grading reads.
- Measured cost of the wider membership: about 237k pairs instead of 120k; the vocab files grew from 40 MB to about 68 MB raw, 17 MB to 21 MB compressed. A learner only ever downloads the file of the card being shown. The #162 rules above bring it to 217,054 pairs, and the vocab files (with each entry's rank) to 92.8 MB raw, 30.1 MB gzipped file by file (98.4 MB and 30.0 MB before #162, measured the same way).

- **Dataset & delivery (per-vocab, no monolithic index)**: near-synonyms are **embedded on each `Vocabulary`** as `synonyms?: VocabSynonym[]` (`VocabSynonym = {id: string; relation: SynonymRelation; shared?: string[]; overlap?: number; curated?: boolean; w?: string[]; r?: string[]; pos?: string[]}`, `SynonymRelation = 'interchangeable' | 'confusable'`), written into `compiled/vocab/{id}.json` at build time by `build-synonyms.ts`. There is **no** `compiled/index/synonyms.json` anymore: at full vocab coverage the combined index is ~11MB (several times the largest other whole-loaded index, frequency.json ~3.2MB) and too heavy to hold in browser memory, so each word's list ships with its own vocab file (already fetched to render the card - lazy, zero extra request). Symmetric (if A lists B, B lists A); a word with no `synonyms` field is inert. Membership is auto-derived, deliberately **lenient**, **regardless of part of speech**, across **all** vocab (not just the top-N by frequency - learners drill words like 作文/作曲 near rank 14k, which the old 8k cutoff excluded entirely). A shared gloss both words use the same way makes a pair (see above); the pair's out-of-context tier is `interchangeable` when ANY of the following holds, `confusable` otherwise (`autoTier`). Every ratio counts all of a word's glosses, rare senses included, so leaving a sense out of membership never promotes a pair:
  - **`overlaps`** - share >=1 (`MIN_SHARED`) normalized sense forming >= `OVERLAP_RATIO` (0.30) of the smaller word's gloss count. This is deliberately low enough to accept **single-shared English homographs** (作文 "essay" / 作曲 "music" share only "composition", 1/3 = 0.33): a resolved product decision to accept a gloss-matching answer rather than mark it wrong, even when the shared English word is a coincidence. A single shared gloss must also be >= `SINGLE_GLOSS_RATIO` (0.25) of both words, shared / √(|A|·|B|): もう一人 shares only "another" with 今's 11 glosses, 1/3 but 0.17. Applied to every pair, that ratio demoted real pairs sharing several glosses (また / 再び), so it applies to one shared gloss only (`docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`).
  - **`senseCovered`** - one word has a whole >= `MIN_COVERED_SENSE` (2) gloss SENSE the other expresses entirely, which the flattened ratio dilutes away on a polysemous word (一番/最高 share 2 of 一番's 18 glosses, but one whole sense is "best, most"). Its covering side is capped at `MAX_COVERING_GLOSSES` (12) so a 54-gloss verb can't absorb the index.
  - **`isTransitivityPair`** - a 自他 pair: same leading-kanji stem (`kanjiStem`), opposite transitivity (one `vi`, one `vt`), and >=1 shared gloss. Catches 並ぶ/並べる (share only "line up", 0.2, below the overlap floor) that are the same verb in different transitivity. The stem + opposite-vi/vt + shared-gloss triple keeps it precise (見る/見つかる share the 見 stem and opposite transitivity but no gloss, so they do not pair).

  Every word with glosses is scanned, whatever its part of speech: the loader used to skip any word without a major class (noun, verb, adjective, adverb), so expressions, interjections and conjunctions had no synonyms at all and 気をつける graded wrong on a card for 気を付けて (`exp`), though both mean "be careful" (reported). That brought the index to about 249k pairs. Two earlier guards were removed as too strict: a required shared major POS (blocked 強い/丈夫, which share four glosses but are い-adj vs な-adj) and a >=2-shared-gloss floor (`MIN_SHARED` is now 1). Checked per sense in #162, the POS guard still loses 一番 / 最高, so membership compares only what the gloss itself shows (`usesMatch`, above). Auto pairs that meet one of the rules above are **`interchangeable`** (graded `minor_error` out of context, see the table above); the hand-authored `data/raw/vocab/synonyms.json` then **demotes** genuinely non-interchangeable pairs to `confusable`, **excludes** false positives, and **adds** pairs that do not gloss-overlap (mirrors how `formality.json` backs the grammar `family`/`usageNote` fields). Policy is start-lenient, tighten later. `build-synonyms.ts` reads every compiled vocab file and rewrites the ones whose `synonyms` changed (skipping unchanged files, so a no-op rebuild touches nothing).
- **Candidates are matched locally, from forms embedded on each entry.** With one shared gloss making a pair, a word lists 8 synonyms at the median but up to several hundred (取る, 439). Each entry therefore carries the other word's answerable forms (`w` written forms kanji-first, `r` readings primary-first including merged homographs' readings, `pos` its inflecting POS codes only), added by the dataset's `build-synonyms.ts` (`synonymForms`); `VocabSynonym.w`/`r` are required in `@gokan/dataset-schema`. A slot asking for a word carries its candidates from the start (`synonymsOf(vocab)` in `wordRelation.ts`, when the exercise is built), so grading tries them with no request at all, only once the word itself has graded `wrong`, entries whose shared meaning the cue uses first (`orderSynonymsForCue`). There is no fetch fallback any more: 0 of 487,490 synonym entries in the compiled dataset lacked forms, and the dataset ships with the app (see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). The forms cost about 0.3 KB compressed on an average vocab file (worst case about 36 KB raw, for a word with 571 pairs).
- **`gradeSlot`'s synonym step** grades each candidate exactly as a production card asking for it would (`wordSlot(candidate, { otherForm: 'correct' })`), so a candidate's conjugated or kanji form matches it too. **The closest match wins whatever the candidate order**: one of a candidate's own forms beats any other correct match, which beats a typo. With dozens of candidates the input can be one word's exact form and another's typo at once (つむ is 積む exactly, but used to match 止む as a typo first), or another's form only through the lenient kanji skeleton (止む is 止む, but 止す's 止 plus any kana grades it too).
- **Credit goes to the word typed, never to the one tested** (`effectsOf`, see Exercise engine). Crediting the tested word for a collision would reward exactly the confusion the issue opens with (必ず and 常に are precisely *not* interchangeable). A synonym that answers the card (`correct` in context, or `minor_error` for an interchangeable pair out of context) credits the typed word through indirect production credit, and the card's own word is **retried** rather than reviewed: the learner has not produced it yet. A neutral synonym (a confusable pair out of context, a curated confusable one anywhere) earns no credit and no penalty, and the card's word is retried too. `SRSService.rescheduleForRetry` sets `needsRetry[quizType]`, leaves `memoryStrength`/`interval`/`difficulty` untouched and moves the due date to now plus the unchanged interval (at least `minInterval`), re-deriving `nextReviewAt`: a retry never touches scheduling, so the answer that starts one has to settle it, as a wrong answer's does. It used to stay at the old, already-past due date, so once the retry was answered the word was due again at once and came back in the same session as a full review with points (reported). The card re-asks until the target itself is produced, so there is no way to coast on a synonym, and answering the retry with the same synonym again earns nothing more: indirect credit is never granted while the asked item is in retry. The message names the word that was actually being tested (e.g. "きっと (きっと) is also accepted here - the word being tested was 必ず (かならず).").
- **An unrelated wrong answer, or a word with no synonyms, is unaffected**: the slot keeps its own grade.
- **The same on every card that asks for a word**: both production cards and a grammar sentence's word blanks, whose cue is the example's English (pattern markers have none: another word in the point's own form is simply wrong). Every card names both words in its feedback, with `LookUpWords` to open either.

### Synonyms and mix-ups on a word's page (`UserProgress.mixups`, issues #150, #162)

A word's page shows its synonyms from the dataset and what the learner typed on its cards instead of it (Your mix-ups). The page is one narrow column in levels (DESIGN_SYSTEM section 5, Word page): the essentials show the first sense's closest synonyms (up to 5, `closestSynonyms`, never a ≠ pair), and Explore holds every synonym in its Synonyms panel (`pages/vocab/explore/SynonymsPanel.tsx`) and the records in its Mix-ups panel, opened on request, each with a search (`utils/exploreSearch.utils.ts`). The synonyms are computed once per page (`useWordSynonyms`) for the tiles, the panel's count and the panel. The Mix-ups page of #150 was removed: it grouped the learner's errors, not synonyms, and some of its words were never typed (see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). `/vocab/mixups` redirects to `/vocab`. A quiz's synonym note links to the asked word's Synonyms panel ("Compare synonyms", `synonymsPath`: `/vocab/{id}#synonyms`, a consult route, so the session pauses): the hash opens Explore on Synonyms, and the page scrolls to Explore once its examples, the one part above it that loads after the word, have settled; a scroll on the first render left it pushed down the screen.

- **The Synonyms panel and Closest synonyms** (their logic in `utils/synonymSections.utils.ts`). The pairs cannot be cut into groups (one shared gloss makes one: 狭い has 30, 取る 439), and filtering them does not remove the noise: it comes from English homographs (強い "firm" lists 商会 "firm", a company), which no threshold sees, and requiring two shared meanings leaves 773 of the 3,000 most frequent words with no synonym (狭い / 小さい shares one) while still listing 114 for one word. So the panel lists every pair, under the sense it shares, in an order that puts useful pairs first, a few at a time:
  - one section per sense with a synonym, labelled by its glosses; a synonym is listed once, under the sense whose glosses (after `normalizeGloss`) hold the most of its shared meanings, the earliest on a tie. A rare or archaic sense (`isRareSense`) gets no section, unless every sense of the word is rare (婦人), since the dataset pairs a word through the same senses; a pair set by hand with no meaning in common goes under the first sense;
  - a word outside the 10,000 most frequent (`f`, the rank the dataset embeds on each entry, see below) is listed only when the learner studies it;
  - order: words mixed up with this one (most often first, both directions), curated pairs, words in `learningQueue`, the most shared meanings in this sense, interchangeable before confusable, the most frequent;
  - Closest synonyms: the first section's first 5 that are not ≠. The panel: 4 groups at a time ("Show 4 more meanings"), plus any group holding a mixed-up word, 3 rows a group ("Show all N for this meaning"); a search keeps the grouping and every group that matches. Measured for a learner who studies the 3,000 most frequent words, on their pages: 5 closest synonyms at the median (no page with a section shows none); the panel lists 20 words at the median (p90 67, max 322, 取る) in 2 groups (p90 5, max 20), and 325 pages have more than the 4 groups shown at first; 175 of them have no synonym, so no tiles and no Synonyms segment (160 before the #162 membership rules).
  - Each row's label comes from `closenessOf` (`wordRelation.ts`), which reads the pair through `outcomeOf` in the section's sense (its glosses as the cue) and out of context: never accepted even in this sense is **≠ Not interchangeable** (a curated confusable pair, 必ず / 常に), accepted out of context too is **= Interchangeable**, accepted only when a card uses the sense is **≈ Close in this sense**. It cannot disagree with grading. A row names the word from the forms on the entry (`listedSynonymsOf`), so no vocab file is loaded per row.
- **The dataset's rank on each pair.** `VocabSynonym.f` is the other word's 1-based position in `index/frequency.json`, absent when unranked, written by gokan-dataset's `build-synonyms.ts`: a word page cannot load that 3.2 MB index for this. 428,126 of 434,108 entries carry it.
- **Recorded by the engine.** `effectsOf` emits a `mixup` effect for any slot asking for a word (production, the production cloze, a grammar sentence's word blanks, retries included) answered with something else, never for a revealed answer or a skip: a synonym grading identified (`outcome` `accepted` or `neutral`, as `OUTCOME_LOOK` reads it), or a `wrong` answer it did not. A record keeps `answer` (as typed, always), `outcome`, `meaning` (the shared meaning the card used, when accepted in context), `cue` (the card's English sentence; absent on the gloss card) and `date`. **`typed` names the other word only when the answer was that word**: one of its forms, or an inflection listed by the generator (`SynonymAnswer.match === 'form'`, from `findSynonym`). A typo of it, the loose kanji-skeleton match (`isFormOfWord`'s stem fallback: 止む passes as a form of 止す) or an unidentified answer is kept as typed only. Their grading is unchanged by this: those are still read as the synonym in the quiz, only the record stops naming it. The pair's tier is not stored; the card reads it live from the dataset.
- **Stored** capped at the newest `MIXUP_LIMIT` (500, `utils/mixups.utils.ts`), compacted behind `StorageService` (`mx`; `outcome` as 0/1/2 under `oc`, `cue` under `cq`, dates as epoch ms). 500 records each with a sentence stay under 100 KB (pinned in `progressCompaction.test.ts`). Drive merges a union keyed by (asked, answer, date). Hydration drops a record without `outcome`: it predates #162, cannot say where the mistake was, and some named words that were never typed.
- **Your mix-ups** (`pages/vocab/YourMixups.tsx`, Explore's Mix-ups panel, reached also from Your study's "You mixed it up N times"): the records where this word was asked (`mixupsAsked`), newest first, 5 then "Show all mix-ups", searchable by the answer and the card's sentence; each shows the answer (a link when `typed` names a word), how it was graded, the card's sentence or "From its English meanings", and the date. A record of this word typed on another word's card belongs to that word's history; both directions count toward the Synonyms panel's "Mixed up N times" (`mixupCounts`).
- Not built yet: per-word nuance notes (#150 phase 3), which would sit on a Synonyms panel row. The per-group drill (phase 4) assumed groups and was dropped; a drill would start from a word's card.

### Meaning quiz: sibling demonstratives (`services/exercise/demonstratives.ts`, issue #123)

それ answered "this" is the wrong word ("this" is これ), so it is never accepted: the こ/そ/あ/ど distinction is what the card teaches. But a flat miss on this classic paradigm taught nothing, so it is graded like a confusable synonym: **neutral** (no credit, no penalty, the word retried through the same `isNeutral` path) with a note naming both words and the series ("それ means "that (near you)". "this" is これ. こ = near me, そ = near you, あ = over there, ど = which.").

- **The series are a curated table** (six series of four: これ, この, ここ, こちら, こう, こんな and their siblings), each member with the meaning the note states and the English answers that name it, plurals included where the dataset glosses them (この "these", あの "those"; その glosses no plural, so その answered "these" is この). The dataset has no relation for them, and the set is closed. The note names the tested word by the exercise's label (`headwordOf`, like every tested word); only the siblings are named from the table.
