- **A word is a member only when its reading matches AND its own glosses include one of the member's answers** (`demonstrativeParadigm`). The reading alone is not enough: こう is also 項 and 校, ああ in the dataset is the interjection 嗚呼, そこ is also 底. 嗚呼 carries none of ああ's demonstrative meanings, so it has no series.
- `meaningSlot(glosses, reading)` attaches the series when the exercise is built (`AnswerSlot.paradigm`); `gradeSlot` tries the siblings on any answer short of correct, and only an **exact** sibling meaning counts (a typo of "this" is too close to "that" to tell which word was meant). An exact sibling meaning outranks a typo of the word's own: "here" on あそこ is one edit from "there", but it is ここ's meaning, so it gets the series note, not a near miss. An answer matching two siblings names both ("that" is それ or あれ). Production and reading slots never carry a series.
- The AI meaning check (`aiRefine.ts`) leaves a neutral grade alone: no sentence makes "this" mean それ.
- **A neutral miss looks neutral** on `SingleAnswerCard`, a confusable synonym as much as a sibling: the near-miss look (`border-l-secondary` note, `border-secondary` input) and no miss overlay, so the learner sees nothing was lost. `missStyleOf` (`components/quiz/quizStyles.ts`) decides it from `isNeutral`; the "Correct answer" label stays.
- The compiled dataset has 19 of the 24 members today: no こんな, そんな, あんな, どんな, and no demonstrative ああ (only 嗚呼). Those rows only name a sibling in a note (こう answered "like that" is そう or ああ) until the words are added.

### Production cloze card (`productionCloze.utils.ts`, `clozeSentence.utils.ts`, `SentenceClozeCard.tsx`, issue #72)

A second production card format, served by the vocab queue exactly like the gloss-prompt card (both set `quizType: 'production'` and share `applyAnswer`/scheduling) - only the CUE differs. The gloss-prompt card asks "which word means this?" off a bare gloss list, which cannot distinguish near-synonyms sharing overlapping glosses (必ず vs 常に both gloss roughly "certainly/always"). This card instead blanks the target word inside one of its own example sentences and prompts with the sentence's English translation - the surrounding Japanese and the English sentence together narrow the answer to one word, the way a gloss list structurally cannot.

**No dataset work was required.** `Sentence.matches?: Record<vocabId, {start, length, reading}[]>` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`) already carries the exact span to blank for a given vocabId - the same field both apps' sentence rendering (`segmentInteractiveSentence`, `@gokan/dataset-schema`) uses to link/highlight matched words in a sentence.

- **`pickProductionClozeSentence(vocabId, sentences, learner, vocab?)`** (`productionCloze.utils.ts`, pure) - restricts to sentences carrying a usable `matches[vocabId]` entry whose blanked span is actually READ as the target word (`readingMatchesWord`, `inflection.utils.ts`, applied whenever `vocab` is passed - the real caller in `useQuizOrchestration` always passes the loaded vocab; omitting it skips the guard and is used only by selection-logic tests), then picks with the shared ranker (`pickSentenceForVocab`, see Shared sentence ranking below): the sentence built most from words the learner is currently learning, sticking until those words mature. The reading guard exists because the dataset keys sentence `matches` by WRITTEN form, so a form shared by differently-read homographs (遊ぶ is both あそぶ and the rare すさぶ) could leave a sentence about あそぶ carrying a match for the すさぶ entry - whose cloze would then blank 遊んでる and grade it `correct` against a "grow wild" cue (reported). `readingMatchesWord` accepts an inflected reading that keeps the dictionary stem (あそんでる for あそぶ), rejects one that does not (あそんでる is not a form of すさぶ), tolerates trailing auxiliaries/particles the inflection tables do not enumerate, and normalizes katakana (a loanword's hiragana reading こーひー matches its katakana コーヒー). The root cause is also fixed in `gokan-dataset`'s `build-data.ts` (`disambiguateByReading` / `src/utils/readingDisambiguation.ts`), which no longer assigns a match to every vocab sharing a written form regardless of reading - the app guard is defense-in-depth that also protects data built before that landed. Returns `null` when no sentence has a usable match - the caller reads that as "fall back to the gloss-prompt card" (coverage is inherently partial: not every vocab has sentences, and not every sentence containing a word was tokenized with a resolved match for it).
- **`splitAtBlanks(clozeSentence)`** (`clozeSentence.utils.ts`, pure) - the sentence's parts in reading order: the text between blanks as `Sentence` fragments (each carrying only the matches entirely inside it, offsets rebased) and the blanks, so `SentenceClozeCard` renders the context through the shared `InteractiveSentence` (clickable, gloss-on-hover words) instead of flat text. Concatenating every part reproduces `sentence.original`; a match straddling a blank boundary is dropped. The production cloze is one blank (`productionClozeSentence`), the grammar cloze one per span (`grammarClozeSentence`), so both clozes render the same way.
- **`emphasizeGloss(englishText, glosses)`** (pure) - decides how the English cue signals which word to produce (the bare translation was too ambiguous to tell). Matches the target vocab's own glosses (cleaned of parentheticals and a leading "to"/article, longest/most-specific first, whole-word, ≥3 chars) against the translation: on a hit, returns the span to **bold in place**; on no verbatim hit (conjugated/reworded translations, e.g. "has" for gloss "to have"), returns `labelGlosses` for the card to show as a small gloss label instead. There is no word-level JP↔EN alignment in the dataset, so this is deliberately best-effort with a guaranteed fallback so every card indicates the word one way or the other.
- **Word-type cue** (`getCoarsePosLabels`, `quizFormatting.ts`) - both production cards (this one and the gloss-prompt card) show the target's part of speech as small chips next to the JLPT chip: a **coarse plain-English** label ("verb" / "adjective" / "noun" / "adverb", collapsing JMdict subclasses), NOT `TagsLookup`'s full grammatical description ("Ichidan verb", "noun (common) (futsuumeishi)") the reading/meaning cards use. On a production card the learner is *producing* the word, so "am I giving a verb or a noun?" is a genuine disambiguator when a gloss like "strong" or "tie" fits several words. Deduped, priority-ordered (verb, adjective, adverb, noun); usually one label, occasionally two (a suru-noun is both). It never leaks the target, so it shows before feedback. The gloss-prompt card previously showed the verbose `TagsLookup` tags here; both cards now share the coarse helper.
- **Resolved once at load time, not at selection time.** `getNextVocabToStudy` picks a production item the same way regardless of cloze/gloss (queue selection has no sentence data available synchronously); `useQuizOrchestration`'s loading effect fetches `VocabularyService.loadSentences(vocabId)` for every production card (not just meaning's `context` mode) and computes `pickProductionClozeSentence` before dispatching `LOAD_VOCAB_SUCCESS`, so which card renders is already decided by the time loading finishes - no extra async round trip after render, and grading stays synchronous.
- **The card is decided when the exercise is built**: `vocabExercise` returns a `'production-cloze'` exercise (carrying the `ProductionCloze` and its `ClozeSentence`) when a usable sentence was found, the `'production'` gloss card otherwise, and `ExerciseCard` renders `SentenceClozeCard` with `ProductionClozeHeader` or `SingleAnswerCard` with `ProductionPrompt` accordingly. The meaning card's context sentence travels the same way, on its `'meaning'` exercise.
- **Grading** goes through the gloss card's `wordSlot`, with the blank's occurrence (its conjugated surface and reading) added as ideal forms - the cloze card changes only the prompt, not what is accepted or the schedule it drives.
- **The blank is the sentence's conjugated surface** (食べたら in 野菜を食べたら？), not the dictionary form. `ProductionCloze.blankReading` carries that span's own reading (たべたら) from the same `matches` entry, and `blankSurfaceOf` gives the surface. The slot accepts both (`wordSlot`'s occurrence forms), the reveal shows the surface with the reading as furigana, and the level-2 hint writes the surface into the answer (issue #95).
- **The revealed word opens its page** (issue #99): on both production cards the revealed answer is a button (dashed underline) calling `onVocabClick`, and when the answer collided with a near-synonym, `LookUpWords` (`components/LookUpWords.tsx`) offers both the tested word and the typed one (the grade's `synonym`). Opening either pauses the session rather than ending it.
- **On anything short of a strict `correct`** (wrong, `confusable`, `pass`, a typo graded `minor_error`, or a synonym), the blank reveals the blanked surface (not the dictionary form) with `blankReading` as furigana, a `ruby` right beside the input, styled `text-feedback-correct` and linked to the word's page: the same reveal every blank of the grammar cloze shows. This replaced showing only a bare reading once below the whole sentence: a learner who blanked 必ず saw かならず at best, never the word 必ず itself. The input is left holding whatever was typed, unchanged.
- **Per-blank hint control**, the same on every sentence cloze: the turn's `hintLevels` (0 = none, 1 = gloss shown, 2 = answer revealed). Reaching level 2 (`EXERCISE_REVEAL_HINT`) writes the slot's `reveal` into the answer, so the card stays submittable, and `gradeSlot` grades a revealed slot `minor_error` whatever it holds.
- **Deliberate departure from the gloss card's "nothing Japanese before feedback" rule** (see `ProductionPrompt`'s doc comment): the surrounding sentence is visible before answering, since it's the cue that makes the item well-posed. The invariant that still holds on both cards is that the **target word** stays hidden until feedback.
- **Full production credit**, not a discount: unlike a grammar blank (where the vocab is incidental scaffolding around the pattern under test), the target word here is the whole point of the card and no part of the answer is shown - its `review` effect is the gloss card's, `strengthModifier` 1.

### Shared sentence ranking (`utils/sentenceRanking.ts`)

**One rule decides which example sentence a card shows**, for every activity that picks one: the grammar review (`computeBlankPlan`'s productivity pass), the vocab production cloze (`pickProductionClozeSentence`) and the vocab meaning-in-context card. It replaced three unrelated policies (grammar argmax, a review-count hash rotation for the cloze, and `Math.random()` for meaning context, which even re-rolled on reload).

The goal is **comprehensible input**, not just familiarity (issue #85): reading research puts unassisted comprehension at roughly 98% known-word coverage (Hu & Nation 2000), so a single unknown word in a short sentence already drops well below that threshold. An unknown word must therefore decide the ranking on its own - it replaced an earlier `targets - readabilityPenalty * unknowns` formula where one extra known word could outweigh almost three unknown ones, which favoured long sentences dense with known words over short clean ones.

Each RESOLVED word gets one role (`wordRole`): **unknown** (resolved to a vocab id the learner has not introduced), **target** (introduced, **production** ring below `CONSTANTS.srs.sentenceSelection.targetRingCeiling`, 100 = first loop full), or **context** (ring at the ceiling, neutral). An unknown word weighs 1, or `CONSTANTS.srs.sentenceSelection.unintroducedKanaWeight` (0.5) when this sentence writes it in kana at every occurrence (the text of its `matches` spans in `original`, a grammar word's `surface`; a word with no span counts as written with a kanji): it can be read, only its meaning may be unknown. How the sentence writes it decides, not `usuallyKana`: 為る written 為る weighs 1. Without this, the kana spans linked through Tatoeba's annotation (する, こと, ある, issue #132) made a sentence a beginner can read except for a する not started yet tie with one holding an unknown kanji word. Both scorers reach it through `scoreResolvedIds`. A word that never resolved to a vocab id at all is invisible to those roles, but is not free either - it feeds the same unknown tally, weighted by how readable it still is on its own: contains a kanji, weighs 1 (the same as a resolved-unknown word - it cannot be read either way); is katakana-only and 2+ characters, weighs `CONSTANTS.srs.sentenceSelection.unresolvedKatakanaWeight` (0.5 - it can at least be sounded out, and is usually a name or loanword rather than genuine unknown vocabulary); anything else (hiragana function words, particles, punctuation, digits) weighs 0. Measured on the corpus at the time this was written: 7% of corpus-mined grammar sentences carry an unresolved kanji word, and 2%/19% of vocab sentences carry an uncovered kanji/katakana word respectively - all invisible to the old ranker, which only ever scored words already resolved to a vocab id.

How "not resolved" is found differs by sentence shape, so there is one scoring entry point per shape rather than a single function guessing at the data. `scoreGrammarExample` walks `GrammarExample.words[]` directly (each word is already segmented, so its `vocabId` says whether it resolved), excluding `patternWordIndices` entirely - the pattern is blanked and is the grammar under test, never an unknown. `scoreVocabSentence` has no word-level segmentation to read, only `Sentence.matches` span offsets into `original`; it scores the text NOT covered by any vocab id's match spans for maximal kanji/katakana runs, with each uncovered fragment (the text between or around covered spans) scored independently, so two separate unresolved words split apart by a known word in between are never merged into one longer run.

Ranking keys, in order (`compareSentenceScores`):
1. **`unknownWeight`**, lowest first - decides the ranking outright whenever it differs, whatever the target count or length.
2. **Length band** (`Math.ceil(length / CONSTANTS.srs.sentenceSelection.lengthBand)`, band size 10), shortest first: bands rather than raw length, so a 14-character sentence does not automatically beat a 15-character one, and ties within a band still fall to the learner's own words below.
3. **`targets`**, most first.
4. The targets' **summed production ring**, highest first. This is what makes a sentence **stick**: each review raises its words' rings so it keeps winning, until a word reaches the ceiling, drops out of the sum, and the sentence rotates. It rotates because its words were learned, never on a timer.
5. A per-sentence hash on a stable seed (the card's own id, **never a review count**), so an exact tie resolves the same way every time.

The vocab cards (`pickSentenceForVocab`/`scoreVocabSentence`) exclude the tested word from the target/unknown tally, since every candidate contains it - its own match span still counts as covered text, just not toward the score. Grammar excludes pattern-marker words the same way (blanked regardless). Deliberately **not** done: rotating among the top few to avoid sentence memorisation. Discussed and rejected in favour of the stickiness above; if memorisation proves a problem, alternating the production cloze with the gloss-prompt card is the recorded mitigation.

**Curated examples compete with the mined pool on a grammar review** (`selectProductivePlan` in `context/quiz/grammarSelectors.ts`, renamed from `selectMinedPlan`, issue #85): on a review turn (`reviewCount >= 1`), the point's own curated examples that already have a located pattern (`patternWordIndices` non-empty) are ranked together with its corpus-mined pool using the exact same keys above, rather than a mined sentence always winning outright whenever one qualifies - previously a short, clean, hand-written example lost automatically to any mined sentence with a target, even a longer or messier one. A curated example with no located pattern is not a candidate here at all: it cannot be blanked on the pattern, and the existing curated fallback chain (`computeBlankPlanFor`) already handles that case on its own. The eligibility gate is unchanged and applies to both sources alike: at least one target (the targets become the vocab blanks). When neither source has a single eligible candidate, this returns `null` and the caller falls through to `computeBlankPlanFor`'s own curated passes exactly as before. The intro card and the very first review (`reviewCount === 0`) stay on curated examples only, unaffected by any of this.

### Inflection generator (`utils/inflection.utils.ts`, issue #95)

**Knows how a word conjugates**, so a conjugated answer is not compared character by character against one fixed string. Before it existed, any form other than the exact expected one fell into `analyzeError`'s kanji-skeleton rule and graded `wrong`, and the kana of a conjugated surface was accepted nowhere.

- `wordClassesOf(vocab)` reads the inflection classes from the JMdict POS tags in `sense.pos` (`v5*` godan, with 行く `v5k-s`, ある `v5r-i` and `v5aru` as special cases; `v1` ichidan; `vk` 来る; `vs-i` する; `vs-s` 愛する, which takes both する and godan す forms; `vs` noun + する; `adj-i`; `adj-ix` いい; `adj-na`). A word can declare several (心配 is a な-adjective and a する-noun) and every class contributes forms.
- `generateInflections(word)` returns `{ written, kana }` pairs: polite, negative, て/た and everything built on て (ている, ちゃう, とく...), ば, volitional, imperative, たい, plus potential, passive, causative and causative-passive, each expanded once as an ichidan verb (so 食べさせられた is produced). Adjective tables cover the usual tense/polarity/politeness forms, including 大変じゃなくて. Written and kana stems are built in parallel; 来る keeps 来 written while its kana stem moves between こ, き and く.
- `kanaOfSurface(surface, word)`: the kana of a generated surface (食べたら gives たべたら), or null.
- `isFormOfWord(input, word)`: exact match against any written/kana form, or a fallback for forms the tables do not list: a kanji answer with the same kanji skeleton that starts with the written **stem** and continues in kana. The stem prefix is what keeps 上げる from counting as a form of 上がる (stem 上が) and 会社 as 会話. A bare stem (書 for 書く) is not a form; it keeps `matchAnswer`'s dropped-okurigana `minor_error`.
- `InflectableWord` (`{ written, readings, classes }`) is small on purpose, so it can ride on a `GrammarBlankPlan` in reducer state.
- `sameFormUpToRegister(a, b, word)`: true when two forms are one tense and polarity of the word, plain or polite (ある / あります, なかった / ありませんでした, 早くない / 早くありません, だ / です / である, ではない / じゃありません), in kanji or in kana. The derived verbs (potential, passive, causative) pair the same way.
- `inferredWord(dictionaryForm, surface)`: the classes of a word read off one occurrence, for a token with no dictionary entry (most grammar markers). The irregular verbs are named (する, 来る, 行く, ある, the honorific ござる group, いい), the copula だ / である is its own `copula` class (forms only, no stem), a conjugated い word is an adjective (the ない of なければ included), and a る verb is ichidan when the occurrence is its bare stem (食べ, いけ), godan after り, ら or っ, and both when it cannot tell (れ, ろ, the dictionary form): these words only ever grade near misses, where the lenient reading is the safe one. Null for what the generator does not conjugate (ます, た, ぬ, a noun).

Consumers: `wordSlot` and `gradeSlot` (`services/exercise/`): the kana of a conjugated occurrence is an ideal form (`kanaOfSurface`), and another form of the slot's word grades by its `otherForm` (`correct` on a production card, `minor_error` on a grammar blank). `hasKanji`/`kanjiSkeleton` live in `utils/kanji.utils.ts`, shared with `matchAnswer`.

### SRS calibration (`services/calibration.ts`)

**One mechanism for every quiz, vocab and grammar alike: each quiz type (reading, meaning, production, grammar) adapts its strength growth to its own recent win rate.** It replaced a single global "adaptive level" that multiplied the **interval** and was capped at x3.

Why growth and not the interval: the scheduler targets ~75% recall per review (`formula.targetRecall`). A learner above that was only ever corrected on the interval side, so strength kept growing by the same fixed factor for everyone and the rings, knowledge points and mastery understated real memory. Reported from a year of real use: 92% over 25k reviews, and the best word at 80% of the first loop while being recalled at 85 days.

- **Formula** (`SRSService.calculateNextState`): the level (`growthLevel`) multiplies a **successful** answer's gain only (a miss costs what it always did); the interval is `strength x lnTarget x frequency preference`, with no level in it. It corrects itself: above target, strength grows faster, intervals lengthen, the win rate falls back, the level settles.
- **Per quiz type**: `UserProgress.calibration: Record<'reading'|'meaning'|'production'|'grammar', { level, history }>`, each with its own rolling window (`CONSTANTS.srs.adaptive`: 50 reviews, adjusts after 10, band 0.70-0.80 centred on the 0.75 target, step 0.05, bounds 0.5-3). Grammar now also applies the user's frequency preference, which it used to ignore (it passed `1.0, 1.0`). Grammar's vocab reinforcement credits production at the production level but does not record a production review.
- **Only real reviews count** (`isCalibratedVocabReview` / `isCalibratedGrammarReview`): not a retry (the answer was just shown), not a word's first review right after its intro, not a confusable-synonym collision (ungraded). These inflated the old window.
- **Seeded from the review logs, not started at x1** (`seedCalibrationFromHistory`, run in `migrateUserProgress` on every load): each quiz type's logged reviews are replayed in date order through the same update rule, giving the window and level it would have reached. Retries never write a log; a word's earliest reading log (or a grammar point's earliest log) within 24h of its intro is dropped as the post-intro check; grammar's vocab reinforcement logs are tagged `ReviewLog.source: 'reinforcement'` and skipped (untagged ones written before the tag existed cannot be told apart). A full live window always wins over the replay, so this fills history in once and self-heals a window that started empty.
- **Merge** (`mergeCalibration`): per quiz type, the side with more recorded reviews, local on a tie. Windows are timestamp-free, so they cannot be unioned. `mergeProgress` spreads `...local`, so without this a remote-only calibration would be dropped.
- **Global win rate stays visible**: Stats' "Global Win Rate" now covers all four quiz types (it counted reading and meaning only), and "Win Rate by Quiz" (`QuizTypeWinRates`) shows each type's all-review rate, its recent calibration window and its growth level (`utils/winRate.utils.ts`).
- **Transition** (`rebaseStrengthsToSchedule`, dispatched as `REBASE_STRENGTHS`): the old interval-only level is folded into strength by raising each entry to what its own scheduled interval demonstrates, `max(S, interval / (lnTarget x frequency))`. For an interval scheduled under the old formula that is exactly `S x level`, with every due date unchanged. It is **idempotent by construction, not guarded by a marker**: under the new formula the expression gives back S. That is load-bearing, because a tab on an older build merges with `...local` and would drop a marker, and re-applying a plain `S x level` would then double it. Unscheduled entries, mastered ones, and intervals at the 1-day success floor are left alone; strength is only ever raised. **It runs only after the Drive sync** (dispatched right after the post-download reload, so it rebases the merged state, then the auto-upload publishes it), or once the initial load completes for a user not signed in. Every level starts at 1.
- **Knowledge curve**: old review logs carried the level in their interval, so inverting them without it yields `S x level`, the same scale the rebase moved stored strength to: one consistent curve, no step at the switch-over.

### Scheduling Service (`scheduling.ts`)

**Single source of truth** for "when is this vocab due" and "is it fully mastered". Previously this question was answered independently in three places (`VocabProgress.nextReviewAt` hand-synced by `applyAnswer`, `reading.dueDate`, `meaning.dueDate`), which could drift out of agreement - e.g. disabling meaning quizzes left a stale `meaning.dueDate` able to make `nextReviewAt` report "due" while queue-selection had already stopped considering meaning reviews.

**Key functions:**
- `isEntryMastered(entry)`: `memoryStrength >= maxMemoryStrength`
- `isVocabFullyMastered(vocab, settings)`: reading mastered AND (meaning quizzes disabled OR meaning mastered) AND (production irrelevant OR production mastered). Production counts as **irrelevant** when the quiz type is off in settings **or** the entry has never been activated - a never-activated entry must not read as `unmastered`, or every word in an existing queue would fail this the day the quiz type shipped
- `vocabNextReviewAt(vocab, settings)`: derives the authoritative `nextReviewAt` - the earliest of the non-mastered reading/meaning/production due dates, **excluding meaning entirely when `enableMeaningQuiz` is false** and production whenever it is disabled or not yet activated
- `isProductionQuizEnabled(settings)` / `isProductionActivated(entry)`: the two gates behind `relevantProductionEntry`, the private helper the three functions above share
- `isVocabDue(vocab, settings, now)`: convenience wrapper (always false for graduated items)

`SRSService.applyAnswer` calls into this module rather than hand-computing `nextReviewAt`/`stage`; `sync/mergeProgress.ts` and the v8+ migration pass do the same, so all three call sites can never disagree.

#### SRS Formula Constants (from `constants.ts`)
- `targetRecall`: 0.75 (75% target recall rate)
- `expectedLatency`: 1500ms
- `minInterval`: 0.2 days (~5 hours)
- `maxInterval`: 3650 days (10 years)
- `maxMemoryStrength`: 626 (≈180-day interval = mastery, the longest retention horizon the research notes recommend; was 1270, ≈1 year, which the research does not support. Lowering it only changes how many points mastery takes: one point is still one ring unit. A word already past 626 graduates on its next correct answer, and words stored at 1270 by "skip" stay mastered)
- `resultFactors`: correct +0.25, minor_error +0.10, wrong -0.40, pass -0.15
- `adaptive`: the per-quiz-type calibration window (50 real reviews, adjusts after 10, raises the growth level above 0.80, lowers it below 0.70, step 0.05, bounds 0.5-3). See SRS calibration.

### Dataset loaders: one request per file (`services/http.ts`)

Every dataset loader (`VocabularyService`, `GrammarService`, `MediaService`) caches through `RequestCache` (or `sharedRequest` for a single file), which holds the **request**, not only its result: callers that ask for a file at the same moment share one download. The loaders used to cache the parsed value after `await`, so the hub's startup calls each started their own: `frequency.json` (3 MB) and the grammar teaching order twice and `conjugations.json` three times per page load (#134). A request that fails is dropped from the cache so a later call retries; what a failure means stays the loader's own (`loadTeachingOrder` returns null, `loadKinds`/`loadConjugations` return `{}`, `loadFrequencyIndex` and `loadVocab` throw). Values derived from a file (`loadUsuallyKanaIds`, `loadContrastsByFocus`) keep their own small caches on top.

### Vocabulary Service (`vocabulary.service.ts`)

Handles loading vocabulary data from compiled JSON files.

**Static Methods:**
- `loadKKLCKanjiIndex()`: Load KKLC kanji index (step → kanji[])
- `loadKKLCIndex()`: Load KKLC vocab index (step → vocabIds[])
- `loadFrequencyIndex()`: Load frequency-sorted vocab index
- `loadVocab(id)`: Load individual vocabulary by ID (cached)
- `loadKanjiIndex()`: Load the full compiled `Kanji[]` array (small, whole-file fetch, cached) and index it by character in-memory
- `loadKanji(character)`: Look up a single `Kanji` by character (used by the Kanji Detail Page)
- `loadKanjiVocabIndex()`: Load the kanji → vocabIds reverse index (which vocab contain a given kanji, sorted by frequency)
- `loadJlptIndex()`: Load the JLPT level → vocab index (level 1..5 → entries, frequency-sorted within a level)
- (Near-synonyms for production grading are **not** a separate index method - they are embedded on each `Vocabulary.synonyms` and come free with `loadVocab`; see Production quiz synonym-aware grading. There is no `loadSynonymsIndex`.)

**Data Location**: `data/compiled/`
- Indexes: `index/kklc.json`, `index/kklc-kanji.json`, `index/frequency.json`, `index/kanji-vocab.json` (kanji character → vocab IDs containing it, frequency-sorted), `index/jlpt.json` (JLPT level → `{id, containedKanji}[]`, frequency-sorted within each level; ~6.4k of the ~36k vocab carry a level). Near-synonyms for production grading are **not** an index file - they are embedded on each `vocab/{id}.json` as `synonyms` (see Production quiz synonym-aware grading)
- Vocabulary: `vocab/{id}.json` (one file per vocab item)
- Kanji: `kanji/{character}.json` (one kanji, what `loadKanji` reads), `kanji.json` (flat array of all `Kanji` objects, read by the dictionary build), `index/kanji-orders/{order}.json` (`loadKanjiOrder`: one learning order's kanji in teaching order), `index/kanji-components.json` (component → stroke count and kanji, for search by component; not read by the app yet)

### Grammar Service (`grammar.service.ts`) and Grammar SRS (`grammarSrs.service.ts`)

**`GrammarService`** - Loads grammar data from `public/data/compiled/grammar/` (synced from the `gokan-dataset` submodule like every other dataset - see Build & Development → Grammar Dataset for how it's produced).
- `loadJlptIndex()`: Load the JLPT level → grammar point ids index (`index/jlpt.json`)
- `loadTeachingOrder()`: Load the authored introduction order (`index/teaching-order.json`, `{ order, chapters }` - see Grammar Activity → Chapter-driven introduction below). Returns `null` on failure so callers fall back to the plain JLPT walk.
- `loadChapterFor(pointId)`: The `GrammarChapter` a point belongs to, from the same teaching order.
- `loadContrasts()` / `loadContrastsByFocus()`: The family → lesson/case contrast index (`index/contrasts.json`) and its focus-point-keyed lookup - see the `GrammarPoint.family` note under Core Data Models → Grammar Activity above, and Chapter-driven introduction below for how `lesson.taughtInChapterId` is consumed.
- `loadGrammarPoint(id)`: Load an individual `GrammarPoint` by id (cached), from `points/{id}.json`
- `loadMinedExamples(pointId)`: Load the corpus-mined `GrammarExample[]` pool for a point (`mined/{id}.json`, issue #73's app half), cached like `loadGrammarPoint`. Returns `null` on a 404 - the common, permanent case (~350/828 points have no pool) - without logging it as an error; a genuine fetch error also degrades to `null` but is NOT cached, so a later call can retry. Consumed by `computeBlankPlan`'s mined-selection pass (see State Management -> Grammar Activity State).

**`GrammarSRSService`** - `SRSService`'s equivalent for grammar, reusing its formula (`SRSService.calculateNextState`, made `static` non-private specifically so this class can call it) rather than inventing a new one:
- `createGrammarProgress(grammarId)` / `applyGrammarIntroChoice(progress, choice)`: mirror `SRSService.createVocabProgress`/`applyVocabIntroChoice`, but against a single `entry` instead of `reading`/`meaning`
- `applyAnswer(progress, result, latencyMs, now, growthLevel?, frequencyModifier?, strengthDeltaModifier?, blankCount?)`: takes an already-combined `AnswerResult` (`gradeExercise`'s `overall`, decided by the pattern blanks, not a naive worst-of-all; called only by `applyEffects`, see Exercise engine). `strengthDeltaModifier` (default 1.0) scales the memory-strength gain by the vocab-correct ratio and is forwarded to `SRSService.calculateNextState`. `blankCount` (default 1, issue #73) divides the measured `latencyMs` by the number of blanks in the exercise before it reaches `calculateNextState`, so a corpus-mined sentence with many more blanks than a curated one is judged on its per-blank pace rather than penalized as "slow" purely for having more to type - the `review` effect passes the exercise's slot count (1 on the conjugation drill). Retry handling (`needsRetry`, a single boolean here) mirrors vocab's per-quiz-type flag exactly, just without the type dimension.
- `deferWithoutCredit(progress, now)` / `rescheduleForRetry(progress, now)`: the study card's Continue (only the due date moves, 24h out) and a retry without credit (the grammar twin of `SRSService.rescheduleForRetry`). The **positive-only** vocab credit a grammar sentence gives its blanks lives in the exercise engine: it is the `reinforce` effect, written by `SRSService.applyProductionReinforcement` into each word's **production** `SRSEntry` (forced result, neutralised latency, scaled by `CONSTANTS.srs.production.reinforcementStrengthRatio`). Filling a blank is English sentence in, Japanese out, which is production's definition; reading asks the opposite question (given the written form, produce its sound) and a blank shows nothing to read, so reading was the one direction of the three this exercise structurally cannot test. Credited at a discount because the conditions are much easier than a production card's: the English sentence, the surrounding Japanese and the bracketing particles narrow the candidates, and kanji is accepted where that card wants the reading. Deliberately **not** split across all three entries - each drives its own schedule, so crediting three would push three due dates out on one scaffolded answer and thin review pressure in two directions it never exercised. A word whose production entry is not yet activated is **seeded first** (`seedProductionEntry`) and credited on top, so it joins the rotation at its designed baseline; a no-op entirely when production quizzes are disabled. Only correct/minor_error blanks are credited, and a word not in `learningQueue` is skipped - so grammar practice can lift a word's vocab mastery but never lower it.
- `getNextCandidates(currentQueue, maxToFind, ignoredIds?)`: walks the dataset's authored teaching order (`GrammarService.loadTeachingOrder()`'s `order`, filtered by `buildTeachabilityFilter` - see below), falling back to a plain JLPT walk (N5 → N1 via `services/jlptWalk.ts`, shared with vocab's JLPT order, source order within a level - no `preferredLearningOrder` setting for grammar, since it has no frequency data to sort by, and no kanji filtering either: kanji-awareness applies to which *vocab words* are blanked in a quiz sentence, not to which grammar points can be learned) only when the order file can't be loaded. **Chapter-bounded**: a batch never crosses a chapter boundary - see Chapter-driven introduction below. `countLearnableGrammar`/`hasMoreLearnableGrammar` are NOT chapter-bound (they answer "is there more content at all, anywhere", for the hub's `moreNew` signal), so the two are allowed to disagree in size.
- `getCurrentChapter(currentQueue)`: the `GrammarChapter` containing the next point that would be introduced right now (the earliest point in `order` that isn't active yet and is still teachable). Derived fresh every call, **never stored** - the dataset can re-cut chapters at any time (it already has, for N3-N1), so a persisted "current chapter" pointer would dangle across a re-cut. Returns `null` once nothing is left to introduce, or when the order can't be loaded.
- `buildTeachabilityFilter()`: excludes points the current quiz cannot actually test - a `kind: 'inflection'` point (teaches a derivation, not a fixed string) is only teachable once `conjugations.json` actually carries drill items for it. Not private specifically so `getCurrentChapter`/`getNextCandidates` and the chapter-completion check in `useGrammarOrchestration` (below) share one definition of "teachable" - a chapter containing an untestable point could otherwise never be considered fully introduced.

### Grammar Activity: Chapter-driven introduction and end-of-chapter lessons

The teaching-order chapter (see `GrammarChapter`/`GrammarTeachingOrder` under Core Data Models) is the unit of introduction, not a flat batch off the dataset. **`advanceGrammarQueue` introduces the whole current chapter in one advance**: it sizes its candidate request to `getCurrentChapter().points.length`, and since `GrammarSRSService.getNextCandidates` stops at the current chapter's boundary anyway, that yields every remaining teachable point of the chapter at once (a chapter is authored as a unit meant to be met together, so splitting it across several advances hid that grouping). `CONSTANTS.srs.grammar.newBatchSize` (3) is now only the **fallback ceiling** for the no-chapter path - when the teaching order can't be loaded and `getNextCandidates` walks the flat JLPT order; it is deliberately not shared with vocab's `newVocabBatchSize`. Crossing into the next chapter happens on a later `advanceGrammarQueue()` call, once every teachable point in the current one is active.

**The current chapter is always derived** (`GrammarSRSService.getCurrentChapter`), never stored: the dataset can re-cut chapters at any time (it already has, for N3-N1), and a persisted pointer would dangle across a re-cut. `useGrammarOrchestration` calls it on every `state.progress` change and turns the result into a `HubChapterStatus` (`describeHubChapter`, `grammarSelectors.ts` - see Chapter progress display below), exposed as `grammarHubChapter`; `MainScreen`'s grammar activity card renders it as a "Current chapter: …" / "Next chapter: …" / "All N chapters introduced" line, shown **unconditionally** alongside its review/new/retry preview (issue #87 - see Application Pages → Main Screen for why it is no longer gated on `preview.new > 0`). `GrammarIntroCard` separately shows a chapter's `title`/`summary` once, on its first point's intro card (pre-existing, from the original teaching-order work - not part of this addition).

**End-of-chapter review step** (`GrammarChapterLessonCard`, `pages/grammar/GrammarChapterLessonCard.tsx`): once every teachable point in a chapter has been introduced, the contrast lessons anchored to it (`lesson.taughtInChapterId === chapterId`) surface once as a consolidated recap, reusing `GrammarContrastCard` per focus point rather than a second set of markup - the same component the per-point intro-time card renders. The two are complementary, not a replacement for each other: the intro-time card is the just-in-time hint (gated per-learner by `selectReadyContrasts`), the chapter-end step is the deliberate review of a now-finished set. A chapter with no anchored lessons shows no step at all - acknowledged silently rather than inventing filler content.

- **`completedChapters: string[]`** (`UserProgress`, see Core Data Models) records which chapters' step has already been shown (or silently acknowledged), so it doesn't re-fire. Purely additive like `grammarQueue`: defaulted to `[]` unconditionally at hydration (`MigrationService.migrateUserProgress`, no version gate) and merged as a pure union in `mergeProgress.ts` (mirroring `mergeGrammarQueues`) - a step shown on one device must not un-show on another.
- **Pure selectors** (`grammarSelectors.ts`): `selectNewlyCompletedChapterIds(chapters, grammarQueue, completedChapters, isTeachable)` - chapters where every TEACHABLE point (mirroring `buildTeachabilityFilter`'s gate, so a chapter stuck on an untestable point isn't stranded incomplete forever) has `introductionAt` set and the chapter isn't already in `completedChapters`. `selectChapterEndFocusIds(contrasts, chapterId)` - the unique `case.focus` ids across every lesson anchored to that chapter, in the order encountered; `GrammarContrastCard` resolves each id's own ready cases on its own, so this only needs to hand it which points to render, not case-level detail.
- **`useGrammarOrchestration`** runs both: an effect (keyed on `state.progress`) recomputes `pendingGrammarChapterLesson` (`{ chapterId, chapterTitle, focusPointIds }` or `null`) - for each newly-completed chapter (in chapter order), a chapter with zero focus ids dispatches `GRAMMAR_CHAPTER_COMPLETE` immediately (no step shown) and moves on; the first one WITH lessons sets the pending state and stops (shown one at a time - dismissing it lets the effect re-run and pick up any more). `grammarActions.dismissGrammarChapterLesson(chapterId)` clears the pending state and dispatches `GRAMMAR_CHAPTER_COMPLETE`, which appends the id to `completedChapters` (no-op, same reference, if already present).
- **`GrammarScreen`** renders `GrammarChapterLessonCard` in place of the loading gate/intro/quiz whenever `pendingGrammarChapterLesson` is set, within the `review`/`learn` states - a deliberate pause on a finished set, not a card the queue serves.

**Families reachable**: `GrammarBrowseScreen`'s family-grouped view (`group === 'family'`) links out to `/grammar/family/:familyId` ("Compare when to use each") on any family group whose `contrasts.json` entry has `lessons.length > 0` - the browse screen's family grouping was already the closest thing to a family index, so it's the natural entry point rather than a new page.

### Grammar Activity: Chapter progress display (issue #58)

Chapters are a **label, not a gate** (decided in #51/#58: gating could stall a learner entirely on one hard chapter, and conflicts with `advanceGrammarQueue`'s due-count-driven refill), but once a chapter exists as authored structure, showing how much of it is actually done is still worth surfacing everywhere a learner might look: the hub, the point's own detail page, and Stats. All four surfaces below compute the same three-way split off the same function, so "how much of chapter N is done" can never disagree between them.

- **`computeGrammarChapterProgress(chapter, grammarQueue)`** (`grammarSelectors.ts`) - a chapter's own point tally: `mastered` (introduced and `isGrammarFullyMastered`), `learning` (introduced, not yet mastered), and `total` (`chapter.points.length`, always the chapter's own count regardless of queue contents). `untouched` is left for the caller to derive (`total - mastered - learning`), mirroring `JlptLevelRow`'s own convention. A point not yet in `grammarQueue` at all counts as untouched, same as one with `introductionAt: null`.
- **`ChapterProgressBar`** (`components/ChapterProgressBar.tsx`) - a slim three-way bar (mastered / in-progress / untouched, solid accent + 35% accent, matching `JlptCoverageBars`' color language) sized for a single chapter rather than a whole coverage report. Takes a `compact` flag that hides the numeric breakdown text, for tight spaces like the hub card.
- **Hub** (issue #87) - `useGrammarOrchestration` derives `grammarHubChapter: HubChapterStatus | null` off the same `GrammarSRSService.getCurrentChapter()` call (never stored, re-derived whenever `state.progress` changes), via the pure selector `describeHubChapter(chapters, currentChapter, grammarQueue)`: `'current'` when the chapter has at least one introduced point, `'next'` when it has none yet, or `'complete'` (once `getCurrentChapter` returns `null`, counted over a synthetic chapter spanning every point in the teaching order) - each carrying the relevant chapter's own `GrammarChapterProgressCounts` via `computeGrammarChapterProgress`. `null` only when the teaching order itself failed to load (the caller checks `GrammarService.loadTeachingOrder()` before calling the selector, since a bare `currentChapter === null` can't tell "curriculum complete" apart from "order unavailable" on its own). `MainScreen`'s grammar activity card renders the status line ("Current chapter: …" / "Next chapter: …" / "All N chapters introduced") plus a compact `ChapterProgressBar` beneath it, both shown **whenever `grammarHubChapter` is set** - no longer gated on `preview.new > 0`, which only ever counts points queued-but-never-reviewed and read 0 for the ordinary state *between* chapters (every introduced point already reviewed once), hiding the line and bar exactly when a chapter was genuinely in progress or a next one was waiting (the bug issue #87 reported).
- **Detail page** - `GrammarDetailScreen` shows a small "Chapter N · x of y" line under the point's title/romaji, linking to `/grammar/chapters?chapter=<id>` (the chapter browser, pre-expanded to that chapter). Computed locally via `GrammarService.loadTeachingOrder()` rather than `loadChapterFor` specifically because the locator needs the chapter's own 1-based position among *all* chapters, which `loadChapterFor`'s point→chapter map doesn't carry.
- **Stats** - `GrammarChapterCoverageChart` (`pages/stats/components/GrammarChapterCoverageChart.tsx`) is a chapter-axis coverage view, reusing the same `JlptCoverageBars` renderer `JlptCoverageChart`/`GrammarJlptCoverageChart` use. This required generalizing `JlptCoverageBars`' row shape from a JLPT-specific `{ level: number, ... }` (hardcoded `N{level}` label) to a generic `{ key: string | number, label: string, ... }`, plus an optional `headline`/`keyColumnLabel` override and a `maxVisibleRows` (default 10) past which the bar list scrolls in a fixed-height pane rather than growing the page unboundedly - load-bearing once there are 151 rows instead of 5. Chapters are a more meaningful coverage axis than JLPT level now, since the curriculum deliberately mixes cross-level register siblings into one chapter (だが N2 sits in the N5 "But" chapter) - JLPT-level coverage increasingly misrepresents what a learner has actually covered. Links out to the chapter browser via a "Browse the full curriculum" button.
- **Chapter browser** (`pages/grammar/GrammarChapterBrowseScreen.tsx`, route `/grammar/chapters`) - all chapters from `GrammarService.loadTeachingOrder()`, in teaching order, each expandable (local `Set<string>` of expanded ids) to its member points. Point rows are resolved from `GrammarService.loadBrowseIndex()` (title/JLPT level per id, already loaded whole for `/grammar/browse`) rather than fetching each point individually, and each carries a small status dot (mastered/learning/untouched, from the same per-point logic `computeGrammarChapterProgress` uses internally) plus a link to `/grammar/:id`. `?chapter=<id>` auto-expands and scrolls to that one chapter on load (used by the detail-page locator and the Stats chart's link) - both the expand and the scroll happen inside a `requestAnimationFrame` callback rather than synchronously in the loading effect, since the chapter's outer container (the scroll target) is unconditionally rendered regardless of expand state. Linked from the Main hub's grammar card ("View all chapters") and reachable from `/grammar/browse` would be redundant, since that page already groups by level/family - the two views answer different questions ("what exists" vs. "what order will I meet it in") and are kept separate rather than merged.
- **`GrammarDifferentiator`** (`components/GrammarDifferentiator.tsx`, pre-existing from the original family work) is now also rendered on `GrammarDetailScreen`, directly above `GrammarRelatedPointsCard` - previously it only ever appeared once, on a point's own intro card, so a learner revisiting the point later (from Stats, search, or a related-points link) had no way to see what actually separates it from its near-synonym siblings. This is a placement fix, not a merge of the two components: `GrammarDifferentiator` renders axis-driven prose ("what separates these"), `GrammarRelatedPointsCard` renders a navigable `RelatedEntriesCard` list ("who are these") - they stay conceptually adjacent rather than combined into one component, since unifying two components with genuinely different rendering models (free-form copy vs. a capped/expandable entry list) is a real design decision left open for a future, more deliberate pass rather than guessed here.

### Storage Service (`storage.service.ts`, `progressCompaction.ts`)

The only module that touches browser storage: `localStorage` and `indexedDB` anywhere else fail lint. **Progress lives in IndexedDB** (#153): one record, key `GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS` in the `progress` store of the `gokan-srs` database, holding the same compacted string localStorage held, so the codec, hydration and the Drive merge are unchanged. Settings, the Drive token, the theme and the last-access day are small and stay in localStorage, read synchronously.

**Why**: browsers cap an origin's localStorage at about 5 MB (and WebKit may count two bytes a character), which a learner with mostly mature words reached at roughly 2,000 to 2,700 words (see Capacity). An origin's IndexedDB quota is a share of the disk: hundreds of MB at least on current browsers.

**Reads are asynchronous.** `loadProgress()` returns a promise, read after any write still queued. `QuizProvider` starts with `progress: null, progressLoaded: false` and dispatches `PROGRESS_LOADED` once the read resolves; until then `App.tsx` renders an empty page, never onboarding (a learner with progress must not be offered a fresh start), and no loader either, since the read takes milliseconds. A read that fails dispatches `PROGRESS_LOAD_FAILED`, the fatal error screen. `PROGRESS_LOADED` keeps progress a Drive download already put in state, since that download merged the stored copy.

**Writes are ordered and never throw.** `saveProgress` serializes the progress at once, then queues the write behind any in flight; when several are queued only the latest is written, so the later save always wins. The persistence effect fires and forgets. A failed write (IndexedDB aborting with `QuotaExceededError`, or a full localStorage after removing the obsolete keys and retrying once) resolves to false and reports through `progressSaveFailed()` / `subscribeToSaveStatus()`, which `useProgressSaveFailed` turns into `StorageFullBanner` (rendered by `App.tsx`) until a save succeeds again. An uncaught `QuotaExceededError` inside a React effect used to take the app down (reported from production). The app keeps running on its in-memory copy, and Drive sync keeps uploading it.

**Migration from localStorage, which cannot lose the only copy.** The store is opened once per page load. If localStorage holds progress, it is written to IndexedDB, read back and compared, and only when equal is the localStorage key removed. If IndexedDB cannot be opened (a blocked or private storage) or the copy fails, progress stays in localStorage and is used exactly as before. Progress found in localStorage while IndexedDB already has a record is not assumed newer: a session that could not open IndexedDB writes it there, but so does a tab still running a build from before IndexedDB, open across the deploy while another tab moved progress and kept studying. The two copies are merged per entry with `mergeProgress` (the record as local, the localStorage copy as remote, as Drive merges), with two differences: kanji knowledge is the union of both copies (every kanji either marked known, the further step when both follow one method), where Drive would take it whole from the record on a tie and drop the old tab's marks; and the merge keeps the record's `_sync`, because a local move is not a sync step and a raised version would win the next Drive merge's kanji comparison against a newer copy from another device. The merge is written, read back and compared before the key is removed. Once progress has been written to IndexedDB, `GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS_IN_IDB` is set in localStorage: a later visit that cannot open IndexedDB and finds no localStorage progress throws `ProgressUnavailableError` (the fatal error, "reload to try again") instead of reading nothing and offering onboarding, whose fresh progress would then be saved and moved over the real one. Clearing progress (a reset) also removes the marker, so a learner with nothing stored is offered onboarding, not the fatal error. A reset in a session running on localStorage also opens IndexedDB to delete the record an earlier session wrote there (the next load would otherwise read it back and undo the reset), and removes the marker once it has; while IndexedDB still cannot be opened the marker stays, protecting that record.

**Eviction.** A browser may clear an origin's storage under disk pressure unless it is persistent. `requestPersistentStorage()` (`navigator.storage.persist()`) is called only from Settings, the Storage section, which says why: Firefox shows a permission prompt for it, so it is never asked on load. The section is shown only when progress is in IndexedDB and the browser can report `persisted()`.

**Keys** (`CONSTANTS.storage`): `GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS` (progress, compacted: the IndexedDB record's key, and the localStorage key it is moved from), `GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS_IN_IDB`, `GOKAN_SRS_SETTINGS`, the Drive token, theme and last-access day. `obsoleteKeys` lists keys older builds wrote and nothing reads: the pre-v8 backups (`GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS_BACKUP_PREV8` and its `_SETTINGS` twin) are removed on every load. Each was a full copy of progress, and the build that wrote it re-created it on every load whenever it was missing, so a large history needed twice its size and deleting the key by hand did not stick.

**Progress is stored compacted** (`progressCompaction.ts`): `compactProgress` runs right before the write and `expandProgress` right after the read, so the code only ever sees the readable field names. A payload without the `"~"` marker (saved before compaction) is read as is. Every rule is lossless once hydrated:
- field names become short codes (`KEY_CODES`); a field not in the table is kept under its own name (escaped with `!` if it would read as a code);
- dates (`DATE_KEYS`) become epoch milliseconds; review logs become `[date delta, result, interval, latency, source?]` tuples, each date stored relative to the previous log's (a log with a field the tuple cannot carry stays an object);
- nulls, values equal to the default hydration spreads back in (`HYDRATION_DEFAULTS`) and a non-graduated item's `nextReviewAt` (recomputed on every load) are left out;
- `stage` becomes 0/1, a boolean history (calibration windows) a `"0101"` string, the kanji set one string.

Floats are **not** rounded by the codec: the Drive copy keeps full precision, and a local copy differing from it by rounding would read as a change to merge (and upload) on every load. A review log's `interval` is the exception that is safe, because it is **born rounded**: `calculateNextState` writes it to 2 decimals (about 15 minutes), so both copies hold the same number. It is only read back by `strengthFromLog` (the history graph); the entry's own `interval`, `memoryStrength` and `difficulty` feed the formula and stay exact. Logs written before keep their precision and age out (20 per entry).

**Capacity.** A mature word is mostly review logs (three entries, up to 20 logs each), and a full-precision log interval was about a third of a log. Rounding it shrinks a word by about 8% at 10 reviews per direction, 19% at 20 and 29% with full history, so roughly 2,000 to 2,700 mature words fit in localStorage instead of 1,500 to 2,000 (#154; moving progress off localStorage is #153). `progressCompaction.test.ts` holds a word answered 20 times per direction through the real formula to at most 1,800 compacted chars (1,778 measured, 2,291 before rounding): a new field that grows every word fails `bun run check` instead of quietly lowering the ceiling. Measured on 2,500 words and 24k reviews: 3.9 MB readable, 1.8 MB compacted (46%); with the old backup copy that was 7.8 MB. In IndexedDB the ceiling is the origin's disk quota: `storage.service.test.ts` saves and loads back 10,000 words with full histories (60 logs a word), a 16.5M-char record, more than three times what localStorage could hold; the maintainer's 1,200 words took 1.7M chars on production. Writing the whole record after each answer is the remaining cost; splitting it into per-word records is worth it only if that proves slow on a phone. Google Drive keeps the readable form, which another device on an older build must still read. **Adding a persisted field needs no codec change, but a new date field must be added to `DATE_KEYS`** (or it is stored as an ISO string, which is still correct, just longer), and a new field with a hydration default can be added to `HYDRATION_DEFAULTS` only if hydration really restores it.

### Google Drive Sync (`services/sync/`)

Cloud sync, split into three modules by responsibility:

**`driveClient.ts`** - Thin wrapper around the Drive v3 REST API (folder/file list, download, upload, trash). No merge/business logic - just HTTP calls and `GoogleAuthError` translation on 401/403, so it can be unit-tested without a network by mocking the class methods it needs.

**`mergeProgress.ts`** (pure, no I/O) - Reconciliation logic:
- `mergeEntry(local, remote)`: merges one `SRSEntry` (reading OR meaning) - the entry with the more recent `lastReviewedAt` wins scheduling-relevant fields (dueDate, difficulty), while `memoryStrength`/`interval` are each taken as the **max** of both sides as a safety net; `history` is a full union deduped by timestamp.
- `mergeVocabProgress(local, remote, settings)`: merges reading and meaning **independently** via `mergeEntry` - a device that only reviewed reading can never clobber another device's meaning review (and vice versa). `stage`/`nextReviewAt` are always **re-derived** via `scheduling.ts`, never merged directly.
- `mergeVocabProgress`'s recency winner (which supplies the merged `lastReviewedAt` and `consecutiveFailures`) counts all three directions' `lastReviewedAt`, production included, so a device that only answered production cards still wins it.
- `mergeLearningQueues`: pure union by `vocabId` (never drops a word).
- `mergeGrammarProgress`/`mergeGrammarQueues`: grammar's equivalent - `mergeEntry` reused directly (one entry, no reading/meaning split to merge independently), `needsRetry` OR'd, `stage`/`nextReviewAt` re-derived via `grammarScheduling.ts`. `mergeGrammarQueues` is a pure union by `grammarId`.
- `mergeProgress`/`mergeSettings`: top-level merge - kanji knowledge is last-version-wins (local wins on a tie, to preserve un-pushed local edits/deletions), stats are field-wise max, `dailyOverride` is OR'd, `grammarQueue` merged via `mergeGrammarQueues`, `completedChapters` merged as a pure union (mirroring `grammarQueue`), `retiredVocabIds` unioned and then the merged `learningQueue` re-filtered by it (so a word one device retired is never respawned by the other device's still-present entry - the durable-against-respawn half of the tombstone, the per-load `migrateUserProgress` filter being the other), `calibration` merged per quiz type via `mergeCalibration`, and the sync version counter always bumps by 1 past the higher input. `watchedEpisodes` is merged per episode by `mergeWatchedEpisodes` (`utils/mediaCoverage.utils.ts`): the newer `updatedAt` wins (local on a tie), so un-marking an episode propagates instead of being re-added by the other device, and it stays `undefined` when neither side has any.

**`googleDriveSync.ts`** (`GoogleDriveSync` class) - Orchestrates the above against Drive:
- **Optimistic concurrency**: captures the remote file's `modifiedTime` when read, re-verifies it immediately before writing, and retries (re-fetch + re-merge, up to 3 attempts) if it changed - closes the classic read-modify-write lost-update window.
- **Duplicate-file reconciliation**: if more than one file matches the expected name (two devices creating it concurrently), all copies are downloaded, merged together, written back to one canonical file, and the rest are trashed.
- **`ensureRemoteBackupOnce()`**: write-once safety net - before this instance's first write, snapshots the current live remote file under `kanji-progress.pre-v8-backup.json` (`CONSTANTS.storage.driveBackupFileName`). No-ops if a backup already exists or there's nothing live to back up. Failures are logged but non-fatal.

**Sync hands its result to the app, never through storage** (#153). `GoogleDriveSync` does not write local storage: `download(localProgress, localSettings)` (the blocking download on load, at sign-in and from Settings' Sync now, which passes the progress on screen) returns the merged envelope, or Drive's copy alone on a device with none, and a background `sync()` returns its envelope in the outcome. `GoogleDriveContext` publishes them as `lastDownload` and `lastBackgroundMerge` (`{ envelope }`, a new object each time), and `useQuizOrchestration` puts them into state; the persistence effect then writes state behind. Reading them back from storage instead meant that with every local write failing, each reload worked from the last copy that fit and reviews came back as due. The welcome page's restore no longer reloads the page: the downloaded progress reaches state, and the setup gate opens.

**Surfacing background changes to the UI**: `GoogleDriveContext` exposes `lastBackgroundMerge` (set after a background sync that pulled in remote changes) separately from `lastDownload` (set only on a full blocking download). `useQuizOrchestration` reconciles (merges) remote changes into live React state via the `RECONCILE_REMOTE` action rather than replacing state wholesale, so an answer submitted mid-sync is never silently overwritten. A `syncPaused` flag surfaces an expired/invalid token visibly (`SyncStatusIndicator` in `App.tsx` shows a reconnect button) instead of silently stopping uploads.

### Data Safety (`progressSerialization.ts`)

The local pre-v8 backup (`BackupService`, removed) is gone: it protected one migration wave long since finished, and doubled the space progress took (see Storage Service). The Drive backup above remains.

**`progressSerialization.ts`**: single shared (de)serialization module used by both `storage.service.ts` and `sync/googleDriveSync.ts`, so a stored payload always hydrates into the exact same in-memory shape regardless of which channel it came from (fixes a previous divergence where the two channels handled `Date`/`Set` fields slightly differently). Exposes `toPlainProgressJSON`, `hydrateProgress`, and `migrateAndHydrateProgress` (migration + hydration in one call).

### Migration Service (`migration.service.ts`)

Handles data format upgrades to ensure backward compatibility.

**Two-tier version scheme:**
- `SYNC_MIGRATION_VERSION` (7): the ceiling the **synchronous** pass (`migrateUserProgress`) can ever stamp on its own.
- `MERGED_VOCAB_VERSION` (8): the version the **async** homograph-merge pass (`migrateMergedVocabsAsync`) reaches.
- `CURRENT_FORMAT_VERSION` (13): the true terminal version, reachable **only** after the second **async** pass, `migrateGrammarQueueIdsAsync`, has also run. Both async passes need a network fetch, which is why they sit behind `needsMigration()` rather than in the synchronous pass. The vocab remap (`migrateMergedVocabsAsync`) runs below this version too, not only below 8, and never lowers a version: the dataset can move a merged word's base (13: 49 bases moved when the merge tie-break changed, 点 from ちょぼ to てん), and progress under the old base id would otherwise be retired once that file is gone. It is idempotent, so **bump `CURRENT_FORMAT_VERSION` whenever `index/merged-map.json` gains an entry for an id that used to be a base.**

Previously both were the same constant, so the cheap synchronous pass could stamp the terminal version on its own and pre-empt the async pass entirely - `needsMigration()` would report `false` immediately after a single synchronous load, and the homograph-merge migration (which needs a network fetch) would silently never run. `migrateUserProgress` now caps at `SYNC_MIGRATION_VERSION`, so `needsMigration()` correctly keeps reporting `true` until the async pass has actually completed.

**Features:**
- Converts old `mastery` (0-100) system to new `memoryStrength`/`interval` system
- Normalizes `needsRetry` (legacy boolean → per-type `{reading?, meaning?}` object) **unconditionally**, regardless of format version, since the field isn't tied to the version-gated passes
- Recomputes `nextReviewAt` unconditionally via `scheduling.ts` on every load, retroactively correcting any value written before that derivation existed
- `grammarQueue` (issue #17) is a purely additive field, so it needs no version-gated pass at all - `migrateUserProgress` just fills `DEFAULT_GRAMMAR_PROGRESS` defaults into each item and recomputes its `nextReviewAt` via `grammarScheduling.ts`, unconditionally, every load
- `completedChapters` is additive the same way - defaults to `[]` unconditionally, no version gate (see Grammar Activity: Chapter-driven introduction)
- `retiredVocabIds` is additive too (defaults to `[]`, no version gate), and the pass additionally **filters `learningQueue` to drop every id in it on every load** - this is the enforcement point for the "queue holds no retired id" invariant, so a Drive merge that unioned a retired word back in is re-cleaned next load (see Error Handling)
- **`migrateGrammarQueueIdsAsync`** transfers a `GrammarProgress` off any id that is no longer introduced on its own, onto the id that replaced it. Two indexes feed one remap, because they strand a stored entry the same way: `index/aliases.json` (points DROPPED as duplicates ingested twice upstream, which 404 on `loadGrammarPoint` while the scheduler still counts them due, so the session can never complete) and `index/variant-groups.json` (points demoted to REALIZATION VARIANTS of a canonical, e.g. じゃ/それじゃ under それでは, or the six どこにも entries for one rule). A variant is the quieter failure: it still loads, so the learner simply keeps drilling it as a separate card beside its canonical, which is the exact duplication collapsing it was meant to remove. Aliases win a collision, since a dropped id cannot be loaded at all. When both ids carry progress the merge keeps the **stronger** entry (higher `memoryStrength`, ties broken by `totalReviews`), then takes the earliest introduction, soonest due date, union of history, and `graduated` if either was - mirroring `migrateMergedVocabsAsync`, so the two merges cannot disagree. **`CURRENT_FORMAT_VERSION` must be bumped whenever either index gains entries**, or a user already at the terminal version never re-runs the pass and their newly-retired ids stay stranded.
- Idempotent migration (already-migrated data not re-migrated)
- Automatic migration on data load (Storage & Google Drive)

**Conversion Formula:**
- `memoryStrength = (mastery / 100) * maxMemoryStrength`
- mastery 0 → memoryStrength 0 (beginner)
- mastery 100 → memoryStrength `maxMemoryStrength` (mastered)

---

## State Management

### Quiz State (`context/quiz/`)

The quiz state machine is split into four single-responsibility modules rather than one monolithic provider:

- **`quizReducer.ts`** - Pure `QuizState`/`QuizAction`/reducer. No I/O, no side effects, no `Date.now()` calls - fully unit-testable in isolation (`quizReducer.test.ts`).
- **`quizSelectors.ts`** - `selectNextView(state, hasMoreLearnable, now)` is the **single source of truth** for "what should the quiz screen show right now". It replaces three previously-independent decision points (a queue-level `nextDue` memo, a `computeSessionView` function, and an ad-hoc `currentProgress.introductionAt` check in `VocabQuizScreen`) that could drift out of agreement. Returns `{ queueItem, sessionState, nextReviewAt, shouldShowIntro }`. Also exposes `selectCurrentProgress`, `collectActionableTaskKeys` (every quiz task actionable now, as `TaskKey[]`), `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` (keeps at most one task per vocab, reading > meaning > production - see Session quiz cap below), `capSessionCommit` (truncates a snapshot to `CONSTANTS.srs.sessionQuizCap`, split evenly across quiz types with spillover), `selectSessionStats`, and `selectNextSessionPreview`. `selectSessionStats(state, hasMoreLearnable, now)` returns `{ done, total, retriesPending, waiting, moreNew }` computed against the **raw, unfiltered** `state.session.committed` - the same set `selectNextView` serves from and `selectNextSessionPreview` counts, so the three can never disagree: `total` = committed set size (**fixed** for the session), `done` = committed tasks no longer actionable (answered, deferred, or graduated), `retriesPending` = committed tasks currently awaiting a retry (a wrong answer this session, shown highlighted and appended to the bar denominator), `waiting` = distinct vocab with tasks due *now* that were never committed to this session, `moreNew` = `hasMoreLearnable` (the "+" in "n+ waiting"). This replaced a `done + liveDueReviews` formula whose denominator **shrank on every wrong answer** (a wrong answer pushes the due date ~12h out - leaving the live due count - without incrementing `done`, and the pending retry was never re-counted). `selectNextSessionPreview(state, now)` returns `{ review, new, retries, remaining }`, a preview of what the *next* study session will contain - shown on the Main hub's quiz activity card before the user even clicks in. `review`/`retries` count **quiz cards**, derived by running the session's own pipeline (`collectActionableTaskKeys`, then `dedupTaskKeysByVocab`, then `capSessionCommit`), so the figure on the card is literally what the session will commit to and cannot drift from it. `remaining` is the overflow past dedup+`sessionQuizCap` combined, rendered as "N more after this session". `new` stays a count of **words**, since a queued-but-not-yet-due word produces no task key and how many cards it becomes depends on choices the learner has not made yet. Buckets stay mutually exclusive per word: a retry-flagged word counts once, as a retry.

  This replaced a per-distinct-vocab count whose due-check listed reading and meaning by hand. It was never extended to production, so a queue with only production due reported "all caught up" while the session had a full run of cards waiting - and counting words understated a session that asks up to three cards per word, which matters much more now the cap bounds the sitting.
- **`useQuizOrchestration.ts`** - Every effect (vocab/sentence loading and building the card's exercise, daily reset, persistence, migration triggering, Drive sync reconciliation, **session lifecycle** `SESSION_START`/`SESSION_END`) and every action (`advanceQueue`, `saveVocabIntroChoice`, etc.); answering is the shared exercise turn (`useExerciseTurn`, see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine). Returns `{ actions, exercise, nextView, currentProgress, sessionStats, nextSessionPreview }`. Mount-once effects use a `useRef` guard instead of the previous string-hack dependency array (`[state.progress ? 'loaded' : 'loading']`). Reads `useLocation()` (it renders inside `QuizProvider`, itself inside `BrowserRouter` - see `main.tsx`) to gate both the vocab-loading effect and the session-lifecycle effect to the `/quiz` route, so browsing Settings/Stats/the Main hub neither keeps fetching vocab in the background nor keeps a session alive - see the Main Screen entry in Application Pages.
- **`QuizProvider.tsx`** - Thin assembler: `useReducer(quizReducer, ...)` + `useQuizOrchestration(...)`, wires the result into `QuizContext.Provider`. Owns the public `QuizContextValue` interface.
- **Startup load order** (`pageRendered`, `components/PageRenderedSignal.tsx`): the study flow's background loads (`loadUsuallyKanaIds` and `hasMoreLearnableVocabulary` in `useQuizOrchestration`, which read the frequency index; `hasMoreLearnableGrammar`, the hub chapter status and the chapter-completion check in `useGrammarOrchestration`, which read the teaching order, kinds and conjugations) wait until the first page has rendered. `PageRenderedSignal` sits after `<Routes>` inside their `Suspense` boundary in `App.tsx`, so it commits only once the route's lazy chunk has loaded, and its effect runs after the page's own effects: a page's requests (`/listening`'s `media/index.json` and `library.json`) are issued first instead of queuing behind 3 MB of frequency index. The hub gets the same data a moment after its frame. A flag that only ever turns on, so later navigation is unaffected. `check:lighthouse` gates it: on the anime list, `media/index.json` and `library.json` must be issued before `index/frequency.json`, and on every SRS page no dataset file may be requested twice in one load (the request cache).

`useQuiz.ts` (unchanged location) still exposes the `useQuiz()` hook and `QuizContext` object; its type import now points at `./quiz/QuizProvider`.

#### State Shape (`QuizState`)
```typescript
{
  progress: UserProgress | null,
  settings: UserSettings | null,
  currentVocab: Vocabulary | null,
  currentQuizItem: PendingQuizItem | null,
  turns: { vocab: ExerciseTurn | null, grammar: ExerciseTurn | null },   // the exercise on screen in each activity: { exercise, answers, hintLevels, feedback, isEvaluating } (see Exercise engine)
  isLoadingVocab: boolean,
  introCandidates: Vocabulary[],
  nextKanjiToLearn: { step: number; kanjis: string[] } | null,
  sessionHistory: Array<{ vocabId, writtenForm, result, delta, vocabDelta?, vocabBreakdown? }>,
  session: { committed: TaskKey[], suspendedAt?: number } | null,   // active study session's frozen task set; suspendedAt set while a consult page pauses it
  sessionGains: { net, gained, lost, vocab },   // cumulative session point total (issue #80) - NOT derived from sessionHistory, see below
  fatalError: string | null
}
```

**`session` (the committed task set)**: `TaskKey` is `` `${vocabId}:${quizType}` `` (built via the exported `taskKey()` helper). `session.committed` is captured **once** when a study session begins - a snapshot of every quiz task actionable at that moment (`collectActionableTaskKeys`), deduped to at most one task per vocab (`dedupTaskKeysByVocab`, reading > meaning > production) then capped (`capSessionCommit`) - and is extended only by the user's own "Learn" choices (`VOCAB_INTRO_CHOICE` adds the learned word's `reading` task). It never grows from background reviews coming due mid-session.

**`sessionGains` (the cumulative point total, issue #80)**: `sessionHistory`/`grammarSessionHistory` are deliberately capped at 50 entries for the `HistoryTicker` display (see Quiz Screen → Shared session-progress header below), but the session's **total** knowledge-point figure (`GainsSummary`'s "+N pts") used to be computed by `reduce`-ing over that same capped array - so once a session passed 50 answers, the oldest deltas fell out of the array and the total silently stopped growing (plateaued) no matter how many more correct answers followed. `sessionGains: { net, gained, lost, vocab }` (`SessionGains`, `quizReducer.ts`) fixes this by tracking the running totals as scalar accumulators instead: reset to zero on `SESSION_START` (and `grammarSessionGains` on `GRAMMAR_SESSION_START`), incremented on every `EXERCISE_CONTINUE` from the exact same `delta` and `vocabDelta` already pushed into the capped history array - so the ticker and the total can never disagree on a per-answer basis, only on how far back they each remember. `vocab` accumulates the indirect credit an answer gave other words: a grammar sentence's vocabulary, or the near-synonym typed instead of a production card's word. `GainsSummary` (`components/SessionProgress.tsx`) reads `gains` directly rather than reducing over `history` - `history`'s only remaining job there is deciding whether anything has been answered yet (`history.length > 0`), not computing the numbers.

**Session lifecycle is route-gated, with consult pages pausing rather than ending it** (issue #99): a session runs only while the user is on `/quiz`. Opening a **consult** page pauses it instead: a word, kanji, grammar point or grammar family detail page (`/vocab/:vocabId`, `/kanji/:character`, `/grammar/:grammarId`, `/grammar/family/:familyId`), classified by the pure `sessionRouteRole` (`context/quiz/sessionRoutes.ts`, which also keeps `/grammar/browse` and `/grammar/chapters` from matching `/grammar/:grammarId`). The rule depends on the destination only, never on the link followed, so the revealed-answer link, the history ticker, a chip inside a detail page and the header search all pause it the same way, whether or not the current card has been answered. Any other page (the Main hub, Settings, Stats, the Vocabulary list `/vocab`, the Kanji page `/kanji`, ...) ends it exactly like running out of due work does, and so does returning after `CONSTANTS.srs.sessionSuspendTtlMinutes` (30); `SESSION_END` simply clears `session`. The whole table lives in the pure `nextSessionTransition` (`useSessionLifecycle.ts`), shared by both activities. A paused session carries `session.suspendedAt` (epoch ms) and keeps its committed set, history and gains; the current card and any pending answer were never at risk, since they live in the provider and an answer is only applied on Continue. While paused, the correct-answer auto-advance timer does not fire (it is gated on the activity route), and resuming an unanswered card restarts its latency clock so the time away is not scored as a slow answer (latency after an answer is already frozen at submit). `ResumeSessionBar` (`components/ResumeSessionBar.tsx`, rendered by `App.tsx` as its own row beneath the header, never absolute or fixed) shows "Back to your vocabulary/grammar session" with the session's `done / total` on a consult page while a session is paused there. "Learn this word" on a word's page (or a search result's "Learn") joins the paused session (`VOCAB_INTRO_CHOICE` keeps `suspendedAt`). There is no longer an end-of-session recap in state - the `[2026-08-04]` change replaced it with a **daily** activity card on the Main hub, sourced from persisted review history (`buildDailyActivity`) rather than the ephemeral session, so it stays accurate across however many sessions happen in a day; see the Main Screen entry in Application Pages.

A vocab with more than one direction due at once (e.g. reading and meaning both actionable) contributes **only its highest-priority direction** to `session.committed` - `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` keeps reading over meaning over production, and the excluded direction(s) are simply not part of this session; they surface as `waiting` and are picked up by a later session's own commit once the committed direction is no longer due (see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). This replaced a same-session-collision mechanism that lived in `SRSService.applyAnswer` instead: a correct reading answer used to push a due meaning's `dueDate` forward 12h so the two weren't asked back to back. That stagger was asymmetric (production was never staggered) and had a second-order bug once a session could commit both directions for one vocab at all - the stagger silently resolved the committed meaning task's `SessionStats.done` without the user ever answering it, so one answer could advance the progress bar by 2.
