Deduping at commit time removes the need for the stagger entirely (there's no second committed task left for it to resolve) and covers all three quiz types uniformly, so `applyAnswer` no longer touches any entry's `dueDate` other than the one it was asked to grade. `selectSessionStats` counts `done`/`total`/`retriesPending` against the frozen, already-deduped `session.committed` set as-is - it does not dedup or filter it itself - and reports mid-session arrivals (plus a vocab's deduped-out other directions) separately as "waiting". The lifecycle (`SESSION_START` on entering review/learn, `SESSION_END` on reaching waiting/exhausted) is driven by an effect in `useQuizOrchestration`, which computes the snapshot with `now` so the reducer stays free of `Date.now()`.

Note: this is **not** the old `sessionQueue`/`sessionBuiltAt` subsystem (a prior "frozen session queue" refactor, see historical `[2026-02-28]` log entry, that nothing ever read - deleted as dead code). That one froze whole vocab items; `session.committed` is a task-key set, and retry-on-wrong-answer is still handled entirely by the per-type `needsRetry` flag on `VocabProgress`, not by the session. Note also that `session.committed` **is** now authoritative over what card is served next (see Session quiz cap below) - it was read only by the progress counter before that change.

#### Session quiz cap

`session.committed` bounds what a sitting can serve, not just what its progress counter counts. `selectNextView` passes it to `getNextVocabToStudy` as an `allowed` set, and the commit pipeline - `collectActionableTaskKeys` → `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` → `capSessionCommit` (all `quizSelectors.ts`) - deduplicates to one task per vocab and then truncates to `CONSTANTS.srs.sessionQuizCap` (200) tasks at commit time. Both `selectNextSessionPreview` and the `SESSION_START` snapshot in `useQuizOrchestration` run the exact same three-function pipeline, so the Main hub's preview count, the in-session progress bar's denominator, and what actually gets served can never drift from each other.

Four things about it are load-bearing:

- **Dedup runs before the cap, not after.** `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` keeps at most one task per vocab (reading > meaning > production), so `capSessionCommit`'s even split-by-type operates on the already-deduped pool. Capping first and deduping after would let a vocab's lower-priority direction occupy a cap slot that a dedup pass would otherwise have freed up for a different vocab entirely.
- **The cap is composed per quiz type, not taken as a prefix.** `getNextVocabToStudy` clears every actionable reading before any meaning, so a prefix of the snapshot would commit 200 readings and zero meanings for a user with 400 due, session after session, while the meaning backlog only grew. `capSessionCommit` buckets by type and fills smallest-pool-first, so each type gets an even share and a type with less work than its share spills the surplus to the others rather than cutting the session short. The split is over the types actually present, so it is thirds now that the production quiz exists, and was halves before it. In practice the dedup step skews this: a user with every word's reading due commits almost entirely reading tasks in early sessions, and meaning/production arrive once reading intervals grow past daily - which is the intended "a vocab's other directions naturally never share a session" behavior, not a bug in the split.
- **The committed set is read unfiltered everywhere it's consumed - for serving and for counting.** A meaning-dropping filter (`filterSessionCommit`) once ran at commit time, which made a dropped task unservable: production was unservable in any session where the word's reading was also due, which for a daily reviewer was every session. Moving that filter to run only inside `selectSessionStats` fixed serving but left the progress counter's `total`/`waiting` disagreeing with both what actually gets served and with `selectNextSessionPreview` (unfiltered). Both problems traced back to the same root cause - trying to solve "don't ask both directions of a word in one sitting" by filtering/staggering *after* the commit, rather than deciding it once when the commit is built - which `dedupTaskKeysByVocab` now does instead (see `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). The committed set is the single deduped, capped actionable set, filtered nowhere downstream of that.
- **Selection bounded to `committed` needs an exit state, or the screen hangs.** Once the committed set is cleared while capped-out work is still due, `sessionState` computed off the live queue stays `'review'` while selection has nothing left to hand back, so `VocabQuizScreen` would sit on its loading gate forever. `selectNextView` returns `'session-complete'` for exactly that case (a session exists, no queue item, no intro candidates, and at least one actionable task outside the committed set), and `getNextVocabToStudy` returns null rather than falling through to new intros so the user is not started on new vocabulary while reviews they were never offered are due.

`sessionHasWork` in `useQuizOrchestration` deliberately counts `'session-complete'` as work. Ending the session there would immediately re-satisfy `nextSessionTransition`'s start condition (work is still due, the route is still `/quiz`) and silently commit a fresh capped set, which would make the cap invisible: the user would just keep being handed cards. Starting another session is the user's explicit choice, via `actions.startNewSession()` (dispatches `SESSION_END`; the lifecycle effect then re-snapshots and re-caps on the next render) from `SessionCompleteScreen`.

#### Actions
- `SETUP_COMPLETE`: Initialize progress after setup
- `LOAD_VOCAB_START/SUCCESS/ERROR`: Vocabulary loading states
- `EXERCISE_SET_ANSWER` / `EXERCISE_REVEAL_HINT` / `EXERCISE_EVALUATING` / `EXERCISE_SUBMITTED` / `EXERCISE_CONTINUE`: the exercise turn of either activity (each names its `host`), handled by `exerciseReducer.ts` and delegated first. `EXERCISE_CONTINUE` writes the answer's effects through `applyEffects` to the current progress, clears the turn for a retry served straight back, and records the ticker entry and the session gains (see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine)
- `ADVANCE_QUEUE`: Move to next vocab item / fetch intro candidates
- `SAVE_SETTINGS`: Update user settings (clears `introCandidates` if the learning order changed)
- `UPDATE_KANJI_KNOWLEDGE`: Update known kanji. Clears `introCandidates` and `nextKanjiToLearn` when the knowledge actually changed (method, step, or kanji set), since which vocabulary is optimal to learn next depends directly on the known kanji - the same invalidation `SAVE_SETTINGS` performs on a learning-order change. Returns state **unchanged** for an identical payload, because `KanjiKnowledgeEditor` fires its `onChange` on mount as well as on edit and must not discard a valid buffer (or loop).
- `OVERRIDE_DAILY_LIMIT`: Bypass daily new vocab limit (legacy - the limit itself is effectively disabled)
- `VOCAB_INTRO_CHOICE`: Handle Learn/Skip on intro card. On "Learn" with an active session, also adds the word's `reading` task to `session.committed` so a word the user chose to learn counts toward the session total (Skip graduates immediately and adds nothing).
- `SET_NEXT_KANJI` / `LEARN_NEXT_KANJI`: KKLC step-unlock flow
- `SESSION_START` / `SESSION_END`: Set/clear `session` (the frozen task set behind the progress counter). `SESSION_START` takes the pre-computed `taskKeys` snapshot; `SESSION_END` is a no-op (same reference) when no session is active, otherwise clears it.
- `SESSION_SUSPEND` / `SESSION_RESUME`: Pause (stamp `session.suspendedAt` with the passed-in `now`) or resume (clear it) the session, keeping its committed set, history and gains. No-ops when there is nothing to pause or resume. Grammar has `GRAMMAR_SESSION_SUSPEND` / `GRAMMAR_SESSION_RESUME`.
- `RESET`: Clear all progress
- `RESET_DAILY_STATS`: Reset daily counters (midnight)
- `SET_EPISODES_WATCHED`: Writes listening-library marks (`{ entries }`, one episode or a whole series) into `progress.watchedEpisodes`. `updatedAt` is stamped by the `setEpisodesWatched` action, keeping the reducer free of `Date.now()`. No-op without progress.
- `RECONCILE_REMOTE`: Assign an already-merged `{progress, settings}` from a background Drive sync. The merge itself happens in `useQuizOrchestration` (via `sync/mergeProgress.ts`) **before** dispatch, so the reducer just assigns the result - everything else (`currentVocab`, the exercise turns) is left untouched, so a background sync can never interrupt an answer in progress.
- `RETIRE_VOCAB`: A vocab whose data no longer exists (`VocabNotFoundError` on load) is dropped from `learningQueue` and tombstoned in `retiredVocabIds`, and the failed load is cleared (`currentVocab`/`currentQuizItem` reset) so `selectNextView` recomputes and serves the next item. Idempotent: a no-op without progress, or when the id is already retired and absent from the queue. See Error Handling.

#### Computed Values
- `isSetupComplete`: Whether initial setup is done
- `sessionState`: Current session state (review/learn/learn-kanji/waiting/exhausted) - derived by `selectNextView`
- `shouldShowIntro`: Whether the currently-loaded vocab should show the intro card - also derived by `selectNextView`
- `currentProgress`: VocabProgress for current vocab
- `nextReviewAt`: Next review timestamp

#### Key Functions
- `setupComplete({ kanjiKnowledge, settings })`: Complete initial setup

- `advanceQueue({ now, overrideDailyLimit })`: Load next vocab
- Answering (`setAnswer`, `revealHint`, `submit`, `continueToNext`, `canSubmit`, `showContinue`) is the `ExerciseTurnApi` each activity exposes as `exercises.vocab` / `exercises.grammar` (see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine). An answer's SRS writes happen once, in `applyEffects`, on Continue.
- `saveVocabIntroChoice(vocab, 'learn'|'skip')`: Handle intro card choice
- `startNewSession()`: End a finished session so the lifecycle effect commits a fresh capped one (the "Start another session" button on `SessionCompleteScreen`)
- `setEpisodesWatched(mediaId, episodes: { number, coverage? }[], watched)`: Mark or un-mark listening-library episodes of one title in a single update (one episode, or the whole series via "Mark all watched"); each `coverage` (0..1) is recorded as `coverageAtWatch` only when marking watched

### Grammar Activity State (`context/quiz/grammarReducer.ts`, `grammarSelectors.ts`, `useGrammarOrchestration.ts`)

The Grammar activity (issue #17) is a **parallel concern within the same `QuizState`/`QuizProvider`**, not a second context/provider. `grammarQueue` lives on the same `UserProgress` object as `learningQueue`, and `useQuizOrchestration`'s persistence/Drive-sync effects key off `state.progress` reference changes generically - so a second provider would need its own read/write path onto that same object, racing the first. Instead:

- **`grammarReducer.ts`** - Defines the grammar-only slice of `QuizState` (`GrammarQuizState`, intersected into `QuizState`) and every `GRAMMAR_`-prefixed action, plus the pure `grammarReducer(state, action)` function. `quizReducer.ts`'s top-level `quizReducer` delegates to it via `isGrammarAction()` (a simple `action.type.startsWith('GRAMMAR_')` check) before its own `switch` - so vocab's reducer code needs no awareness of grammar's action shapes.
- **`grammarSelectors.ts`** - `selectNextGrammarView(state, hasMoreLearnableGrammar, now)` mirrors `selectNextView` (same `{ queueItem, sessionState, nextReviewAt, shouldShowIntro }` shape, `sessionState` typed `GrammarSessionState = Exclude<SessionState, 'learn-kanji'>` since grammar has no kanji-gated step). `computeBlankPlan(point, progress, reviewCount)` (**async**) is grammar-specific: walks the point's examples starting from a deterministic pick (hashed on `${grammarId}:${reviewCount}`, so repeated reviews of the same point cycle through its examples instead of re-rolling every render, wrapping around all examples) in four preference passes. **The grammar construction is the primary thing tested, vocabulary is secondary** - there is one `SRSEntry` per grammar point, so what gets graded has to consistently reflect grammar-point recall, or the schedule that entry drives doesn't mean what it claims to:
  1. **PRIMARY** - the first example whose grammar-pattern markers were located at dataset build time (`GrammarExample.patternWordIndices`, non-empty - see `gokan-dataset`'s `docs/SCHEMA.md` and its pattern-location issue). Blanks those markers unconditionally, regardless of vocab knowledge, then layers in - as **secondary** reinforcement - any other content word in that same example the user already knows (`learningQueue` entry with `introductionAt !== null`), sorted by position. Unknown vocab stays pre-filled as literal context either way.
  2. **FALLBACK** - the pattern isn't locatable in any of the point's examples (rare: ~1.9% of points as of the dataset's last build, all conjugation-transformation-style points with no literal marker in common across their own examples, e.g. potential-form verb tables). The first example containing at least one known word - blanks every known word in it. This is the *original* vocab-only behavior, now demoted to a fallback for the residual the primary path can't cover.
  3. **FALLBACK** - no example has a known word either, on one of these rare pattern-less points: the first example with ANY blankable word (resolved to a vocab id) at all, blanking exactly the single most frequent one (`VocabularyService.loadVocab(...).frequency.kanjiRank`, lowest wins) rather than every content word - blanking everything produced unanswerable cards for a learner with no vocab overlap yet.
  4. If literally no example in the whole point has a single blankable word, returns a read-only plan (`blankWordIndices: []`, `readOnly: true`) - it becomes a `'study'` exercise (`StudyCard`), pure study material with no Submit step, since grading zero blanks previously auto-passed and silently granted SRS credit.

  For the blanks it selects, `computeBlankPlan` builds one `AnswerSlot` per span (`buildBlankSlots`, through the exercise engine's `wordSlot`; vocab fetched with `VocabularyService.loadVocab` once per blank, at load time, so grading stays synchronous; a failed fetch degrades to surface and reading). Pattern-marker slots are `core` and decide the grammar point's result (the worst of them, **`wrong` > `pass` > `minor_error` > `correct`**), so a missed *vocab* reinforcement blank can never turn a demonstrated grammar core into `wrong` (issue #33 follow-up). Vocab slots are `support` and scale the reward through `gradeExercise`'s `strengthModifier` (`SUPPORT_COEFFICIENT_FLOOR` 0.5 up to 1, by the fraction right, applied only when the pattern was a success), since there's one SRSEntry per grammar point and its schedule must reflect grammar-point recall rather than vocabulary that happened to sit in the sentence. On the fallback examples with no located pattern, every slot is `core` (worst-of-all at full strength, the original behaviour). A revealed blank grades `minor_error` (RC3 item 3 - previously `pass`), an empty one `pass`; `'pass'` stays reachable by typing it too. Grading itself is `gradeExercise`, as on every card (see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine). Also exposes `selectCurrentGrammarProgress` and `selectNextGrammarSessionPreview` (mirrors `selectNextSessionPreview`'s `{ review, new, retries }` shape, same mutually-exclusive-bucket logic, for the Main hub's grammar activity card).

**Conjugated blanks (issue #95)**: for an inflected word (`GrammarExampleWord.baseForm` set), the slot's ideal forms are the surface and its kana **as conjugated** (`kanaOfSurface`, はやかろ for 早かろ), so the right answer typed in hiragana grades like the kanji one, and the word's dictionary forms are a near miss. `GrammarExampleWord.reading` is an ideal form only when it is not one of the lemma's dictionary readings: the compiled data stores the surface's reading for some tokens and the lemma's for others (早かろ carries はやい), and treating a lemma reading as ideal made the dictionary form grade `correct` while the right kana answer graded `wrong`. Every blank with a loaded vocab carries its `InflectableWord` (`slot.word.lemma`) with `otherForm: 'minor_error'`: another form of the right word (食べた where the sentence wants 食べて) is a near miss, never a failure, on a pattern marker too. **A whole marker in another conjugation is a near miss too** (`AnswerSlot.inflections`, built by `markerInflections` in `services/exercise/slots.ts`): for each conjugated word in a pattern span, and a verb ending it in its dictionary form (ことがある), the slot keeps the text before the word, the word, and the text after its inflection, and an answer that keeps that text with the word in another of its forms grades as one. **Only politeness changed** (がある for があります, なければなりません for なければならない, ことにしています for ことにしている): `correct`, the sense being the same (`sameFormUpToRegister` pairs each tense and polarity's plain and polite forms), unless the card asks for a register: a point whose formality hint is casual, polite, formal or literary keeps it a near miss, and a register a sibling point covers is already in the near tier, which is checked first. **Anything else** (があった, がありません, ことにした): `minor_error`. The particle is part of the construction, so ある alone for があります stays `wrong`. Most marker words link to no vocab entry (4,446 of the 5,539 conjugated words in pattern spans, mined sentences included: する, だ, なる, ない, いる, ある...), so their conjugation is read off the token (`inferredWord`, see Inflection generator); a word with an entry uses its POS tags. Measured over the dataset, 94% of the pattern spans holding a verb or a conjugated word get the rule; the rest are classical auxiliaries (べし, ぬ, まじ, ごとし) and ます or た standing without their verb. **The conjugation drill's slot has no word**: there the form is what is being tested, so a different form of the right word stays `wrong` (a product decision, recorded in `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md`). Word blanks also carry the word's near-synonyms (`synonymsOf`), graded against the example's English exactly as on a production card; pattern markers carry none. `applyVariantRotation`/`applyFamilyInterchange` widen only the pattern slots' `accept`/`near`.

**The conjugation drill prints a cue label, not the dataset's `formLabel`**: `computeConjugationPlan` passes it through `cueFormLabel` (`grammarSelectors.ts`), which removes the kana parts of its parentheticals, because those spell out the ending being asked for ("past negative (じゃなかった)" is shown as "past negative"). English parts stay, since they name the form without giving it away ("prohibitive (な / do not)" becomes "prohibitive (do not)"). A test runs it over every label in `conjugations.json`, so a new kana parenthetical in the dataset cannot leak.

**Productivity selection, curated examples competing with the mined pool (issue #73's app half, extended by issue #85)**: for a REVIEW turn only (`reviewCount >= 1` - the intro card and the very first review, `reviewCount === 0`, always stay on curated `point.examples`), `computeBlankPlan` first tries `selectProductivePlan` (`grammarSelectors.ts`, renamed from `selectMinedPlan`), which ranks the point's own curated examples that already have a located pattern together with its corpus-mined pool (`GrammarService.loadMinedExamples(point.id)`, built in `gokan-dataset` - see Build & Development -> Grammar Dataset's "Corpus-mined sentences" note). This is a fifth selection pass layered in front of the four described above, not a replacement for them - it only ever applies to a review, and only when it finds something worth serving:
- Both sources are ranked by the shared ranker (see Services & Business Logic -> Shared sentence ranking): unknown weight first (every unresolved or not-yet-introduced word, weighted by how readable it still is), then length band, then most **target** words (known, **production** ring below the first-loop ceiling, not full mastery), then the highest summed production ring. Pattern-marker words are excluded from the vocab scoring on both sources (blanked regardless). The pick is seeded on the point id alone, so the same sentence keeps coming back across reviews until its words mature - a short, clean curated example is no longer automatically outranked by any mined sentence that happens to qualify.
- A curated example with no located pattern (`patternWordIndices` empty) is not a candidate here at all - it cannot be blanked on the pattern, and `computeBlankPlanFor`'s own fallback passes already know how to handle that case.
- Only sentences (from either source) with at least one target are eligible (a sentence drilling nothing but mature vocab has nothing to offer over another).
- The chosen sentence's blanks are its `patternWordIndices` plus every target index, sorted by position - the same PRIMARY-pass reinforcement mechanism above, just sourced from productivity targets instead of "every known word". Slots are built identically via `buildBlankSlots`.
- Returns `null` (computeBlankPlan then falls through to the curated `computeBlankPlanFor` passes unchanged) when neither source has a single sentence with a target - covering both "no mined pool at all" and "nothing anywhere scores a target".
- `grammarExercise` reads `plan.example` rather than `point.examples[plan.exampleIndex]` (see `GrammarBlankPlan.example`'s doc comment): a mined sentence, like a rotated variant realization, does not live in `point.examples`, so indexing there would show and credit the wrong sentence's words entirely.
- **Latency normalization**: a mined sentence can carry many more blanks than a curated one, and typing N answers legitimately takes ~N times as long as typing one. `GrammarSRSService.applyAnswer`'s new `blankCount` parameter (default 1) divides the measured `latencyMs` by the blank count before it reaches `SRSService.calculateNextState`, so the exercise is judged on its per-blank pace rather than reading as "slow" purely for having more to type. The `review` effect carries the exercise's slot count as `blankCount` (1 for the conjugation drill).
- **`useGrammarOrchestration.ts`** - Grammar's equivalent of `useQuizOrchestration`: loading (route-gated to `/grammar`, same guard-by-comparing-`currentGrammarQuizItem` pattern as vocab's vocab-loading effect, `await`ing `computeBlankPlan`'s vocab fetches and dispatching `GRAMMAR_LOAD_SUCCESS` with the built exercise), the session lifecycle, and the actions (`advanceGrammarQueue`, `saveGrammarIntroChoice`, `dismissGrammarChapterLesson`). Answering is the shared exercise turn (`useExerciseTurn`, see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine), so grading, the auto-advance and the SRS writes are the vocab cards' own: the point's `review` effect is scaled by the support coefficient, and the word blanks answered right without a revealed hint earn **positive-only** indirect credit on their own production entry (skipped while the point is in retry, so a training redo can't over-credit the same words; a wrong vocab blank earns nothing, so grammar practice can never *penalise* vocab). The study card's Continue writes a `defer` effect (`GrammarSRSService.deferWithoutCredit`), which reschedules `dueDate` by a fixed 24h cooldown **without touching `memoryStrength`/`interval`/`difficulty`** - genuinely no SRS credit, just enough to keep the ungradable card from reappearing on the very next pick. Deliberately does **not** duplicate persistence/Drive-sync wiring - dispatching through the shared `quizReducer` gets that for free.
- **Per-blank hint control**: the turn's `hintLevels` (one per slot, reset whenever a new exercise loads) - 0 = none, 1 = gloss shown, 2 = answer revealed (`EXERCISE_REVEAL_HINT`, capped at 2). Reaching level 2 writes the slot's `reveal` into the answer through the reducer, rather than the card substituting it at render time; the earlier render-only form meant the input showed the answer while state stayed empty, which is what blocked submission when the last remaining blank was revealed. A revealed blank always grades `'minor_error'`, matching `CONSTANTS.srs.formula.resultFactors.minor_error` (+0.10) - giving up on a word you don't know still leaves an impression from reading the answer, so it's graded the same as a near-miss rather than as harshly as `'wrong'` (-0.40) or as a genuine skip (`'pass'`, -0.15).
- **Session-progress bar parity** (issue #32 follow-up): grammar now has the same frozen-committed-set progress counter vocab has, brought in once the v1 scope cut ("a smaller, reasonable scope decision") started to feel like a real gap. `grammarReducer.ts` gained a `GrammarSessionTracking { committed: string[] }` slice (`grammarSession`, mirroring vocab's `SessionTracking` but simpler - a `GrammarProgress` has one task per point, so `committed` is just grammar ids, no `TaskKey`-style `id:quizType` composite needed) plus `GRAMMAR_SESSION_START`/`GRAMMAR_SESSION_END` actions and a `grammarSessionHistory` array (`{ grammarId, title, result, delta }`, mirroring vocab's `sessionHistory`). `grammarSelectors.ts` gained `collectActionableGrammarIds(queue, now)` (grammar's `collectActionableTaskKeys`, using `isGrammarDue`/`needsRetry`) and `selectGrammarSessionStats(state, hasMoreLearnableGrammar, now)` (grammar's `selectSessionStats`, same `{ done, total, retriesPending, waiting, moreNew }` shape - no staggering to account for, since there's no reading/meaning split). `useGrammarOrchestration.ts` runs the matching session-lifecycle effect (route-gated to `/grammar`, active on `review`/`learn`) and each answer's ticker entry is `applyEffects`' record, the same as vocab's. `GRAMMAR_INTRO_CHOICE`'s "learn" path adds the point to `grammarSession.committed`, mirroring `VOCAB_INTRO_CHOICE`.

`QuizContextValue` exposes the grammar activity as its own group of fields/actions (`grammarSessionState`, `grammarNextReviewAt`, `currentGrammarProgress`, `shouldShowGrammarIntro`, `nextGrammarSessionPreview`, `grammarSessionStats`, `grammarActions`) alongside the vocab ones, and `exercises: { vocab, grammar }`, each activity's `ExerciseTurnApi`, - `useQuiz()` is the single hook both activities read from.

---

## Application Pages

### Main Screen (`pages/main/MainScreen.tsx`)

The activity hub - the landing page (route `/`) after setup, replacing the previous behavior of dropping users straight into the quiz. Activities (the main actions a user can take) are presented as cards: "Vocabulary quiz session" (`/quiz`), "Grammar quiz session" (`/grammar`, issue #17) and "Listening" (`/listening`, the listening library: no settings cog, so `ActivityCard`'s `settings` prop is optional; it spans both grid columns, shows the covers of the learner's **four best-fitting anime** through `ActivityCard`'s `aside` slot (the top of `rankLibrary` under their `listeningGenres` filter, from the same cached `index.json` + `library.json` the library page uses, loaded after the hub renders; no covers if either fails), and its description counts episodes watched so far). `ActivityCard`'s `footer` (the grammar card's "View all chapters") is pinned inside the card's border at the bottom right, not below the card: below it, that grid cell was taller than its neighbour's card, so the two cards' borders did not line up. Settings, Stats, and Kanji are **not** activities - they stay in the global header toolbar (`App.tsx`, rendered outside `<Routes>` so it's present on every page), unchanged by this page's introduction.

Both activity cards render their preview description via a shared `renderSessionPreviewDescription(preview, nextReviewAt)` helper (`MainScreen.tsx`): `"{review} review · {new} new"`, with `· {retries} retries` appended (in the error color) only when `retries > 0`. When all three counts are 0, it falls back to a caught-up message, showing the next review's ETA when one is known. When `preview.remaining > 0` a second line reads `"{remaining} more after this session"`: only the vocab card can overflow (its session is capped, grammar's is not), and saying so up front is the difference between "this session is the work" and "this session is a slice of it", which changes whether the user stops after it. The vocab card sources its preview from `selectNextSessionPreview(state, now)`; the grammar card from `selectNextGrammarSessionPreview(state, now)` - see State Management for both selectors, and Modification Log `[2026-08-04]`/`[2026-08-05]`. The grammar card also shows a chapter status line beneath the counts, sourced from `grammarHubChapter` (`useGrammarOrchestration`, derived via `GrammarSRSService.getCurrentChapter` + `describeHubChapter` - see Services & Business Logic → Grammar Activity: Chapter progress display), rendered **unconditionally** (issue #87) rather than gated on `preview.new > 0`: `"Current chapter: Chapter {n}: {title}"` once the chapter has an introduced point, `"Next chapter: Chapter {n}: {title}"` before it does, or `"All {n} chapters introduced"` once nothing is left - each paired with a compact `ChapterProgressBar` (issue #58) showing the relevant chapter's (or, once complete, the whole curriculum's) three-way point tally. The line and bar are omitted only when the teaching order itself failed to load (`grammarHubChapter` null); until it has loaded (undefined) placeholders hold their place, and the Listening card holds its covers' places (`MediaCoverPlaceholder`) while the library loads, so nothing below moves when the data arrives (#134). The grammar card also carries a "View all chapters" link (a sibling of the card's own button, same reasoning as the settings cog below) to `/grammar/chapters`, the chapter browser.

Each activity card carries a **settings cog** in its top right corner, opening that activity's `QuizSettingsMenu` (see Per-activity quiz settings below). This is the only entry point to activity-scoped settings.

`DailyActivityCard` (`pages/main/DailyActivityCard.tsx`) replaced the old ephemeral end-of-session recap (`[2026-08-02]`-era `lastSessionRecap`, removed `[2026-08-04]`): a **today** rollup (reviewed / correct / incorrect) plus a compact 7-day bar chart, both derived from `buildDailyActivity(progress, 7)` (`utils/activity.utils.ts`) - the same per-day bucketing `DailyProgressionChart` uses on the Stats screen. Reading persisted `reading.history`/`meaning.history` logs instead of session-local state means the card stays accurate across however many small sessions happen in a day, rather than being overwritten by the next session like the old recap was.

### Page frame and navigation (`components/Page.tsx`, `components/PageHeader.tsx`, `components/nav/`)

Every routed page except the two sessions and onboarding renders inside `Page` (`width: 'narrow' | 'wide'`, `max-w-3xl` or `max-w-5xl`, with the top padding and a bottom padding that clears the mobile tab bar plus the safe-area inset). Wide: the Vocabulary list, vocab, kanji and grammar detail, the Grammar home, Stats, Kanji. Narrow: the Study hub, Settings, Listening, a grammar family, About.

`PageHeader` is left-aligned: an optional `← Back` row, the title (`type-page-title`), an optional subtitle and an optional `actions` slot. Back calls `navigate(-1)` when the previous history entry is inside the app (`location.key !== 'default'`) and otherwise goes to the page's `parentPath`. Nothing links to `/grammar` as a Back: that route starts a session.

**Routes and destinations** (`utils/navigation.utils.ts`, tested): Study `/`, Vocabulary `/vocab`, Grammar `/grammar/chapters` (its two tabs are `/grammar/chapters` and `/grammar/browse`), Listening `/listening`, Stats `/stats`, Kanji `/kanji` (the old `/profile` redirects there), Settings `/settings`. A destination is active on its own pages too (`/vocab/:id` is inside Vocabulary, any `/grammar/...` page but the session inside Grammar).

**`AppHeader`** (`components/nav/AppHeader.tsx`) renders outside `<Routes>`. Its inner container has the wide page width, so the logo and the links line up with page content. From `md`: logo, the text links, then sync status, search and Settings on the right; between `md` and `lg` search is an icon that opens the field over the header row. Below `md`: logo and a search icon, with the destinations in **`BottomTabBar`** (Study, Vocabulary, Grammar, Stats, More; More opens a sheet with Listening, Kanji, Settings, About and the Dictionary). **Session focus mode** on `/quiz` and `/grammar` (`isSessionPath`): no links, no tab bar, no footer; the header shows the logo, a search icon and "End session", which goes to the hub (a `leave` route, so it ends the session exactly as leaving always did). `AppFooter` (About, Dictionary) shows on every non-session page from `md`.

**Search** (`components/SearchBar.tsx`) is a combobox: focus stays in the field, arrows move a highlighted option (`aria-activedescendant`), Enter opens it, Escape closes. Each result carries `WordStudyStatus`: Learning or Mastered for a word in the queue, otherwise a "Learn" button that adds the word in place through `saveVocabIntroChoice` and turns into "Added". Two things about the phone results panel are load-bearing: it is `fixed` with a **measured** `top` (the field's `getBoundingClientRect().bottom`), with the page scroll-locked while it is open so the measurement stays valid; and its height comes from `window.visualViewport?.height` when available, so it ends above the on-screen keyboard. The input is `text-base` on phones (under 16px, iOS Safari zooms in on focus).

**Search ranking** (`rankVocabSearch`, `utils/vocabSearch.utils.ts`, over `index/search.json`), best first: the word shown as the query (its headword, in kana for a word learned in kana); an exact spelling or reading (the whole romaji converted counts: "taberu" is たべる); a written form or reading starting with, then containing, the query as typed; the query as a whole English word of the gloss (`containsWholeWord`, `@gokan/dataset-schema`, the same test as the dictionary's); a reading starting with the romaji (`romajiReadingPrefixes`, below), then containing its kana; last a gloss merely containing the query. Within a rank, shorter forms first, then index order. The romaji ranks follow the whole-word gloss, as in the dictionary: a Latin query is more often an English word ("house", read ほうせ, the start of 宝石) than the start of a reading. The shorter-first tiebreak stays over frequency: `search.json`'s order ranks a word learned in kana by its kanji spelling (する is 18,213th), so ordering a rank by it would bury those words, while length keeps する near the top of the `su` prefixes (at the cost of 多弁 before 食べ物 for `tabe`). The vocabulary list's search (`/vocab`, `matchesVocabQuery`) keeps a word on the same matches, unranked, in the list's own order.

**Reading a romaji query** (`readRomajiQuery`, `@gokan/dataset-schema`, used by every search: the header search, the vocabulary list and the dictionary): `normalizeRomajiQuery` reads it as an IME would, spaces dropped (`tabe ru` is たべる), `-` as ー (`ko-hi-` is コーヒー; readings are compared in hiragana), `'` kept only after n as the ん separator (`kan'i` is かんい, not かに). Then `romajiToHiragana` gives the whole kana and `romajiReadingPrefixes` the prefixes below. In `romajiToHiragana`, `nn` at the end or before a consonant other than y is one ん (`kann` かん, `kannji` かんじ); before a vowel or y it is ん and an n syllable (`konnichiha` こんにちは, `konnyaku` こんにゃく).

**Romaji typed partway** (`romajiReadingPrefixes`, `@gokan/dataset-schema`, used by both apps' "the reading starts with" only): `romajiToHiragana` leaves an unfinished last syllable in Latin letters (たべr), which no reading contains, so each letter typed would empty the list until the syllable completed. The helper returns the kana a reading can start with instead: complete syllables as converted, an unfinished one as every syllable it begins (`r` gives ら り る れ ろ りゃ りゅ りょ, `ky` gives きゃ きゅ きょ), a single consonant also as っ (`chot` on the way to `chotto`; not after a っ or a ん, and not for y or w, which never double: `oy` on the way to `oya` does not list おっと), a final `n` as ん or the start of an n syllable (`kan` on the way to `kana`), a final `nn` as one ん, and an unfinished `nny` as ん plus an unfinished n syllable (`konny` gives こんにゃ こんにゅ こんにょ). An exact match still needs the whole romaji converted.

`ListRow` stretches its link over the row (`after:absolute after:inset-0`) instead of wrapping the row in it, so a trailing button (a "Learn") is never nested inside an `<a>`.

### Quiz Screen (`pages/quiz/VocabQuizScreen.tsx`)

Route `/quiz`. The study session itself - **explicit and boundable**, per the Main Screen's activity model: starting it snapshots the vocab available at that moment (`session.committed`, see State Management), and it ends the moment the user navigates to any page other than a consult page (Main hub, Settings, Stats, ...) or naturally runs out of due work. Leaving early and running out both end the session identically - there's no separate "abandoned session" state. Resuming later (navigating back to `/quiz`) starts a brand new session against whatever is available then, never reopening the previous one. The exception is a consult page (a word, kanji or grammar point's detail page), which pauses the session for up to 30 minutes; see "Session lifecycle is route-gated" under State Management.

Main study interface. Switches **exhaustively** on `sessionState` (a TypeScript `never` check at the `default` case fails to compile if a new `SessionState` value is ever added without being handled):

- **`'waiting'`**: Show `WaitingScreen` (next review time). Includes a "Back to activities" link to `/`, since `/quiz` is now a sub-page reached from the Main hub rather than the landing page itself.
- **`'exhausted'`**: Show `ExhaustedScreen` (no more content). Same "Back to activities" link as `WaitingScreen`.
- **`'session-complete'`**: Show `SessionCompleteScreen` (cards cleared, words still waiting, plus a "Start another session" button wired to `actions.startNewSession`). Reached only when the per-session quiz cap held work back; see Session quiz cap under State Management.
- **`'learn-kanji'`**: Show `LearnKanjiCard` (KKLC step unlock)
- **`'review'` / `'learn'`**: Loading gate, then `shouldShowIntro` (from `selectNextView`) decides `VocabIntroCard` vs. `ExerciseCard` for the exercise on screen (`exercises.vocab`: reading, meaning, the production gloss or the production cloze, decided when the exercise was built - see Exercise engine and Production cloze card under Services & Business Logic)

**Auto-advance logic**: Owned by `useQuizOrchestration`. If the queue has no valid items but can introduce new vocab, automatically calls `advanceQueue()`.

**Shared formatting** (`pages/quiz/quizFormatting.ts`): `formatReadingList`, `getUniquePosTags`, `getUniqueRelatedCompounds`, and the `useExpandableDefinitions` hook are shared across the vocab prompts (`pages/quiz/VocabPrompts.tsx`) and `VocabIntroCard` rather than being reimplemented in each.

**`InteractiveSentence`** (`components/InteractiveSentence.tsx`, issue #89) renders through a pure `segmentInteractiveSentence` helper (`interactiveSentence.ts` in `@gokan/dataset-schema`, shared with the dictionary) rather than segmenting inline: it splits a `Sentence.original` into ordered text/vocab-match segments and flags which ones are the studied `targetVocabId` or fall inside an optional `highlightRanges` prop (character offsets - e.g. a grammar pattern's markers, see `patternHighlightRanges` below), so the component only renders what the helper decided. The target word now always renders with the accent/bold/underline target styling, whether or not it is clickable (`allowTargetClickable`) or glossed (`showGlossOnHover`) - previously the target segment fell through an early return meant for plain non-interactive text and never got the target class at all, so the meaning-in-context quiz (`MeaningPrompt`, `targetVocabId={vocab.id}`) and the vocab detail page (its sentences, `targetVocabId={vocabId}`) both silently rendered their target word as plain text. A `highlightRanges` entry that overlaps a vocab-match segment flags the **whole match** rather than splitting it, so the match's click/gloss behaviour stays untouched; an entry over plain (unmatched) text is split out into its own highlighted sub-segment instead. Either way, concatenating every returned segment's content always reproduces `sentence.original` exactly - the sentence clozes (`SentenceClozeCard`) are unaffected, since their target words are the blanks themselves, already cut out of the rendered text by `splitAtBlanks`.

**Shared focus management** (`hooks/useQuizFocusManagement.ts`, issue #32 follow-up RC4 item 2): every card shell (`SingleAnswerCard`, `SentenceClozeCard`, `StudyCard`) needs the same two behaviors - focus the first input on a fresh question (or when feedback clears for a retry), and focus the Continue button once feedback is showing, *unless* the answer is about to auto-advance on its own (`grade.autoAdvance`), in which case focus is left alone rather than yanked onto a button about to disappear. The grammar card once had none of this: after submitting, focus was left on the now-disabled blank inputs, so Enter did nothing and a mouse became mandatory to continue. `useQuizFocusManagement({ feedbackShown, skipContinueFocus, continueFocusDelay }, deps)` returns `{ firstInputRef, continueRef }`; the shells key it on `[turn.exercise, feedback]`, and `StudyCard` focuses Continue straight away. `SingleAnswerCard` still owns its own separate effect for the incorrect-answer reveal-delay, which is a display concern, not a focus one.

**Shared session-progress header** (`components/SessionProgress.tsx`, issue #32 follow-up): the `done / total` counter plus its embedded `HistoryTicker` (most-recent-answers strip, correctness color + score delta) is fully presentational, parameterized over a `stats: SessionProgressStats` object and a `history: SessionHistoryEntry[]` array (`{ key, href, label, result, delta }`) rather than reading vocab-specific context directly - the same "mutualize, don't duplicate" treatment `SRSHistoryGraph`/`useQuizFocusManagement` got. `VocabQuizScreen` builds its props from `sessionStats`/`state.sessionHistory`/`state.sessionGains` (links to `/vocab/:id`); `GrammarScreen` builds its own from `grammarSessionStats`/`state.grammarSessionHistory`/`state.grammarSessionGains` (links to `/grammar/:grammarId`, `waitingNoun="grammar points"` vs. vocab's `"vocab"`). Session-progress bar parity for grammar (previously a deliberate v1 scope cut) is covered in State Management → Grammar Activity State. The `HistoryTicker`'s per-item label now renders in `font-mincho` (was `font-serif`, which has no CJK fallback - the same class of bug the `[2026-08-05]` `GrammarIntroCard` title fix addressed), since both vocab written forms and grammar titles are Japanese text. It also renders a `GainsSummary`: **one net knowledge-point figure**, so an answer reading `+6` moves the total by `+6`. It carries a second figure (`+N vocab`) for the points that answer credited to other words (a grammar sentence's vocabulary, or the near-synonym typed instead of a production card's word), which was previously invisible. A row names the item that was asked, never the word typed: a vocab row for a synonym answer reads 狭い, with the credit to 小さい in its breakdown. Each ticker row repeats its own `+N vocab` mark and reveals the per-word split on hover (`私 +2 · 思う +3`), so the aggregate can be traced to the words that earned it. The tooltip is positioned `fixed` off a measured rect rather than absolutely inside the row: the ticker is `overflow-hidden` (it has to be, to clip the strip as entries age out) and an absolutely-positioned child would be clipped with it, the same measured-fixed approach the header search panel uses. `applyEffects` builds the split from the deltas it actually wrote, and labels each word by its headword, since "思う +3" is a word the learner can look up where "思っ +3" is a fragment. The gained/lost breakdown moved to the tooltip. This replaced a `+X / -Y pts` pair that could not be reconciled with the rows beneath it: the ticker printed mastery percentages while the total summed points at half their value, so three numbers described one quantity in two units. See Constants -> knowledge points for the unit itself. **`GainsSummary` reads the `gains: SessionGains` prop (`state.sessionGains`/`state.grammarSessionGains`), not `history`** (issue #80): it used to `reduce` over `history` for the net/gained/lost/vocab figures, which plateaued once a session passed the 50-entry `sessionHistory`/`grammarSessionHistory` cap - see `sessionGains` under State Shape above for the full fix. `history` still decides only whether anything has been answered yet. To keep this session-scoped, `SESSION_START`/`GRAMMAR_SESSION_START` now also **reset** `sessionHistory`/`sessionGains` (and their grammar equivalents), so both the ticker and the gains figure reflect only the current session rather than accumulating across sessions. This is the lightweight "recap" that replaced the idea of a separate end-of-session recap screen (the earlier `[2026-08-04]` removal of `lastSessionRecap` stands): it counts for both activities via the shared component, with no new screen.

### Grammar Screen (`pages/grammar/GrammarScreen.tsx`)

Route `/grammar` (issue #17). The Grammar activity - a second SRS-driven study session alongside `/quiz`, following the same "explicit and boundable" activity model but with its own, simpler state machine (see State Management → Grammar Activity State). Switches exhaustively on `grammarSessionState` (`GrammarSessionState`, a `never`-checked default case same as `VocabQuizScreen`):

- **`'waiting'` / `'exhausted'`**: Inline `CenteredCard` messages (not `WaitingScreen`/`ExhaustedScreen` - those are vocab-copy-specific, e.g. "Learn more words"), each with a "Back to activities" link.
- **`'review'` / `'learn'`**: `pendingGrammarChapterLesson` (set once a chapter completes and has anchored contrast lessons - see Services & Business Logic → Grammar Activity: Chapter-driven introduction) takes priority over everything else here, rendering `GrammarChapterLessonCard` instead: a deliberate pause on a now-finished chapter, not a card the queue serves. Otherwise: loading gate, then `shouldShowGrammarIntro` (from `selectNextGrammarView`) decides `GrammarIntroCard` vs. a `SessionProgress` header (`stats={grammarSessionStats}`, `history={...grammarSessionHistory mapped to /grammar/:grammarId links}`, `waitingNoun="grammar points"`) plus `ExerciseCard` (`exercises.grammar`), positioned the same way `VocabQuizScreen` positions its own `SessionProgress` above its card (issue #32 follow-up).

**`GrammarIntroCard`** (`pages/grammar/GrammarIntroCard.tsx`) - mirrors `VocabIntroCard`'s layout: a top-right `MasteryRing` (`currentGrammarProgress?.entry.memoryStrength ?? 0` - reads `0` here since an intro candidate has no `GrammarProgress` entry yet, same as a fresh vocab intro card would; issue #32 follow-up RC3 item 1), JLPT chip, title (rendered in `font-mincho`, **not** `font-serif` - the title contains Japanese characters and `font-serif`'s font stack, "Source Serif 4, Georgia, serif", has no CJK fallback at all, unlike `font-mincho`/`font-gothic` which both list a Noto JP fallback; caught via a scripted browser pass, see Modification Log `[2026-08-05]`), `point.romaji` when present (a small muted line beneath the title - issue #47, one of only two places romaji ever renders, the other being `GrammarDetailScreen`), short explanation, and the `formation` template in its own bordered box - appropriate here since this is the "here's what you're about to learn" screen, unlike the quiz card below where it would be the answer.

**`GrammarChapterLessonCard`** (`pages/grammar/GrammarChapterLessonCard.tsx`) - the end-of-chapter review step: a heading naming the completed chapter, one `GrammarContrastCard` per focus point of every lesson anchored to it (`pendingGrammarChapterLesson.focusPointIds`), and a single "Continue" button (`grammarActions.dismissGrammarChapterLesson`). No Learn/Skip choice - there is nothing to introduce here, only to review.

**The grammar cloze** (a `'grammar-cloze'` exercise: `SentenceClozeCard` under `GrammarCardHeader`) - the fill-in-the-blank translation exercise: a top-right `MasteryRing` reflecting the point's own `SRSEntry` (`currentGrammarProgress?.entry.memoryStrength ?? 0`, issue #32 follow-up RC3 item 1), then `example.en` as the prompt, then the sentence rendered through `InteractiveSentence` (a gloss on hover over every word, furigana once answered) with one `ClozeBlank` per span (see `computeBlankPlan`). Deliberately does **not** show `grammarPoint.formation` **or** `grammarPoint.title` anywhere on this card (RC3 item 2; RC4 item 1 for the title) - unlike the intro card, this one is testing recall of the blanked pattern, and either one can literally spell out the answer (many points' `title` *is* the formation written out, e.g. `それじゃ、～`). Only the JLPT chip, the register hint and, once answered, the link to the point stay. Each input is sized from its own **live typed value**, not from the expected answer - sizing from the answer both froze the width and leaked the answer's character count before the user typed anything (issue #32, item 1). On feedback, each blank's own border colour reflects its own result, and any blank short of strictly right reveals its form beside it (with furigana when its reading is known, linked to its word when it is one).

Each blank also has a small "?" hint button (hidden once feedback is shown): first click shows the word's English gloss beneath the input, second click reveals the answer (the slot's `reveal`) directly in the now-disabled input and grades that blank as `'minor_error'` on submit regardless of what was typed before (RC3 item 3 - previously `'pass'`) - see Per-blank hint control in State Management → Grammar Activity State.

When `computeBlankPlan` returns a read-only plan (no blankable word in any of the point's examples - issue #32, item 6), the exercise is a `'study'` one and `StudyCard` renders it: the full Japanese sentence (furigana and glosses on every word), no inputs, and a single Continue button that writes a `defer` effect, bypassing the Submit/feedback cycle since there's nothing to grade. `canSubmit` additionally requires at least one slot, so an empty card can never reach Submit. It deliberately does **not** require every blank to be filled: see Submit is never gated on filled blanks below.

When `point.usageNote` is set, the cloze card shows it as a small italic line in its feedback note once answered (before answering it can name the form; the structured register hint, `FORMALITY_HINT` in `GrammarPrompts.tsx`, stands in for it), and the study card beneath the JLPT chip (issue #42 - near-synonym points like でも/しかし/けれども are otherwise ungradable from the English prompt alone, since nothing in the sentence signals which register is expected). Nothing renders for the 409/828 points without one - no placeholder. Deliberately not shown on `GrammarIntroCard` or `GrammarDetailScreen`'s answer-adjacent areas beyond the explanation cards, since those already show `title`/`formation` and the note's whole purpose is disambiguation *without* those. Originally verified by hand-patching a point locally, before the dataset had any real `usageNote` values (see the Core Data Models `GrammarPoint` note); the dataset now populates it for 419/828 points as of the `apps/gokan-srs/dataset` submodule bump in issue #47.

### Search appearance (`index.html`, `public/favicon.*`)

What Google and DuckDuckGo show for the site is decided entirely by `apps/gokan-srs/index.html`, and three things about it are worth knowing.

**`/favicon.ico` must exist as a real file.** Search engines and their favicon services request it directly at the site root regardless of what `<link rel="icon">` says. With nothing there, CloudFront's SPA fallback answered `index.html` at `200 text/html`, so DuckDuckGo rendered no icon at all: it was asking for an image and being handed a web page. This is the same distribution-wide `custom_error_response` behaviour documented under Deployment, showing up somewhere it actively broke something.

**The icons are generated by `scripts/build-favicon.py`, run manually when the logo changes.** It is Python and Pillow in a Bun repository for one reason: the mark draws 語感 as SVG `<text>`, so rasterising it needs a CJK font at render time, and the JS-side rasterisers available here fall back to a font without CJK coverage and silently emit tofu boxes. Pillow lets the font be named explicitly and the glyphs asserted non-empty before anything is written. The `.ico` is packed by hand rather than through Pillow's ICO save, which only accepts one source image and resizes it: each size is drawn at its own scale instead, so the ring still reads at 16px rather than being a blurry downscale of the 48px art.

**The `<noscript>` block is not dead weight.** Googlebot executes JavaScript and indexes the rendered page, but DuckDuckGo took its result snippet from this block rather than from `meta description`, so both need to say something sensible and both need updating together.

**Each route declares its own canonical** (`utils/seo.utils.ts`'s `routeSeo`, rendered by `components/RouteSeo.tsx` once in `App`, above every gate; React 19 hoists the tags into `<head>`). `/` and `/about` also carry their own title and description (and their `og:` and `twitter:` copies): with one static pair in `index.html` the two indexed pages had the same title. `index.html` keeps only a default `<title>`, the homepage's, for clients that do not run JavaScript; React inserts a route's `<title>` before the first one in `<head>`, so the route's wins. It also keeps the homepage's `og:` and `twitter:` title and description, for the link-preview scrapers that do not run JavaScript (Facebook, X, Slack, Discord): without them a shared link showed a bare card. A shared `/about` link therefore previews as the homepage until About is prerendered. A test checks each of the pair appears once and equals `routeSeo('/').page`; `index.html` has no static meta description, since it would sit beside the route's as a duplicate. `index.html` carries no canonical, `og:url`, `twitter:url` or robots tag, because it is every route: its old `canonical` of `https://gokan-srs.com/` told Google `/about` was the homepage, so `/about` was never indexed (a test keeps them out). `/` and `/about` are canonical to themselves. The detail routes that show a dictionary entry are canonical to its dictionary page (`/vocab/:id`, `/kanji/:character`, `/grammar/:id`, `/grammar/family/:id` to `/dictionary/.../`): the dictionary writes one page per vocab file, kanji, grammar point and family id, and the app's family route is keyed by a contrast entry, whose keys are all family ids (checked against the dataset when this was written; a contrast key with no member point would point at a missing page). Everything else (sessions, lists, stats, settings, listening) is `noindex`: it shows the visitor's own state. The origin is the page's own. `/about` renders before onboarding (`isAboutPath`, the same path test `routeSeo` uses), since a crawler is a visitor who has not set up: without that, the URL indexed as About would show the onboarding flow. It keeps the app's header and footer then, the footer at every width (`AppFooter`'s `alwaysVisible`; after setup a phone has its links in the tab bar's More sheet), so the indexed page has links into the site on a phone too (`check:layout` asserts a visible dictionary link there), but not the bottom tab bar (every tab leads to onboarding) nor `PageRenderedSignal` (no study flow to preload). The header search works then, but `WordStudyStatus` shows no Learn button with no progress to add the word to. A test reads `App.tsx`'s routes and checks that every fixed route under `/vocab/`, `/kanji/` and `/grammar/` is `noindex` and every parameterised one has a dictionary canonical, so a new screen there cannot silently claim a dictionary page that does not exist. The dictionary's mount path is `DICTIONARY_BASE_PATH` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`), shared by these canonicals, `DictionaryLink` and the dictionary's own `BASE_PATH` default; `@gokan/deploy`'s key prefix is tested against it.

`meta keywords` was removed: every major engine has ignored it since 2009, and it only advertised the target terms to competitors.

### Onboarding Flow (`pages/setup/OnboardingFlow.tsx`)

Wrapper for new users, replacing a direct `SetupScreen` call. Manages a two-step process:
1. **Welcome Screen** (`WelcomeScreen.tsx`, its sections in `welcome/`): the homepage a crawler renders (it never has a setup), so its `<h1>` says what the page is ("Japanese vocabulary for the kanji you know"). Top to bottom: a header (the brand as plain text, Dictionary and About), the hero (`WelcomeHero`), "Why kanji first" with a kanji demo (`KanjiDemo`: tap the kanji you know and the words made of them come into focus), "How it works" (`HowItWorks`: three steps carrying one example word, which changes with the order picked in step one, down to a working mini review card, `ReviewDemo`), "Also in Gokan" (its "Your kanji book" line is `supportedOrdersList` from `@gokan/dataset-schema`, over each order's `listName` in `ORDER_INFO`, so it cannot promise fewer orders than the picker offers; `check:conventions` rejects a second `Record<KanjiOrderId, …>` in an app), a closing call and a footer. The demos' words come from `welcome/welcomeExamples.ts`, one example per offered order, checked against the compiled dataset by its test (positions in `index/kanji-orders/`, readings, and the sentence with the word's match). `ReviewDemo` is a marketing demo, not an exercise: it checks an answer against the example's own accept-list (`demoAnswerIsRight`) and never touches the engine or SRS state. The paths:
   - *I'm just starting out (Beginner)*: Skips the wizard entirely - initializes with KKLC step 0, an empty kanji set, and the `kanji_coverage` learning order.
   - *I already know some kanji (Kanji Learner)*, and the closing *Set up Gokan*: Proceeds to the Setup Screen wizard.
   - *Used Gokan on another device?*: `GoogleLoginButton` (`welcome/GoogleLoginButton.tsx`, "Restore from Google Drive", inline in the hero's small print) - Drive login to restore existing progress (auto-retries the download once authenticated; the restore under way and a retry show in the same place). The restored progress goes straight into state (see Google Drive Sync), so the app leaves onboarding without a reload.
2. **Setup Screen** (`SetupScreen.tsx`): The wizard for the "Kanji Learner" path - collects kanji knowledge (method + step/count) and learning order preferences. The decisions come first and the fine-tuning last: kanji learning method, the count on its own (the stepper's `knownReadout` shows "120 of 2,300 in KKLC order known" in place of "Known: 120 kanji", so the count's meaning shows without the grid: with nothing stored the two counts are equal, and showing both stated it twice), vocabulary order, **Start learning**, then "Mark individual kanji", the known-kanji grid in a `Disclosure`, closed. The grid used to sit open between the count and the vocabulary order: 2,300 kanji in KKLC put the order choice and the button about 15,000px down on desktop and 22,000px at 320px, with nothing on screen saying there was anything past it, on the very path for learners who know many kanji. `KanjiKnowledgeEditor` takes `collapseGrid` and `beforeGrid` (what goes between the count and the closed grid) for this; the profile page passes neither and keeps the grid open with the stepper in its toolbar. `check:layout` holds the vocabulary order heading and the button within a fixed distance of the top at 320, 375 and 1280px (`SETUP_REACH_MAX`). A sticky "Start learning" footer over the grid was rejected: it fixes the button but not the vocabulary order, and a fixed bar over a tall tap grid takes space from the tiles on a phone. Instead the open grid ends with a ghost "Done marking", which closes the disclosure (`Disclosure` takes a controlled `open`) and brings its title to the bottom of the screen, Start learning just above it; `check:layout` holds that action within a screen of the page's end and the button on screen after it.

Calls `actions.setupComplete()` when either path produces a valid `SetupValues` object.

### Settings Screen (`pages/settings/Settings.tsx`)

Reached from the header's **person icon** (`UserRound`), not a cog: the cog now belongs to the per-activity panels below, so keeping one in the header would read as "the same thing, globally".

Section order, top to bottom:
1. **Account (Google Drive sync)** - moved to the top of the page from the bottom. Signing in is what makes every other setting and all progress follow the user across devices, so it is the first thing worth doing here.
2. **Appearance** - theme.
3. **Review pacing** - `learningFrequency` only. This is the one learning preference that is genuinely global: both `SRSService` and `grammarSrs.service.ts` read it.
4. **AI Context Validation** - Gemini toggle, API key, validate-all toggle. Deliberately **stays global** even though only the vocab meaning quiz consumes it today: context-aware validation is expected to reach other activities.
5. **Danger zone** - grammar-only reset, then reset-all (both with confirmation).

Activity-scoped settings are deliberately **not** here: they live on the Main hub's activity cards (below), so the page holds only what applies to the whole app.

### Per-activity quiz settings (`components/QuizSettingsMenu.tsx`, `pages/settings/sections/`)

Settings that only affect one kind of quiz are reached from a cog on **that activity's card on the Main hub** (`MainScreen.tsx`), and from nowhere else - not from the global settings page, and not from inside a running session. The hub is where the user picks an activity, so it is also where they set that activity up.

**`QuizSettingsMenu`** - the cog affordance and the panel chrome, nothing more. Owns open/close (Escape, backdrop click), and renders the panel through a **React portal onto `document.body`**: an inline absolutely-positioned panel would be clipped by the card and constrained by the hub's grid. Every panel footer links out to `/settings` for the global options.

The cog is a **sibling** of `ActivityCard`'s `<button>`, absolutely positioned over its top right corner, not a child of it: a button nested inside a button is invalid HTML, and a nested cog's click would bubble up and start the session instead of opening the settings.

**`pages/settings/sections/VocabQuizSettings.tsx`** - every setting that only affects the vocabulary quiz: `preferredLearningOrder`, `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement` (shown for every order except `kklc`, a no-op there since it's gated by step rather than by kanji set - including `jlpt`, which is kanji-filtered by default and relies on this toggle to opt back into its old unconditional behavior), `kanjiCoverageTarget`, `enableMeaningQuiz`, and `meaningContextThresholdPoints` (issue #91). Takes `{ settings, onUpdateSettings, dense }`; `dense` is the narrow single-column layout the popover always uses. "Train meaning in context" renders as a `SettingSlider` (0-200, step 10) rather than the three-preset `OptionGrid` it used to be - the value text reads "From the first review" at 0, "Never" at 200, and "From {n} on the meaning ring" in between; writing it saves only `meaningContextThresholdPoints`, resolved for display via `meaningContextThresholdOf` (see `UserSettings` above) so an existing user reading a legacy `meaningContextThreshold` still sees their preset's equivalent point value as the slider's starting position.

**`pages/settings/sections/GrammarQuizSettings.tsx`** - a placeholder message. Grammar genuinely has no settings of its own yet (everything a grammar session reads is shared), and the honest placeholder was preferred over inventing a grammar-only toggle just to fill the panel. The cog is still shown, for symmetry with the vocabulary quiz.

**Shared controls**: `components/ui/SettingToggle.tsx` (the label + description + switch row, with `dense`/`disabled` variants), `components/ui/SettingSlider.tsx` (the label + description + `<input type="range">` row, styled to match `SettingToggle`; issue #91's "Train meaning in context" is its first user), and `OptionGrid`'s `dense` prop were extracted so these panels reuse the settings page's controls rather than restyling their own.

### Submit is never gated on filled blanks (`useExerciseTurn`)

`canSubmit` checks, for the grammar cloze and the conjugation drill (`submitsEmpty`), that a gradable card is loaded and that feedback is not already showing. It does **not** check that every blank has been filled, and that is a deliberate robustness choice rather than a convenience one.

Requiring every input non-empty makes the card **blockable**: any state where an input cannot be filled leaves the learner with no way forward at all, no matter what else is on screen. That has now happened three times, each from a different cause, and the `blankWordSpans` and `GrammarQuizState.example` doc comments record the first two (a multi-token marker split across boxes with no way to know which box wanted which token, and blank indices from one sentence applied to another). The third was the hint button: revealing a blank showed the accepted form as a render-time display value while the answer in state stayed empty, so revealing the last remaining blank left Submit permanently disabled.

Each individual cause is fixed, but the shared failure mode is the gate itself, so the gate is gone. An empty blank is now a supported answer meaning "I do not know this one": `gradeSlot` grades it `'pass'` (the same result a literally typed "pass" gives, -0.15) rather than `'wrong'`, and the accepted form is revealed in the feedback, which is what the learner was reaching for the hint button to get. Auto-advance still requires every blank strictly correct, so a skipped blank always pauses on feedback instead of skipping past the answer. The single-answer vocab cards keep requiring an answer, so a stray Enter does not grade a pass.
### Vocabulary list (`pages/vocab/VocabularyListScreen.tsx`)

Route `/vocab`, a daily tool rather than a report, so it left Stats. Every word in `learningQueue` as a `ListRow`: headword and reading, the first sense's glosses, one ring per trained direction under column headers that serve as their legend, the next review (relative) and the JLPT chip; on a phone the rings are hidden and the glosses take a second line. Toolbar: search (word, reading, meaning; romaji read as the header search reads it, `readVocabQuery`, so `taber` keeps 食べる), a status group with counts, a JLPT select, a sort select and a reverse toggle; 50 rows a page, controls persisted through `hooks/usePersistedControls.ts`. The status filters and sort keys are pure and tested (`utils/vocabStatus.utils.ts`): **Due now** (`isVocabDue`), **Learning** / **Mastered** (`isVocabFullyMastered`), **Struggling** (3 or more `wrong` among the last 10 reviews, every direction merged by date), **All**. Default: Learning. `?status=` from the URL overrides the persisted one (the Stats tiles link to it).

### Grammar home: All points (`pages/grammar/GrammarBrowseScreen.tsx`)

Route `/grammar/browse`, the second tab of the Grammar home (`GrammarHomeHeader`: title, size, `TabNav` with Curriculum and All points). In learner mode (the default) every point of the teaching order as a `ListRow` (title, romaji, short explanation, status), grouped by JLPT level, with search, level and status filters, a sort (teaching order, next review, most mistakes, mastery: `utils/grammarBrowse.utils.ts`, tested) and a Level or Family grouping. Family groups with an authored contrast lesson link to `/grammar/family/:familyId` ("Compare when to use each"). It replaced the grammar list that used to sit on Stats.

A **Dataset details** toggle (off by default, persisted) restores the inspection view the page used to be: one neutral line of dataset facts per row (id, quiz kind, formality, family axis, variant relation, order index, chapter, anchored examples, drill items), the coverage tiles, the dataset filters, and the points outside the teaching order (realization variants). No categorical colours in either mode.

**Two `min-width: auto` floors had to be released before this page fitted a phone**, and the first fix addressed only one of them, so the page still overflowed by 770px. Both are the same CSS default in different places: a flex or grid item will not shrink below its content's min-content width unless told to.

1. Each filter group wraps, and below `sm` the groups stack. A non-wrapping group could neither wrap nor shrink, and the Family group's five long labels set a floor far past a phone's width.
2. **The page root carries `w-full`**, and `PointCard`'s root carries `min-w-0`. These are the ones that actually mattered. `App.tsx` wraps every page in `flex flex-col items-center`, and under `align-items: center` a flex item is sized to `fit-content`, not stretched: without `w-full` the root simply grew to its own `max-w-6xl`, so at a 375px viewport the document measured 1161px. Every other page root in the app already had `w-full`, which is why this page alone broke. Once the root was constrained, the cards still forced 591px each, because the `truncate` classes inside them set `white-space: nowrap`, making each card's min-content the full untruncated string; `min-w-0` is what lets truncation actually engage.

Measured rather than eyeballed, which is why the second floor was found at all: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth - clientWidth` went 786 to 770 to 0, checked at 320px and 375px, with the three-column desktop grid confirmed unchanged at 1280px.

Its filter and sort controls persist in `sessionStorage`, so navigating into a grammar detail page and back does not reset them, like the Vocabulary list's. Both share `hooks/usePersistedControls.ts` rather than each carrying their own copy of the read/write pair. Two details in that hook are load-bearing:
- **Sets are stored as arrays.** `JSON.stringify(new Set())` is `{}`, so persisting the multi-select filters directly would silently save them as empty and look exactly like the filters not being kept at all.
- **The snapshot is read via a lazy `useState`, not `useRef(read(key)).current`.** A ref's argument is still evaluated on every render even though only the first result is kept, so the previous form re-ran `getItem` plus `JSON.parse` on every keystroke and discarded the result. It also reads a ref during render, which the React lint rules reject.

`sessionStorage` rather than a React context: a context is lost on reload and would need a provider above every screen using it, while these are per-screen UI controls nothing else reads.

### Grammar home: Curriculum (`pages/grammar/GrammarChapterBrowseScreen.tsx`)

Route `/grammar/chapters` (issue #58), the Grammar home's first tab and where the header's Grammar link goes. Every chapter of the authored teaching order under level headings (`groupChaptersByLevel`: consecutive chapters of one level, so the teaching order is kept), each expandable to its points. See Services & Business Logic → Grammar Activity: Chapter progress display for the shared `computeGrammarChapterProgress`/`ChapterProgressBar` this page and the hub card reuse.

- On load it expands and scrolls to the learner's **current chapter**, the one the hub's grammar card names (`grammarHubChapter.chapterId`), marked "Current"; `?chapter=<id>` (from a grammar point's locator) overrides it. Applied once per mount (a `useRef` guard), so it never fights a later manual collapse.
- A chapter row: its number, title, two-line summary, the progress bar and `chapterProgressLabel` ("1 mastered · 2 learning · 1 new", fixed order, zero parts omitted). Points inside: status dot and title, each a `ListRow` to `/grammar/:id`. Point titles come from the already-whole `GrammarService.loadBrowseIndex()`.
- A plain text search filters chapters by title and summary.

### Profile Screen (`pages/profile/UserProfileScreen.tsx`)

Hosts `KanjiKnowledgeEditor` (shared with the setup wizard), built around the two things people actually come here to do:

- **Move the known-kanji count by a fixed amount** (they studied another N kanji elsewhere): `KanjiCountStepper` pairs the raw count field with `-N`/`+N` buttons whose increment is the user's own, editable inline and persisted as `UserSettings.kanjiCountStep`. The profile page owns that persistence (`saveSettings`) and passes it down as `countStep`/`onCountStepChange`. The setup wizard supplies neither, and the whole stepping affordance (buttons plus increment field) is then left out rather than defaulted: a first-time user enters a count once, and there is no settings object to persist an increment into anyway.
- **Look one kanji up and see whether it counts as known**: `KanjiKnowledgeGrid` lays the ordered list out in rows of 10 (five on a phone, where ten left each tile about 20px wide once the gutters and gaps were taken out, so a 24px glyph overflowed its own tile and nothing was big enough to tap) with a position gutter (so "the kanji around 1240" is findable by eye), plus a search box resolving either a position (`1240`, `#1240`) or a pasted character via `utils/kanjiSearch.utils.ts`'s pure `findKanjiMatch`. The grid sits in the page flow under a sticky toolbar (the search, and the stepper when it is passed as `toolbar`); a match is ring-highlighted and scrolled to the middle of the screen. Changing the count scrolls the same way, to the frontier the new count reaches (skipping the first render, so opening the page doesn't animate anywhere the user didn't ask to go), but only when the caller sets `followCount` (the profile page, where the stepper rides in the toolbar; the decision is `followsCountChange` in `utils/kanjiGrid.utils.ts`): in setup the stepper sits above the closed grid, and following the count there would carry the page away from the field being typed in. Clicking any tile still toggles it known/unknown, as does the search result's own button.

The rows span the full width, matching the search box above them, so the tiles (and therefore the kanji) are as large as the page allows; they stay square via `aspect-square`.

The editor's method picker lists `KANJI_ORDERS`: the four most followed (KKLC, RTK, JLPT, frequency) and the other books behind "More methods". Picking one loads its `index/kanji-orders/{id}.json` (`KanjiFormProvider`, the previous list kept up while it loads). The editor reports a change only once the list has loaded, so a learner is never saved with one method's count measured in another method's list. The grid's label is the order's own (`kanjiOrder(method).label`).

**The known set is one durable union; orders only fill it** (`context/KanjiForm/kanjiFormReducer.ts`, pure and tested directly). A learner who knows KKLC's first 500 and some RTK or JLPT kanji on top can say so:

- Switching order keeps the set as it is and starts the count input at 0. The grid's legend ("N of 3,000 in RTK order known") still shows how far the set already reaches into the new order.
- Raising the count adds that order's kanji up to the new count, and remembers which of them were not known yet (`raised`, editor state only). Lowering takes back only those. Kanji known before a raise (the stored set, another order, the kanji page, "Learn next kanji", a hand mark) stay: removing them is what hand marks and Reset are for. So +N then -N restores the set exactly. A switch and a Reset clear `raised`, so lowering in one order never reaches kanji another order's raises added.
- After a reload `raised` is empty: the count shows the stored `step`, and lowering it moves the input but removes nothing that was stored.
- During setup, before anything is marked, the set simply follows the count in the chosen order (the default count is not a stored set, so lowering it takes kanji away and picking an order keeps the count).
- A kanji marked one by one in the grid (`marked`) wins over every count, in every order, until a Reset. Opening the page starts from the stored `kanjiSet`, not from the first N of the stored order, so kanji marked on a kanji's detail page or by "Learn next kanji" are kept.
- **Reset** (behind an inline confirm) is the escape hatch: only the current order's first N, no marks ("Keep only the first N of RTK?"; at 0, right after a switch, "Clear all N known kanji?" empties the set). "Known: N kanji" under the stepper is the size of the whole set, independent of any order.

`method` stays the primary order, and `step` is saved as its **known prefix** (how many of its first kanji are known: `knownPrefix`), not the count input. `step` drives the "By kanji" learning order (`SRSService` walks steps 1..`step` of the method's index), so saving the input's 0 after a switch would stop new words; the prefix keeps sequencing on what is known whatever the input shows. Positions are still named in the primary order ("RTK #346"). Nothing about storage changed: `kanjiSet` was always the resolved set.

Its scroll pane carries the `.scrollbar-subtle` utility (`index.css`), which styles both `scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` and the `::-webkit-scrollbar` pseudo-elements. Chrome/Edge otherwise draw a wide opaque track that reads as a bright band down the side of a dark pane, where Firefox's thin scrollbar already looked right.

### Kanji Detail Screen (`pages/kanji/KanjiDetailScreen.tsx`)

Route: `/kanji/:character`. Mirrors `VocabDetailScreen`'s card-based layout at a smaller scale:
- Header (`DetailHeader`): the glyph, its readings labelled On and Kun (`kunReadingLabel`: まな(ぶ)), neutral chips for the JLPT level and the kanji's number in the learner's own method ("RTK #346"), and the Known / Not known status with its button. Loaded from `kanji/{character}.json` (about 1 KB), not the whole `kanji.json`
- Body (`DetailBody`, the vocab page's 8/4 grid): main column Meanings, then `KanjiVocabListCard`; side column Components (each a `KanjiTile`, linked when the component is itself a kanji of the dataset, found by `loadKanji` per component; plain otherwise) with the stroke count and radical, then a "Dictionary details" disclosure (school grade, radical number, newspaper frequency, name readings, and the kanji's number in every book that teaches it). Below `lg`: Meanings, Components, words, details
- `KanjiVocabListCard` (`pages/kanji/KanjiVocabListCard.tsx`): capped/expandable list of vocabulary containing this kanji, sourced from the `kanji-vocab.json` reverse index, each row navigating to `/vocab/:id`

`VocabDetailScreen`'s kanji-breakdown card (see Core Data Models → Vocabulary) provides the reverse link: each kanji in a word's `containedKanji` is a clickable chip navigating to `/kanji/:character`, so users can move vocab → kanji → vocab.

### Grammar Detail Screen (`pages/grammar/GrammarDetailScreen.tsx`)

Route: `/grammar/:grammarId` (issue #32 follow-up). Mirrors `VocabDetailScreen`'s card-based layout, letting a user look at a single grammar point outside of a live review:
