If a future agent needs to add data to `gokan-dataset`, it needs credentials scoped to that repo too (a broader GitHub App token or a PAT) - this hasn't been set up, so cross-repo dataset changes currently need a human/maintainer step.

### Media Dataset

The listening library's data follows the same split as every other dataset: selection, fetch, build and compiled output all live in the **`gokan-dataset`** submodule, synced into `public/data/compiled/media/` by `sync-dataset.ts`.

- **Source**: [Jiten](https://jiten.moe), whose derived data (vocabulary lists, frequency and difficulty statistics) is CC BY-SA 4.0, attribution required. Jiten analyses subtitles but never distributes them, and neither does the dataset: only which words an episode uses and how often. Word ids are JMdict ids, the same as Gokan's.
- **Selection**: `dataset/data/raw/media/selection.json`, a list of Jiten deck ids: four picked by hand (のんのんびより 16685, ガールズ&パンツァー 51581, からかい上手の高木さん 34772, 名探偵プリキュア！ 134921) plus 100 marked `source: 'easiest'`, added by **`select:media`** (`dataset/scripts/select-easiest-anime.ts`, network, run by hand): it walks Jiten's anime difficulty ranking (`get-media-decks-by-type-ranked/1`), easiest first, skipping Adult and Ecchi genres (a study app, with covers on the Main hub; such a title can still be added by hand) and titles over 52 episodes (a long-runner costs thousands of requests and a multi-megabyte file). Films and specials qualify and become one-episode titles. Jiten's terms forbid systematic extraction of its database, so only selected titles are ever fetched; there is no bulk dump to ingest anyway.
- **`fetch:media`** (`dataset/scripts/fetch-jiten.ts`, network, run by hand): for each selected deck, `GET /api/media-deck/{id}/detail` (series + episode sub-decks with stats) then each episode's `GET /api/media-deck/{episodeId}/vocabulary`, paginated 200 at a time (default ordering is by row id, stable across pages), 750ms between requests. Writes a trimmed snapshot (word id + occurrences per episode, stats, links, genre ids, tags) to `data/raw/media/jiten/{deckId}.json`, committed, one file per title as it goes so an interrupted run resumes. By default it fetches only selected titles with no snapshot yet; deck ids as arguments refetch those, `--refresh` refetches everything. A deck with no episode sub-decks (a film) is fetched as its own single episode. `data/raw/media/` is exempted from Git LFS in the dataset's `.gitattributes` so the hand-edited selection and the snapshots diff readably.
- **`build:media`** (`dataset/scripts/build-media.ts`, offline, chained at the end of `build:data`): keeps only words that resolve to a compiled vocab entry, redirecting merged homographs (`mergedVocabs`) to their base id so a known base word is not counted unknown under its merged id. About 64% of an episode's distinct words survive. Output: `compiled/media/index.json`, `compiled/media/library.json` (every title's series words) and `compiled/media/{id}.json`. Genre ids become names (`JITEN_GENRES`, mirroring Jiten's enum).
- **`fetch:covers`** (`dataset/scripts/fetch-anilist-covers.ts`, network, run by hand after `fetch:media`): one AniList GraphQL request (public, no key) per 50 selected titles, by the AniList id in Jiten's links, writing cover **URLs only** (the 100px, 230px and 460px files) to `data/raw/media/covers.json`; `build:media` attaches them as `cover` when present. The images stay on AniList's CDN: the artwork is the studios' and outside the dataset's license.
- **Adding a title**: add its Jiten deck id to the selection, `bun run --cwd apps/gokan-srs/dataset fetch:media <id>`, then `fetch:covers` and `build:media`, then bump the submodule pointer here.

### Audio (issue #164)

Words, example sentences and grammar examples have recordings: text-to-speech, made once by the dataset and served from a bucket of their own. Nothing plays them yet (play buttons are #178, listening exercises #165 and #166).

- **Engine and voice:** VOICEVOX 0.26.0, the voice 冥鳴ひまり (ノーマル, style 14), Ogg Opus at 24 kbps (`.opus`, `audio/ogg`, plays in Safari on iOS). Chosen by a listening test, all of it in the dataset's `data/audio/voice.json`. The voice's terms require the credit `VOICEVOX:冥鳴ひまり`, which the About page shows from `index/audio.json` (below). Every clip is TTS: Tatoeba's own recordings cover 1.4% of our sentences and 11 of them are under an open licence (MODIFICATION_LOG.md, 2026-10-29).
- **What it says:** the `forced` variant. A word is read from its written form when the engine says it as our `reading.primary`, else from its kana. In a sentence, a matched word the engine reads differently from `matches[].reading` is sent in kana, so the clip says what the furigana shows.
- **Scope of the first build:** 107,408 items: 35,872 word readings, 68,591 sentences (each word's first 3 in the order a new learner meets them) and 2,945 authored grammar examples, in 107,307 clips (items with the same text share one). The other grammar examples are Tatoeba sentences of ours and share their clip. 710 MB (words 2.4 KiB, sentences 8.5 KiB, grammar examples 9.1 KiB a clip).
- **The key** (dataset `src/audio/key.ts`): the first 16 hex characters of a SHA-256 over everything a clip is made from: engine and version, style, synthesis parameters, format, and the exact text sent. A reading fix, a new voice or a parameter change gives a new key, so a new clip; the same input always gives the same key, so a clip is written to the bucket once, ever. A test pins one key and checks that each input changes it.
- **Schema** (`@gokan/dataset-schema`, `audio.ts`): `audio?: AudioRef` (`{ clip }`) on `Vocabulary`, `Sentence` and `GrammarExample`, set only when the dataset commits the clip (a deploy uploads it first, below); `grammarExampleToSentence` carries an example's onto the `Sentence` it makes. `index/audio.json` is an `AudioIndex` (`engine`, `engineVersion`, `voice`, `style`, `credit`, `termsUrl`, `extension`, `contentType`); the dataset writes it with the first attached clip and removes it when none is. A clip is served at `/audio/{clip}.{extension}`.
- **The credit:** `services/audio.service.ts` (`loadAudioIndex`) reads `index/audio.json` through `fetchJson`, or answers null without asking when the pinned dataset has no such file (`__AUDIO_INDEX_SHIPPED__`, set by `vite.config.ts` from the submodule): a missing dataset file answers with the SPA shell, which would not parse. The About page renders the file's `credit` (`pages/about/VoiceCredit.tsx`: `lang="ja"`, gothic, never broken across lines), linked to `termsUrl`, and keeps no copy of it. About is lenient: if a shipped file fails to load, the credit is just not shown. The player (#178) is where a broken file must not go unnoticed: it needs the file's `extension` to build any clip URL, so it treats a failed `loadAudioIndex` as fatal, as every other dataset file is.

**Where the clips live.** One bucket, `gokan-srs-audio-<account>` in eu-west-3, shared by staging and production, owned by the production stack (`apps/gokan-srs/terraform/main.tf`). `staging/main.tf` builds its name and address rather than looking it up: a lookup fails the staging plan until production is applied, so the change adding the bucket could not plan green before it merged. Production is still applied first: until the bucket exists, staging's `/audio/*` has nothing behind it, and staging's `check "audio_bucket"` warns on plan and apply. Private, read through OAC by both distributions (its policy allows this account's CloudFront distributions, so neither stack names the other's), with a `/audio/*` behaviour on each: the request path is the object key, `audio/{clip}.opus`. Clips are uploaded with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, which `CachingOptimized` honours up to its one-year maximum; a new clip has a new key, so nothing is ever invalidated. No versioning, no lifecycle rules, no replication (each is charged per object), and `prevent_destroy` on the bucket. The SPA fallback (`custom_error_response`) is distribution-wide, so a missing clip answers 200 with the app's shell; the app only asks for clips the dataset lists, and a deployed dataset lists only clips the bucket holds (below).

**The clips are committed, in Git LFS** (the dataset's `data/audio/clips/{clip}.opus`, mirrored by the bucket's `audio/`), with `data/audio/clips.json`: each item's clip and a hash of the text and readings it was made from. The dataset's `.lfsconfig` (`lfs.fetchexclude`) keeps their content out of every clone, pull and CI checkout, which get the pointer files only: no workflow downloads the 710 MB, and the organisation's LFS quota (10 GiB of storage and 10 GiB of downloads a month on the free plan) goes to the one upload that needs it. The voice's terms ask only for the credit and allow any use, so the clips can sit in a public repository; they stay outside its CC BY-SA licence, which the dataset's README says.

**The deploy is the only writer of the bucket.** `deploy-site.yml` (staging and production alike) runs the dataset's `upload:audio` on the dataset version it ships, before publishing either app: it lists the bucket (one LIST per 1,000 keys), fetches from LFS only the committed clips the bucket lacks, and writes them, one PUT each, never overwriting or deleting (staging and production may ship different dataset versions). It writes at most 2,000 and fails over that, the guard against a key bug rewriting the bucket, so a deploy never ships a dataset naming clips it could not upload. A first fill or a voice change sets the `AUDIO_MAX_UPLOADS` repository variable for one deploy and deletes it after. Its key (`AUDIO_AWS_*` secrets, the `AUDIO_BUCKET` variable) belongs to an IAM user that can only list this bucket and put objects under `audio/`; the site's deploy key cannot touch the bucket, and no machine holds a key for it. `deploy-s3.ts` still writes and prunes the site bucket only.

**Making them** (in the dataset, by hand, the engine running; see its README):

```bash
bun run build:audio -- --engine http://127.0.0.1:50022   # synthesise and encode the missing clips into data/audio/clips/ (resumable)
bun run attach:audio                                     # offline: write `audio` from data/audio/clips.json
git add data/audio compiled                              # a dataset pull request; the next deploy of it uploads the new clips
```

- `build:audio` makes the most frequent words first, prints progress and time left, stops cleanly on Ctrl+C, and refuses an engine whose version differs from `voice.json`'s (that would rename every clip). A run that finishes removes the clips no item uses any more (the bucket keeps them). On a GPU (VOICEVOX's Windows DirectML engine) the first build took 18.7 hours with two workers, encoding included (1.59 clips/s); on a CPU a clip takes 1.6 s.
- `attach:audio` runs at the end of `build:data` and `build:grammar`, offline. It writes `audio` only where the item's hash still matches `clips.json`, so an edited sentence loses its clip until it is made again, and it fails if `clips.json` names a clip `data/audio/clips/` lacks.
- The first fill is ~107k PUTs, about $0.60; a later deploy pays only for new clips; a voice change pays it once more.

### Test Infrastructure

**Test Framework**: Vitest

**Test Files:**
- `src/services/srs.service.test.ts` - SRS algorithm unit tests
  - Formula verification tests (8 test cases covering different scenarios)
  - Minor error classification tests through `applyAnswer` (こたへ / こーたえ minor, こえ wrong)
  - Alternative reading matching tests
  - Per-quiz-type retry flag behavior tests
  - A correct reading answer no longer pushes a due meaning's `dueDate` forward (regression guard for the removed stagger - see State Management → Session quiz cap)
  - Meaning-quiz-disabled scheduling tests (graduation on reading mastery alone)
  - JLPT learning-order tests: N5→N1 walk order, kanji filtering on by default (and disabled via `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement`), already-queued exclusion, frequency fallback once the lists run dry (without re-serving a JLPT word, and respecting the same toggle), and the matching `countLearnableVocabulary` counts
  - `rescheduleForRetry` (no credit: strength, interval and difficulty untouched, rescheduled at the unchanged interval from now and never below `minInterval`, the word not coming back as a full review once its retry is answered, only its own direction flagged, still recording `lastReviewedAt`/`totalReviews`, a fresh entry seeded rather than throwing if somehow unactivated), and `applyProductionReinforcement` (indirect production credit)
- `src/services/exercise/grading.test.ts` - the one grader: the reading, meaning and word slots (alternatives, gloss splitting, typos, any form `correct` on a production slot, the kanji-skeleton and dropped-okurigana rules, a cloze blank's surface and reading); the parity table (a production slot and a grammar word blank grading typos, a dropped tail, another word, pass, empty, a revealed hint and near-synonyms identically, differing only where the conjugation counts); hints and empty answers; near-synonyms (by written form or reading typo, an exact match beating a typo whatever the order, conjugated forms, tried only on a miss, graded by the cue, curated pairs); `gradeExercise` (core decides, support scales, worst-of without a core, auto-advance only when strictly right, a confusable synonym neutral, the messages); the production builder (gloss or cloze, the reveal as the sentence writes it); a demonstrative answered with a sibling's meaning (neutral with the series note, every sibling named, the plurals, an exact sibling meaning over a typo of its own, its own meanings still correct, no false neutral on a typo, another word or the homophone 嗚呼, none on a production slot)
- `src/services/exercise/effects.test.ts` - `effectsOf` (a review on an ordinary answer, a retry plus credit to the typed word on a synonym, a retry alone on a confusable one, a grammar card reviewing the point and crediting its vocab once per word, nothing for a revealed blank, a grammar synonym crediting the typed word, a retry when every deciding blank was confusable, the study card deferred) and `applyEffects` (the review and its record, calibration only for a review, a retry without credit, no synonym farming on the retry loop, indirect credit to production only and below a real answer, seeding, a no-op when production is off or the word is not studied, the breakdown order, the study card's defer)
- `src/components/quiz/quizStyles.test.ts` - `missStyleOf`: a sibling demonstrative and a confusable synonym both get the near-miss look without the overlay, a plain wrong answer keeps the miss look

- `src/utils/clozeSentence.utils.test.ts` - `splitAtBlanks` (rejoins to the original, drops the blanked word, rebases offsets, drops a straddling match, several blanks and blanks at either end), `productionClozeSentence` and `grammarClozeSentence` (one blank per span, a reading only when known)
- `src/utils/answerMatching.test.ts` - the shared matcher: kanji-skeleton rule (dropped tail minor, different kanji and swapped okurigana wrong), romaji-keystroke typos (つま minor, やむ wrong, katakana like hiragana), a dropped kana wrong on a short word (おばさん, ゆき, きて) and minor on a long one (ひつようじゃなかた), `lenient` (dropped kana from 4 kana, two slips from 8, kanji and short words still strict), English normalization / length-scaled budget / partial answers / an empty-normalizing form never matching, and `matchBest` ordering and pass
- `src/utils/inflection.utils.test.ts` - the inflection generator (issue #95): `wordClassesOf` per class from real POS tags (and several classes on one word, none on a plain noun); `generateInflections` spot checks per class (ichidan with derived verbs, every godan sound change, 行って, ある's ない, 来る/こない/きて, する and する-nouns, い-adjectives, いい/よくない, 大変じゃなくて); `kanaOfSurface`; `isFormOfWord` accepting kanji/kana forms and the stem fallback while rejecting 上げる for 上がる, 会社 for 会話, たかい for 食べる and a bare stem

- `src/services/sync/driveClient.test.ts` - Drive REST wrapper tests (auth-error translation)
- `src/services/sync/googleDriveSync.test.ts` - `download` returning the merged progress itself with every local write failing (and writing none), and Drive's copy on a device with none; CAS retry-on-conflict, duplicate-file reconciliation, write-once remote backup
- `src/utils/knowledge.utils.test.ts` - Knowledge-points model tests: mastery-curve normalisation (a vocab mastered in reading + meaning is worth exactly 200), the interval→strength inversion (including undoing the `wrong`/`minor_error` post-processing multipliers and the frequency modifier), and curve construction (per-day bucketing, pre-window baseline collapsing, skipped-vocab crediting, knowledge loss after a failure, future-dated-log rejection)
- `src/utils/activity.utils.test.ts` - `buildDailyActivity` tests: zeroed buckets with no history, correct/minor_error grouped as correct and wrong as incorrect with pass excluded, reading + meaning history aggregated together, calendar-day bucketing (not exact timestamp), and logs outside the requested window dropped
- `src/utils/mediaCoverage.utils.test.ts` - listening library: `buildWordKnowledge` (mastered vs learning, a never-introduced word left out), `computeCoverage` (occurrence-weighted and distinct counts, empty list reads 0), `aggregateEpisodeWords`, `unknownWords`, `speechSpeedLabel` bands, the library filters (`libraryGenres` most common first, `filterLibrary` any-of genres and a search over titles and tags), `rankLibrary` (filtered, best coverage first, a title missing from the word file ranked last at 0%), and the watched marks (`isEpisodeWatched`, `mediaIdOfKey` reading an `episodeKey` back, `watchingByTitle` never counting a title whose id only shares a prefix, `watchingFirst` pinning a title being watched in a hidden genre while the search still hides it and a completed one follows the filter, `mergeWatchedEpisodes` newest-wins with local on a tie, undefined when neither side has marks)
- `src/utils/wordOrder.utils.test.ts` - listening "words to learn" sorts: most used here, general frequency, JLPT (N5 first, no level last), KKLC (frequency within a step), the fallback while indexes load, and the learner's own order (follows their learning order, learnable words first, the ignore-known-kanji switch, the kanji_coverage score, frequency once every kanji is covered); `isLearnableNow` (step-gated under kklc, all kanji known otherwise, unindexed words not learnable); `computeUncoveredKanji` (how many more words each kanji needs, a kanji written twice counting once)
- `src/utils/kanjiCoverageOrder.utils.test.ts` - the kanji_coverage score: pure frequency with nothing credited, a fixed rank ratio per credited kanji capped at 2, no bonus outside a kanji's top 3 remaining words; the picker (自分 before 自分自身, re-scoring after each pick, a coverage target above 1, frequency order once everything is covered)
- `packages/dataset-schema/src/grammarSentence.test.ts` - `grammarExampleToSentence` tests: `jp`/`en` carried over unchanged, cumulative-offset match derivation (not a hand-tracked index), particle/unresolved words excluded from `matches`/`vocabIds`, repeated occurrences of the same vocabId accumulating into one match list, and a stable per-example `id`; `patternHighlightRanges` tests (issue #89): correct offsets from cumulative surface lengths, adjacent pattern words merged into one contiguous range, non-adjacent pattern words kept as separate ranges, an empty `patternWordIndices` giving `[]`, and the sliced ranges reproducing the pattern text exactly
- `packages/dataset-schema/src/interactiveSentence.test.ts` - `segmentInteractiveSentence` tests (issue #89): the target vocab's match segment flagged `isTarget` even with no click handler involved (the reported bug), no `isTarget` anywhere when `targetVocabId` is omitted, a `highlightRanges` entry over unmatched text split into its own flagged sub-segment, an entry covering only part of a vocab match flagging the WHOLE match rather than splitting it (and the same for an entry covering it exactly), a match a range doesn't touch staying unflagged, a mid-segment range splitting the plain text on both sides, the earliest-start-wins rule preserved for overlapping vocab matches, and an empty/out-of-range highlight range dropped without side effects - every case additionally asserts the rejoined segment content reproduces `sentence.original` exactly
- Data-pipeline tests (tokenizer/Kuromoji integration, data-integrity checks) now live in the `gokan-dataset` submodule's own test suite, not here - `vite.config.ts` explicitly excludes `dataset/**` so they aren't double-run as part of this repo's `bun run test`.

**CI/CD Integration:**
- GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) includes test stage
- Tests run automatically on push to main branch
- Deployment only proceeds if all tests pass
- Uses Bun as the test runner for consistency with development environment

## Functional Workflows

### Learning Queue Logic

The SRS study session follows a **stateless** priority system with natural buffering:

1. **Old Reviews + Retry Items (Priority 1)**:
   - Items with `totalReviews > 0` and `nextReviewAt <= now`
   - Items with `needsRetry === true` (wrong answer in current session)
   - These are mixed randomly together
   - Order: Random selection from the pool (to prevent interference effects)
   - **Reading and meaning quizzes are batched, and the active batch is sticky**: `getNextVocabToStudy(queue, settings, now, preferredType)` takes an optional `preferredType` hint (the `quizType` of the card currently on screen, threaded in by `selectNextView` from `state.currentQuizItem`). While that type still has actionable work, it keeps being served - even if an item of the *other* type becomes actionable mid-batch (a retry flag flipping, or a review simply coming due while the user studies). Only once the active type's pool runs dry does selection fall back to the reading > meaning > production priority. Production is served last because it is the hardest direction and reads better once the word has already been seen in the easier ones this session. Without this, a reading item becoming due partway through a run of meaning quizzes would hijack the very next card - see the `[2026-07-24]` changelog entry for the bug this fixes and the kind indicator (`SingleAnswerCard.tsx`) for the accompanying on-screen "Reading"/"Meaning" phase label.

2. **New Intros (Priority 2)**:
   - Items with `introductionAt === null` and `stage !== 'graduated'`
   - When queue runs out of reviewable items, **3 new vocab are introduced at once**
   - All get `nextReviewAt = now` when user chooses "Learn"

3. **First Reviews (Priority 3)**:
   - Items with `totalReviews === 0`, `introductionAt !== null`, and `nextReviewAt <= now`
   - These are introduced vocab that haven't been tested yet
   - Lower priority than new intros ensures buffering

4. **User Actions on Introduction**:
   - **Learn**: Item activates with base memory strength. `nextReviewAt` set to `now` (becomes immediately reviewable)
   - **Skip**: Item is marked as **Fully Mastered** (`maxMemoryStrength`). Stage set to `graduated`. It will not appear in reviews
   - **Mastery**: If `memoryStrength >= maxMemoryStrength` after a review, item graduates. `nextReviewAt` is cleared

5. **Retry Mechanism (Wrong Answers)**:
   - `needsRetry` is **per quiz type** (`{ reading?: boolean; meaning?: boolean; production?: boolean }`): a wrong reading answer sets `needsRetry.reading` and never blocks a due meaning or production review (and vice versa).
   - When user gives wrong answer: `needsRetry.<type> = true` is set. Item review schedule is updated based on the failure.
   - Item appears in current session (mixed with old reviews)
   - On retry attempt:
     - If Correct: `needsRetry.<type> = false`. **SRS state is NOT updated** (training only). Original failure scheduling stands.
     - If Wrong: `needsRetry.<type> = true` (loop until correct). SRS state is NOT updated (prevent double penalty).
   - This ensures retries help user learn correct answer without artificially inflating memory strength after a failure.

6. **Queue Refill**:
   - Triggered automatically in `useQuizOrchestration` when `selectNextView`'s `queueItem` is null and `sessionState` is 'learn'
   - Adds 3 new vocab at once (batch size = 3)
   - Respects daily limits and kanji knowledge constraints
   - Paces words learned in kana (see below): at most `CONSTANTS.srs.newUsuallyKanaPerSession` (2) per session
   - **The kanji coverage order** (`kanji_coverage`, the default) offers words that use known kanji the learner has not met in enough words yet, without giving up usefulness. A known kanji is uncovered while it appears in fewer than `kanjiCoverageTarget` of the learner's words (`computeUncoveredKanji`, `utils/wordOrder.utils.ts`, a kanji written twice in a word counting once). Each word the order could offer (all kanji known unless `ignoreKnownKanjiRequirement`, not in the queue, never a word learned in kana) is scored `min(2, credited) × ln 4 − ln(rank + 200)` (`kanjiCoverageScores`, `utils/kanjiCoverageOrder.utils.ts`; the numbers are `CONSTANTS.srs.kanjiCoverage`): log-scale frequency, plus a 4× rank discount for each uncovered kanji that credits the word, at most 2. **A kanji credits only its 3 most frequent remaining words**, so 生年月日 gets no bonus for 生 while 学生 and 先生 do, and a compound (自分自身) cannot take the bonus its everyday word (自分) needs. `pickKanjiCoverage` picks the best score, then re-scores, so one batch does not cover a kanji twice; with nothing left uncovered the score is pure frequency. The same scorer ranks the listening library's "Your learning order", so the two cannot drift. Why the score replaced a linear `× 2500 − rank` one, with measurements: docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md, 2026-10-20.

7. **Completion**:
   - Session ends when: No Due Reviews AND (Daily Limit Reached OR No More Learnable Content)

8. **Words learned in kana** (`Vocabulary.usuallyKana`: ここ, この, ある, 沢山, whose JMdict headword is a rare kanji spelling nobody learns). The dataset decides which words these are at build time (`decideUsuallyKana` in the submodule's `scripts/build-common.ts`, documented in its `docs/SCHEMA.md`); the app only reads the flag:
   - **Display**: every place that names a word goes through `headwordOf` (`@gokan/dataset-schema`), which gives the reading for these words; `secondaryForm` puts the kanji spelling where the reading would go, and `KanjiSpellingNote` says "Usually written in kana. Kanji spelling: 此処". The meaning card shows that note before answering, because two such words can share their kana (いる is 居る and 要る) and the kanji spelling tells them apart. Grading accept-lists keep every kanji spelling, so typing 此処 still counts.
   - **No reading quiz**: asking the reading of a word shown as ここ tests nothing. `VocabProgress.usuallyKana` (required, so every object handed to scheduling must say) makes `isReadingRelevant` false: the reading entry is ignored by `isVocabFullyMastered`, `vocabNextReviewAt`, `isReadingActionable` and the forecast, and the first review is meaning, or production with meaning off (`introQuizType`; `applyVocabIntroChoice` sets that direction due now, and the reducer commits it to the session instead of reading). With both off the word has nothing to learn and graduates on intro. The flag is a copy of dataset knowledge: set by `createVocabProgress` from the vocab, re-synced on load and on every queue change from the frequency index (`syncUsuallyKana`, dispatched as `SYNC_USUALLY_KANA` only when something differs), so an exclusion added later gives the word its reading quiz back (due now). A Drive merge keeps the local copy.
   - **Learning orders**: these words need no kanji, so the indexes list them with empty `containedKanji` and the kanji gate lets them through, at their kana spelling's frequency rank. The kanji-driven orders leave them out (`orderIncludesUsuallyKana`): KKLC by its index, which never lists them, and `kanji_coverage` in `SRSService`. Frequency and JLPT offer them, paced by `usuallyKanaPacer`: past the session's budget (`usuallyKanaBudget`: the cap, less those introduced this session, tracked on `SessionTracking.usuallyKanaIntroduced`, less those already waiting among the intro candidates) they are deferred, unless nothing else could be offered. Most are function words (この, ほど, もらう), hard to tell apart when several arrive together.

**Natural Flow Example**: 
- Introduce 3 vocab → All 3 get `nextReviewAt = now`
- Priority shows 3 more new intros (if available)
- After 3 more intros, no more new intros available
- System shows 6 pending first reviews
- Pattern: Intro × 3 → Intro × 3 → Quiz × 6 → Intro × 3 → ...
- If wrong answer: Item gets `needsRetry = true` → Appears again in current session

### Session State Computation

Implemented in `quizSelectors.ts` via `selectNextView()` - the single function that also decides the queue item to load and whether to show the intro card (see State Management above):

```typescript
if (hasDueReviews) return 'review'
if (canIntroduceNew && hasLearnableVocab) return 'learn'
if (hasUnlockedKanjiPending) return 'learn-kanji'
if (hasUpcomingReviews) return 'waiting'
return 'exhausted'
```

### Answer Evaluation Flow

The same for every exercise, vocab and grammar alike (see Services & Business Logic → Exercise engine):

1. The exercise is built when its card loads (`vocabExercise` / `grammarExercise`): its slots (what each answer accepts), its cue and what its card shows. `quizType` (`'reading'` | `'meaning'` | `'production'`) and `quizMode` (`'base'` | `'context'`) come from `currentQuizItem` and live on the exercise's `host`.
2. The learner types into the card's slots, and may reveal a hint (`EXERCISE_SET_ANSWER`, `EXERCISE_REVEAL_HINT`).
3. Submit (`useExerciseTurn`): the latency is frozen, then `gradeExercise` grades every slot the same way (a revealed hint, an empty answer, the accepted forms, the near forms, another form of the word, then on a miss the near-synonyms) and decides the result, the reward scale, the message and whether the card moves on by itself.
4. **Meaning in context only**: if `enableGeminiContext` is enabled (the Settings master toggle) AND `geminiApiKey` is configured AND a sentence is available, `refineMeaningWithAi` (`aiRefine.ts`) asks Gemini for a second opinion:
   - If `alwaysUseAiForMeaningContext` is `true` (default), AI validates ALL answers (including strict-correct ones)
   - If `alwaysUseAiForMeaningContext` is `false`, AI only validates answers that were strict-wrong or strict-minor_error
   - On AI network error (400, 500, etc.): silently falls back to the strict evaluation result
   - Note: `enableGeminiContext` gating was previously missing from this check - a `geminiApiKey` left over from before the user disabled the feature would silently keep triggering AI calls. Fixed so the toggle is actually authoritative.
5. `effectsOf` decides what the answer does to SRS state, and the feedback is shown (`EXERCISE_SUBMITTED`).
6. Continue, or the auto-advance, dispatches `EXERCISE_CONTINUE`, and the reducer writes the effects through `applyEffects` to the current progress. A vocab `review` calls `SRSService.applyAnswer()` with both `quizType` and `quizMode`: `meaning_base` and `meaning_context` update the same `vocab.meaning` SRSEntry, but use different `expectedLatency` values (10s vs 15s) for the latency multiplier calculation.

### Grammar Quiz Flow (issue #17, blank-selection/grading revised for issue #32, made pattern-primary for issue #33)

1. `useGrammarOrchestration`'s load effect resolves a `GrammarPoint`, `await`s `computeBlankPlan` for this turn's plan (`{ exampleIndex, example, blankWordIndices, blankWordSpans, slots, readOnly, conjugation?, realization? }`) and builds its exercise with `grammarExercise` - see State Management → Grammar Activity State for the four-pass curated-example selection rule (prefer an example with a located grammar pattern, blanking it plus any known vocab as reinforcement; else fall back to known-vocab-only, then a single most-frequent candidate, then read-only) and the mined-sentence productivity pass (issue #73) that runs ahead of it on a review
2. A read-only plan is a `'study'` exercise: Continue only, no grading - see Application Pages → Grammar Screen. An `inflection` point with drill items is a `'conjugation'` exercise, one slot asking for the form
3. Otherwise the user types into each blank (one per span, live-sized to what's typed); the words not blanked are shown through `InteractiveSentence`. Each blank also has a "?" hint control (gloss, then reveal - see State Management)
4. Submit grades through `gradeExercise`, as on every card: the pattern-marker slots (`core`) decide the point's `overall` result, so a missed vocab blank never makes a demonstrated grammar core `wrong`; the vocab slots (`support`) scale the reward through `strengthModifier`; a near-synonym typed in a vocab blank is graded against `example.en`. Auto-advance fires only when *every* blank was strictly correct with the word itself, so a pattern-correct card with a missed vocab blank pauses for review instead of skipping past it. (Fallback examples with no located pattern grade worst-of-all at full strength.)
5. Feedback shown per blank (each border reflects its own result, and the right form is revealed beside any blank short of strictly right) plus the combined message
6. Continue writes the effects: the point's `review` through `GrammarSRSService.applyAnswer()` (scaled by `strengthModifier`, with `latencyMs` normalized by the blank count - issue #73, see State Management), positive-only indirect credit on the production entry of each word answered right (or of the synonym typed), or, for the study card, a `defer` (`GrammarSRSService.deferWithoutCredit()`: no grading happened, so no SRS credit). Then the next queued grammar point loads
### Daily Reset Logic

- `stats.newLearnedToday` resets at midnight
- Implemented via `RESET_DAILY_STATS` action
- Triggered by date change detection

## Constants & Configuration

### Key Constants (`commons/constants.ts`)

**Knowledge points (`utils/knowledge.utils.ts`):**
- `KNOWLEDGE_POINTS_PER_ENTRY`: 200. **One knowledge point is one mastery point**: `entryKnowledgePoints` returns `calculateMasteryPercentage` unchanged, which already spans 0..200 across the MasteryRing's two visual loops. A fully mastered entry is 200 and a word mastered in reading + meaning is 400.
- The scale is arbitrary, so it is set to the one value that removes every conversion. It was 100 per entry, normalising the 0..200 mastery curve by half, which meant the ring advanced 6, the session ticker printed "+6%" and the session total moved by 3: one quantity, three places, two units, and arithmetic the user could not check by eye. Changing the ring to 0..100 instead would unify equally well but would destroy the deliberate two-loop design, so the scale moved rather than the ring.
- Consequence worth knowing: the Stats knowledge curve's absolute numbers are **twice** what they were. The curve's shape, and every comparison drawn from it, is unchanged.
- Production **counts** in the curve alongside reading and meaning, so a word studied in all three directions is worth 600 and a production answer moves the session total and the curve alike. Its no-history entries are the one asymmetry: see Production quiz rollout.
**SRS Limits:**
- `dailyNewLimit`: 20 new vocab per day
- `newVocabBatchSize`: 3 (introduce three at a time for buffered learning) - vocab only, grammar deliberately does not share it
- `grammar.newBatchSize`: 3 - **fallback ceiling for grammar's no-teaching-order path only**. `advanceGrammarQueue` normally introduces the whole current chapter at once (sizing its request to `getCurrentChapter().points.length`); this ceiling applies only when the teaching order can't be loaded and `getNextCandidates` walks the flat JLPT fallback (see Services & Business Logic → Grammar Activity: Chapter-driven introduction)
- `sentenceSelection.targetRingCeiling`: 100 (the production ring at which a word stops counting as a target), `sentenceSelection.unresolvedKatakanaWeight`: 0.5 (unknown-weight of an unresolved katakana-only word, 2+ characters - a resolved-unknown or kanji-bearing unresolved word weighs 1), `sentenceSelection.unintroducedKanaWeight`: 0.5 (unknown-weight of a resolved word not introduced yet, written in kana at every occurrence in the sentence - written with a kanji it weighs 1), `sentenceSelection.lengthBand`: 10 (characters per band for the ranker's length key). See Services & Business Logic -> Shared sentence ranking.
- `maxReviewsPerDay`: 150
- `sessionQuizCap`: 200 (maximum quiz tasks one study session commits to; unlike the two per-day limits above, which are effectively disabled, this one is real)
- `sessionSuspendTtlMinutes`: 30 (how long a session paused by a consult page stays resumable; past it, returning starts a fresh session)
- `production.seedStrengthRatio`: 0.4 (fraction of a word's meaning strength its production entry starts from when activated)
- `production.seedDelayHours`: 24 (delay before the first production review, so it lands outside the session that activated it)
- `production.reinforcementStrengthRatio`: 0.5 (fraction of a full production gain a grammar answer's vocab reinforcement earns; right direction, easier conditions)

**Quiz Timing:**
- `correctAnswerAutoAdvanceDelay`: 1800ms
- `incorrectAnswerRevealDelay`: 400ms

**Storage Keys:**
- `progressStorageKey`: "GOKAN_SRS_PROGRESS"
- `settingsStorageKey`: "GOKAN_SRS_SETTINGS"
- `googleDriveFileName`: "kanji-progress.json"

**Setup Defaults:**
- `defaultKanjiCount`: "10"
- `defaultKanjiLearningMethod`: 'kklc'
- `minimumKanjiCount`: 0
- `maximumKanjiCount`: 2300

## Error Handling

### Fatal Errors

**Data Integrity**: If a vocabulary file fails to load for a reason that is not a clean "not found", the application must **suspend operation** (Critical Error). A transient or corrupt load must not be silently skipped, as that masks fundamental data corruption.

**Retired vocab is the one deliberate exception** (`VocabNotFoundError` / `RETIRE_VOCAB` / `UserProgress.retiredVocabIds`): a word whose compiled file is *genuinely absent* (the dataset build dropped it) is not corruption, it is an orphaned reference in the user's own `learningQueue`. `VocabularyService.loadVocab` throws `VocabNotFoundError` only for a **definitive** not-found: a real 404 (dev server / true origin 404), or the prod CloudFront SPA shell served at 200/`text/html` (its distribution-wide `custom_error_response` rewrites a missing key to `index.html`). A transient **5xx / 403 / 429**, an offline fetch rejection, or a 200 with an unparseable body all stay ordinary (recoverable, fatal) errors and are **never** retired - retirement is a permanent tombstone, so a deploy or a CloudFront/S3 hiccup must not be able to delete a legitimate word. `aws s3 sync` never removes a file still present in the new dataset, so a still-existing vocab cannot 404 mid-deploy either; only the genuinely-dropped files do. The quiz orchestration then dispatches `RETIRE_VOCAB` instead of fatal-erroring: the id leaves `learningQueue` and is tombstoned in `retiredVocabIds`, so it is never served again. The tombstone is durable against respawn: it is unioned across a Drive merge (and the merged `learningQueue` re-filtered by it) and re-applied to the queue on every load (`migrateUserProgress`), so a union merge that re-adds another device's still-present entry is immediately re-cleaned.

**Error Display**: `App.tsx` checks `state.fatalError` and shows full-screen error with reload button.

**Fatal error sources** (a retired/absent vocab is deliberately NOT one of these - see above):
- A vocabulary file that fails to load for any reason other than a clean not-found (a network/transient failure, or a parse error on a file that genuinely IS present)
- Index corruption
- Invalid data format

## Modification Log

The detailed, dated modification log lives in [docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md](MODIFICATION_LOG.md), so this file stays focused on current-state documentation. That file is shared by both `CLAUDE.md` and `GEMINI.md` and is not auto-loaded into agent context: read it on demand when you need the history or reasoning behind a decision.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> **`git log` is the primary record of what changed; the log file is not.** Add a `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md` entry only when a change carries reasoning the diff alone does not convey: the result of an investigation, why a non-obvious approach was chosen over the obvious one, or a subtle behavioral interaction a future agent could otherwise reintroduce. Do not log routine, mechanical, or self-evident changes: git history already covers those. When you do add an entry, add it to `docs/MODIFICATION_LOG.md` (once), not here.
